Record: this register; bart's manual page. Marked: mine, 2026-09-29: "For the multinomial fit's offset and its test rows, we should also use your recommendation." [dec-A111]

**A new missing value in a fit-time test set is refused**
The refusal of a missing value in a column that had none in training covers test data given when fitting - bart's test argument, dbartsData's test data and the sampler's test setters - not predict alone, and no na.action reaches it: na.pass on the fit governs the training rows only. The alternatives were checking at predict only, which leaves the fit's own test predictions sending those values down an unlearned route, and extending predict's na.action to fit-time test data. A user with such a test set must drop or impute those rows, or fit with keepTrees = TRUE and call predict with na.action = na.pass, which predicts NA for those rows and computes the rest. 0.9-34 silently returned predictions for such rows from whichever branch the missing value fell down. The further alternative was predicting NA for those rows with a warning instead of refusing. See also: [dec-B34].
Record: docs/plans/composition-refusals.md. Marked: mine, 2026-09-29: "I think we can deviate from base R semantics because we can actually do different things with NAs when they're predictors than can be done in an lm. That argues for your recommendation, so let's stick with that." [dec-A112]

**A hurdle fit refuses a predictor missing only on zero rows**
bart(family = "hurdle.lognormal") refuses at fit time, naming the columns, a predictor whose missing values all sit on rows where y is zero: the positive part trains on the positive rows alone and so learns no route for a missing value, yet the composed fit evaluates it on every row. A column with missing values on both kinds of row fits. The alternative was exempting those rows, which would feed unlearned routes into the fit's own combined outputs. A user whose missingness falls only among the zeros must impute, drop the column, drop those rows with na.action = na.omit, or fit the two parts separately, and the message names the remedies. The pattern arises two ways the package cannot tell apart: the predictor is missing because y is zero, in which case it follows from the outcome and no fit using it would be right, and the message says so; or a few missing values land on zero rows by chance, which is common in zero-heavy data (three missing values at 70 percent zeros all fall on zeros about a third of the time), where nothing is wrong and the user loses those rows or imputes. Fitting and returning NA at those rows would leave holes in the fitted values, residuals and log-likelihood on training rows, and fitting on would return predictions down a route nothing learned, where predict and a fit-time test set refuse a new missing value (dec-A112). A factor level that appears only on zero rows is not refused: the positive part meets a level it has no rows for and its trees place it as they place any level absent from a node, and refusing it would stop fits on every rare level. The maintainer, shown the two causes and the factor case on 2026-10-05, left the choice to the orchestrator: "I'd like to defer to whatever is going to be most useful to users." See also: [dec-B34], [dec-A112].
Record: docs/plans/composition-refusals.md. Marked: not mine, 2026-10-05. [dec-A113]

**The fit methods' surface: the details under the rulings**
Under the single predict argument order (dec-B07) the agents gave hurdle predict an offset argument in the same position as every other class, which only refuses a value since a hurdle fit takes no offset, rather than no such argument (ordinal predict's offset argument, which began the same way, takes an offset under dec-A137); and, matching fitted's type vocabulary to predict's and extract's, left type = "forest" out of fitted, since fitted's average over draws does not apply to per-forest values, and type = "class" out of extract, since a class is a reduction over draws. The alternatives were no offset argument on that class, and adding both types. A user sees an offset argument that can only error on hurdle predict, and gets a type error from fitted(fit, type = "forest"). The offset argument stays: a hurdle fit takes no offset only because that part of the family is unfinished, and removing the argument now and adding it back later would shift every argument after it once 1.0-0 is out, so it is a reserved slot that starts working when hurdle offsets land; the maintainer on 2026-10-05: "Hurdle offsets are coming." Leaving type = "forest" out of fitted was reversed by dec-B236, under which fitted takes what extract takes. Leaving type = "class" out of extract stands, a class being chosen from the averaged probabilities and not a draw. See also: [dec-B07], [dec-A137], [dec-A15], [dec-B236].
Record: docs/plans/predict-surface.md. Marked: revisit, 2026-10-05: the offset argument stays and fitted takes type = "forest". [dec-A114]

**Ordered factors: the details under the ruling**
Under the ruling that ordered factors split between their level codes (dec-A09) the agents kept ordered factors admissible as linear or Gaussian-process leaf covariates, entering as equally spaced standardized level codes, and as monotone directions; took the split grid from the factor's declared levels rather than the observed ones, so an unobserved interior level stays splittable and predictable; and refused a test value that is not a level the fit knows, as for unordered factors, rather than reading it as a point between levels. The alternatives were refusing ordered factors in those roles, a grid from the observed levels, and interpolation. A user giving an ordered factor as a leaf covariate assumes equal spacing between its levels, the convention of the linear contrast lm gives an ordered factor, and the manual now says so and that other spacings need a numeric column; the maintainer on 2026-10-05, between that and refusing an ordered factor as a leaf covariate: "Accept it, and say so in the manual." The grid from declared levels also stands: with no training row at an interior level, that level is predicted between its neighbours (2.95 against neighbours near 2 and 4, over eight seeds), where a grid from observed levels would refuse it, as lm refuses a level it dropped as unused, and would move whenever a subset missed a level; the cost is that the boundary around an empty level holds two split points the data cannot tell apart, so it has twice the prior weight. The maintainer on 2026-10-05: "Declared levels." A test value that is not a declared level is refused, as for unordered factors and as predict.lm refuses a new level. See also: [dec-A09].
Record: docs/plans/column-kind-consolidation.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-05, for the leaf covariate and the declared levels. [dec-A115]

**The sampler's row mask: the details**
The sampler's setActiveRows is named for active rows rather than subset, which dbartsData already uses for physically dropping rows, or mask; it accepts only exact 0 and 1, refusing the whole call on any other value; treats an all-ones vector as clearing the mask and accepts an all-zeros one, which leaves the trees moving under the prior alone; keeps an inactive row in its leaf for count-based rules and still gives it a fitted value; keeps the cut grid and the response scaling at their full-data values under a mask; has setData clear the mask; reports NaN as an inactive row's pointwise log-likelihood; and keeps the mask out of the saved sampler state, the R sampler object reinstalling it whenever it rebuilds. The alternatives were fractional values, a finite or minus-infinity log-likelihood, and storing the mask in the state. A caller who moves a sampler's state elsewhere, or replaces its data with setData, must reinstall the mask. The pointwise log-likelihood at such a row is ruled: where a weight is a precision, a row of weight zero, like a masked row, is not in the likelihood and reports NaN, which makes a user drop those columns before a tool such as loo counts them as observations; where a weight is a count, the row's value is multiplied by it, as brms and rstanarm multiply theirs and as a logistic fit's trial counts already are. The maintainer on 2026-10-05: "Option 1 for precision weights. For any models with count weights, we can multiply." Masked rows count toward the rule against empty leaves, as toward every other count, under dec-B238, so a masked row differs from a deleted one by design. The name and the exact values stand: the maintainer on 2026-10-05, between that, setMask and setSubset: "setActiveRows with exact 0/1."
Record: docs/plans/latent-subset-mask.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-05, for the name, the values and the log-likelihood; the counting is dec-B238. [dec-A116]

**Binding data frames with a sparse factor column needs R 4.6**
rbind of data frames holding a sparseFactor column works only on R 4.6.0 or later: base R binds a factor column by matching the incoming values against the levels, and before 4.6.0 that matching refuses the package's sparse object with "'match' requires vector arguments". The manual and NEWS state the version, and the manual gives a way that works on any version and keeps the column sparse: lengthen the first frame by row indexing and assign the other frames' rows into it, which the tests cover alongside the refusal on older R. The alternative was making the sparse factor class inherit from factor, which would let rbind work everywhere but would send it through every base method written for a factor's integer codes, which it does not store. Users on R 4.2 to 4.5 cannot rbind such frames directly. The maintainer kept the R 4.2 floor rather than raising it to 4.6 for this. See also: [dec-A100].
Record: docs/plans/sparse-factor-frames.md. Marked: mine, 2026-09-29 ("Yes, just document it"), with the tests binding by assignment on older R at the maintainer's suggestion. [dec-A117]

**Retiring NA: which spellings warn and which are refused**
The seed and residual-scale rulings say an NA warns for one release on the entry points that defaulted to it in 0.9-34, and the six further applications give the test-data setters' updateState a one-release warning as a 0.9-34 argument. Checked against 0.9-34, several of those premises are false: dbarts had sigma but no sigest or seed, xbart had sigma but not sigest, dbartsControl's seed was called rngSeed, dbartsSpec did not exist, and the three test-data setters listed updateState only in the manual, so passing it was already an error. Applying the rulings' own condition, the agents made an NA refused, naming NULL, for dbarts's sigest and seed, xbart's sigest, dbartsControl's seed and dbartsSpec's sigest, and left the test-data setters without updateState. The 0.9-34 spellings still reach NULL through their rename warnings (sigma = NA, rngSeed = NA). NA still warns for a release where 0.9-34 had the argument: bart's sigest and seed, xbart's and bartBT's seed, n.samples, the sampler methods' updateState and run's counts. The agents also made NaN a refused value, never a stand-in for "not given", on every argument the change touched. The alternative was a warning for every listed site, as the rulings' text reads. A user who passes NA to one of the refused spellings gets an error now instead of in 1.1-0. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B136], [dec-B137], [dec-B140].
Record: docs/plans/na-as-none.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B336, dec-B374. [dec-A118]

**Extracted trees keep a chain column at one chain**
extract(type = "trees") always carries a chain column, 1 on a one-chain fit, since a draw's trees belong to one chain and cannot be combined across chains, so tree extraction always separates chains as extract and predict do under combineChains = FALSE, which keeps the chain dimension at one chain. The sampler's own getTrees keeps the sampler's rule, inherited from 0.9-x, of a chain column only when there are several chains, as its run returns a one-chain fit's draws without a chain dimension. The alternatives were dropping the column at one chain everywhere, as 0.9-34 did, which left tree extraction the one extract output that separates chains yet drops their index, and keeping it on getTrees too, which set getTrees against the rest of the sampler. A one-chain extract(type = "trees"), a bartBT fit's included, gains the column against 0.9-34 without the caller opting in. See also: [dec-A79], [dec-A96].
Record: this register. Marked: mine, 2026-09-29, after asking "We said that other extract results kept a chain column because that was consistent with other Bayesian packages, right? So if I adopt your recommendation, extract(type = \"trees\") would become an outlier?": "Yes, use (c)." [dec-A119]

**A control copied from a fit: refused by bart, stripped by dbarts**
A control object taken from a fitted sampler carries that fit's model configuration on internal attributes, the variance forest, the survival status, the forest declaration among them. A control from a plain fit, such as a default gaussian or probit one, carries none of them and is accepted by every entry point. bart and xbart, which build their own control, refuse such an object by name; dbarts and dbartsSpec, and the sampler's setControl, strip those attributes and fit from the rest, since passing a sampler's control on to a new dbarts call was taken to be a 0.9-x pattern and the attributes did not exist then; run on CRAN's 0.9-34 on 2026-10-05, it is not one: dbarts given a fit's own control, with a formula or with the fit's data object, stops there with cannot replicate NULL to a non-zero length, the stored control having lost its cut count, and only the sampler's setControl and its direct construction from a fit's control, model and data accept one. Earlier on the pre-release branch, dbarts carried them silently, so reusing a variance-forest fit's control fitted a variance forest nobody asked for, and a plain fit could inherit another fit's forest column; 0.9-34 has no such attributes, so that was never released behaviour. The alternative was refusing on dbarts and dbartsSpec too, which would break 0.9-x scripts that reuse a control. The split goes away under dec-B239: the configuration moves to the model and data objects, a control then carries none of it, and every entry point takes any control. See also: [dec-A96], [dec-B239].
Record: this register. Marked: revisit, 2026-10-05: superseded by dec-B239. [dec-A120]

**The leaf-prior slice: the calls under the rulings**
Implementing the k-or-sd leaf prior, the agents routed a writer's change of spread through the engine's leaf-scale writer, made to compute the scale exactly as creation does so that an equal write moves no draw, and a change of law through setModel, re-pinning a fixed residual sd; served DART samplers; let setLeafPrior fill a linear or Gaussian-process leaf's columns from the sampler while the constructors still require them; let xbart's sd grid take invchi entries as its k grid takes chi ones, made a named sd with no grid a one-cell sd axis swept smallest first, and refused a k in leaf.prior beside an sd grid; re-applied a named sd after a re-anchor on single-forest samplers from the latest write, so that after setState the next re-anchor restores that write; and carried the engine's k when a writer switches between the k and sd forms under a drawn law, so the next sweep's spread scales by the ratio of the anchors, as documented. The slice's calls on what an sd-named fit carries were replaced by dec-B192 to dec-B194: such a fit stores k and first.k as every fit does, where it carried sd and first.sd in their place; extract answers k and leaf.prior.sd on every fit, with one number for a value held fixed, where the slice added extract(type = "sd") and refused type = "k" on such a fit; and summary's default leaf-scale row on such a fit is leaf.prior.sd, where it was sd. What prior.sd refers to is the prior.sd.of entry of getLeafPrior's list under dec-B141, where the slice put it in an attribute. The alternatives, per call, were the direct opposite or refusal. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A105], [dec-A107], [dec-B141], [dec-B192], [dec-B193], [dec-B194].
Record: docs/plans/leaf-prior-k-or-sd.md, whose landing note lists the calls. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: revised under dec-B356, dec-B376; covered by earlier rulings dec-B141, dec-B192, dec-B193, dec-A105, dec-A107; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A121]

**Arguments forwarded through a wrapper resolve where they were written**
An argument a user's wrapper forwards through its dots to rbart_vi, bart or xbart resolves as it would in a direct call: rbart_vi's group.by and group.by.test name a column of the data when a column has that name and otherwise the variable where the caller wrote it, as lm's weights and subset resolve in the data first even inside wrappers; the retired resid.prior and split.probs spellings resolve their prior constructors and their num.vars vocabulary in the environment the caller wrote them in; and bartBT names bart(family = "ordinal") for an ordered factor of three or more levels. Before, on the pre-release branch, rbart_vi through a wrapper failed to find group.by, and the retired resid.prior through a wrapper could not find chisq; 0.9-34, given data and a group.by naming a variable that is not one of its columns, grouped by the data's first column without a warning, called directly or through a wrapper. The alternative for group.by was preferring a wrapper's own variable over a data column of the same name, which departs from lm. Not yet ruled on.
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B337, dec-B375. [dec-A122]

**The count dispersion in force is readable per chain**
The sampler gained getDispersion, returning the negative-binomial dispersion currently in force, one value per chain, repeated when the dispersion is fixed, and NULL on every other family, as getSigmas returns the residual sd. A host driving the sampler one sweep at a time reads it without running a sweep or serializing the sampler's whole state. Nothing in the package or its three consumers calls it: bart's sweep-by-sweep negative-binomial driver read it until 2026-09-27 and has since read the dispersion run returns. The alternatives, not weighed when it was added on 2026-08-15, were reading the dispersion off run's result, which needs a sweep, and out of storeState's saved state. It is new in 1.0-0, a public method on the sampler object, and the precedent the leaf prior's current-value reader follows (dec-B141). Not yet ruled on.
Record: docs/plans/adoption-slate.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-02 ("We can keep it"); the parameter's name, and so the accessor's, is not settled by this mark. [dec-A123]

**The leaf-prior reader slice: the calls under the ruling**
Implementing dec-B141, the agents made the prior a list per forest whose leaf.prior element is the specification in the terms it was named in, written back through setLeafPrior bitwise inert; left the multi-forest calibration entries out of the list on forests the calibration does not set, so they read NULL; reported NA for the response scale and shift, the leaf spread and the calibration's leaf-scale entries when chains disagree, which only a setState of chains from different samplers produces, and made an NA spread unwritable everywhere a leaf prior is taken; defined anchor, since named k.scale (dec-B201), as the value the spread in force is anchor divided by getK, the data's anchor under a k-named prior and under the improper invchi(df, 0) and twice the named sd or invchi scale otherwise, so the data's anchor is no longer readable under an sd-named prior; and had getK report the engine's k, which under sd naming is relative to that anchor rather than the data's. The alternatives, per call, were NaN or zero-length entries, a per-chain value or an error on disagreement, keeping anchor as the data's anchor beside the engine's, and exposing both anchors. The NA on disagreement can no longer arise: a state carries no leaf spread or anchor (dec-B195), and a state stored under another response transform is converted as it is installed (dec-B200), so the chains share the sampler's one prior and transform, and dec-B191's refusal of such a state was withdrawn and never built; the list shape, the absent calibration entries and getK's report follow dec-B141; on getK the maintainer, on 2026-10-02: "`getK()` should return `k`, the parameter. `getK()` is an interface on the sampler that only exists for advance use. The samples of `k` (if there are any) should be stored as `k`. End-users should use `extract`", which is what the sampler does; the anchor, the value that k is relative to, was not named in it. The calibration entries' NA after a state install is closed by dec-B195, under which a state brings no calibration. The anchor's definition, the value the engine's k is relative to, and its name, k.scale, are ruled by dec-B201; under sd = invchi(df, 0), which that ruling does not name, k.scale is the data's scale, as under a k-named prior. See also: [dec-B141], [dec-A108], [dec-B191], [dec-A121], [dec-B195], [dec-B200], [dec-B201].
Record: docs/plans/leaf-prior-reader-shape.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: revised under dec-B376; covered by earlier rulings dec-B141, dec-B195, dec-B200, dec-B201. [dec-A124]

**Release notes compare releases, as pruned by the agents**
Under the maintainer's rule that NEWS carries only what interests users and compares one release with the last, the agents rewrote dbarts's 1.0-0 section (152 items to 54), wrote stan4bart's 0.0-14 section, and rewrote bartCause's as 1.1-0. They left out, as too fine-grained: in dbarts, partial matching of shortened argument names ending, bart's unused-argument wording, the tree-prior all.equal tolerance, n.cuts above 65533 refused (0.9-34 did not clamp it, as the agents first wrote, but kept every cut and could hang), keepSampler fits always carrying n.chains, a refused state install no longer leaving an empty engine, the reference-build configure flag, and DESCRIPTION credit wording; in stan4bart, the save_raw_parameters and ridge_move sampler options and the constant placeholder diagnostic rows; in bartCause, a pate extraction fix whose defect arose only under dbarts 1.0 and never reached a release. Checked against the released 1.0-10 source, bartCause's section then gained the semiparametric missing-response refusal (1.0-10 fitted group.by with missing responses) and five released defects the first pass had missed. The alternative was listing each. On 2026-10-04 the maintainer, shown that 0.9-34 accepted the three shortened names 1.0-0 makes ambiguous (bart2's t= and u=, xbart's n.th=) and could hang above 65533 cuts: "OK, sure, add them both back"; both are back in dbarts's section. See also: [dec-B143].

**The multi-forest writer's two calls under the ruling**
Designing dec-B142's writer, the orchestrator settled that the leaf-prior reader reports a calibration-map forest's prior as forest(sd = ), the spelling creation and the writer take there, rather than normal(sd = ) in response units, which nothing accepts on such a forest, so the reader's output writes back, as the single-forest reader's does; and that creation refuses forest(sd = Inf) as the writer does. The alternatives were a second normal(sd = ) spelling in the writer that creation never takes, and accepting an infinite sd in both, which creation had accepted with no test of what a run then does. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B141], [dec-B142], [dec-A124].

**The monotone plan's cancel state, setModel refusal and staging**
Planning the monotone priors, the orchestrator made three calls. When a slow order count is interrupted or runs out of memory, the chain restores the tree being moved and rebuilds its fits from the trees, one pass over every tree, so the sampler is left in a valid state, but the interrupted sweep may be partly applied, its earlier trees holding new draws; the orchestrator had first written that the state is left unchanged, which the ruling on slow counts does not say. setModel refuses a model whose monotone directions or prior differ from the sampler's, that lacks the monotone attribute on a monotone sampler, or that adds a constraint to an unconstrained sampler, as setControl already mirrors creation; when this was planned such a model was accepted, and a copy of the sampler then fitted without the constraint. The plan staged the work as seven reviewed commits (the redraw, empty-leaf and reachability fixes; the monotone() constructor and vocabulary; the counter, unwired; the move, prior switch and exact gate, followed by a stop to measure the corrected engine's counts; a cache of those counts; the slow-count warning, interrupt and memory handling, pushed together with the move; then equivalence scenarios, SBC arms and docs), with the help pages waiting for the default ruling; as landed, a stage for the leaf geometry of factors and missing values was added, and the cache of counts is deferred to after 1.0-0 (dec-A134). The alternatives were leaving the sweep unrebuilt after a cancel, which the sampler cannot trust, rolling the whole sweep back, which needs a snapshot of every tree each sweep, accepting any model on setModel, and one combined change. Code driving the sampler object can continue an interrupted run from where it stopped, while an interrupted bart call returns nothing to continue; a user who swaps in a model without the constraint gets an error instead of a silent unconstrained copy. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B145], [dec-B146], [dec-B149], [dec-A134].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md. Marked: covered, 2026-10-05: what an interrupt leaves behind is dec-B256, and the setModel refusal is dec-B254, a constraint being structure. [dec-A128]

**Monotone stage 1: the calls made while implementing it**
Implementing stage 1 of the monotone plan, the orchestrator settled that a tree breaking the constraint is reseeded to all-zero leaves, drawing nothing, not only on warm starts but also after a forced setPredictor, setCutPoints or setData, whose collapse or remap can relate merged leaves to new neighbours; that setState's refusal of such a state names the monotone cause, which took a new out-parameter on the engine facade's setState; that setControl no longer stores a control before the engine accepts it, a defect 0.9-34 has too; and that the truncated-normal primitive reports a stalled tail rejection as NaN, where it returned a draw outside the interval, and takes a uniform proposal on a narrow interval far in a tail, which changes draws there, with every caller's fallback checked, since no recorded draw moved. The alternatives were refusing those predictor and data changes on a monotone sampler, which breaks the embedded use the sampler exists for, the generic refusal message, and leaving the two pre-existing defects to separate slices. The reset is ruled on in dec-B278, which keeps it on a forced update; the rest is not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A128], [dec-B278].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B355, dec-B374, dec-B375; revised under dec-B278. [dec-A129]

**Monotone stage 2: the calls made while implementing it**
Implementing the monotone() constructor, the agents kept it unexported, reached through dbartsForests and resolved by bare name like interactions(), with no print method of its own; accepted the strings "1", "-1" and "0" as directions, since c("increasing", 0) turns numbers into strings; refused non-integer numbers such as 0.5, which used to round, and TRUE; let prior go through match.arg, so it accepts abbreviations ("l" for "leaf") while directions match exactly; and refused a partly named vector and a predictor named twice, which used to fail on an empty name or keep the last direction silently. A package calling bart through an argument list, as stan4bart's bart_args does, reaches the constructor as dbartsForests$monotone. The alternatives were exporting monotone(), exact matching for prior, and refusing string codes. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B146], [dec-B147], [dec-B150].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: direction words abbreviate as the prior's name does, under dec-B288; the string codes are kept ("Accept "1", "-1" and "0" as the numbers they spell."). [dec-A130]

**Monotone stage 3b: what the constraint means at factor levels and missing values**
Fixing the monotone geometry, which read a factor split as a range of codes and ignored where missing values go, the agents settled that the fitted function is monotone along a constrained predictor wherever that predictor is observed, at every level of every factor and at a missing value of any other predictor; that a missing value of the constrained predictor itself has no position along it, so nothing is claimed there; that an axis carries a missing position only when its training column had missing values, since predict refuses a missing value in a column that had none and giving every factor axis one made the constraint depend on how a level split was labelled; and that a tree an update's new missing values make infeasible is reseeded to all-zero leaves. The alternatives were a missing position on every axis, which keeps the order unchanged when an update first brings missing values, and refusing such updates on a monotone sampler. Fits that split on a factor or carry missing values in a free predictor draw differently; no monotone fit shipped in a release. The reset on an update is ruled on in dec-B278, which refuses an unforced update that would need it; the rest is not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A129], [dec-B278].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-07, shown the alternative of a missing position on every predictor: "Only a predictor that had missing values in the training data." [dec-A131]

**Monotone stages 4 and 5: the calls made while implementing them**
Implementing the exact move and its slow-count handling, the agents carried the monotone prior as a second model attribute beside the directions, so copy() and reload keep it and setState keeps the sampler's own; ran the monotone enumeration gate in CI in quick mode only, as its own job, since full mode passes the job's timeout; added a per-move record with the count's time and size to the move census build; after a cancelled count rebuilt the total fits alone, since the running residual is rewritten by the next sweep's first roll before anything reads it; turned engine exceptions into R errors on every run entry, two of which could crash R before; and let setModel read the sampler's own directions and prior from its shape to refuse a change. On the checkpoint, the orchestrator ran the timing fits without counting moves the free bound decides, so the trigger saw what users pay, and a shorter pass counting every move for the hybrid's quantities. The alternatives were the prior folded into the directions, full-mode CI on a longer timeout, and rebuilding the residual as well. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A128], [dec-B149].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-07, shown the alternative of adding the monotone check to the full run started by hand: "As built. If we have weekly tests or somesuch we can do exhaustive on an infrequent basis." Weekly workflows exist (the equivalence and rchk runs, Mondays), so the full mode joins a weekly schedule: TODO weekly-full-exact-gates. [dec-A132]

**Monotone stage 6: the one-tree SBC arm becomes a diagnostic, and a successive-conditional check joins the gates**
The plan required the one-tree monotone SBC arm to pass, and it flagged under both priors. Investigating, the orchestrator found no engine defect: the sampler leaves the right law in place when started from a prior draw, and draws the leaves and sigma exactly given the tree, but a one-tree chain that can only grow and prune does not find its way back from trees with extra splits on an informative design, over 20,000 sweeps and more. The unconstrained twin gets stuck the same way, and its leaf count fails calibration too; it passed only because extra splits do not change an unconstrained fit, while under the constraint they force the stranded leaves apart and steepen the fitted function. The orchestrator made three calls. The one-tree arm stays as a diagnostic, not a pass requirement, on a design with a fifth of the data (20 rows), where, beside the leaf count, one summary still flags under each prior, against eight of nine under "leaf" and four under "joint" at 100 rows, and it reports the leaf count so a reader sees the limit. A new check, run in CI beside the enumeration gate, starts the chain at a draw from the prior, simulates data from it and requires every summary to stay centred after a few sweeps; it needs no mixing, reaches trees too deep to enumerate, and fails the old move in about a minute. The 20-tree arms remain the release-level calibration check. The alternatives were keeping the one-tree arm as a pass requirement, which the grow-and-prune sampler cannot meet on that design, and adding a structural move beyond grow and prune under the constraint, which the plan leaves out. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A132].
Record: docs/plans/monotone-exact-birth-death.md; man/monotone.Rd, the sentence on very few trees (f668cc86); TODO monotone-structural-move. Marked: mine, 2026-10-07, shown three options, nothing in the help, a sentence in the monotone help now, and a new tree move before the release, with the second recommended and the third as research after the release: "Use your recommendation for the monotone question." [dec-A133]

**The monotone order-count cache waits with the hybrid**
The plan's stage 4b, a cache of the "leaf" prior's order counts that lowers only the cost of fits with very few trees, moves after 1.0-0 with the Barker hybrid, which would replace the recount it serves. The alternative was building the cache before release. Only users who choose "leaf" and fit one to five trees see any difference, and they keep the slow-count warning and interrupt. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B153].

**Offsets and stored formulas at new rows: the calls under the rulings**
Implementing dec-B154, dec-B184 and dec-B185, the orchestrator settled five points the rulings left open. The default offset.test is formed as predict forms its offset under dec-B184: the expression offset was given as is evaluated on test, so offset = log(exposure) reads the test set's exposure, and a plain training vector applies only to a test set of as many rows, otherwise refused with a request for offset.test; the sampler keeps offset.test linked to later setOffset calls only where that default comes out as the training offset itself. An explicit offset.test is added to the formula's offset() terms evaluated on test, as predict's offset is added to them, and offset written inside it means the offset argument's own share, not the training offset that already includes the terms. An offset() term is refused when test or newdata is a matrix, with a request for a data frame. The training terms and, under factors = "indicators", the training level table ride as attributes on the predictor matrix beside the ones it already carries, and the exported makeModelMatrixFromDataFrame leaves the level table off. The stored terms keep base R's environment, the nearest thing to none that still evaluates, so base functions resolve by name, and every package function is written pkg::name (pkg:::name when not exported). The alternatives were a default offset.test that keeps tracking the training offset, an explicit offset.test that replaces the terms' share as well, reading a matrix's named columns, new slots on the data class, and the empty environment with base functions qualified too. A fit with an offset expression and a test set gets a different yhat.test and loses the link; a fit saved before the attributes existed predicts by replaying its term labels, as before. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B154], [dec-B184], [dec-B185].
Record: merge 51bea192. Marked: in part by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: the test set's default offset is the fit's offset expression evaluated on the test rows, as predict does it, against 0.9-34's reuse of the training values ("As built."; NEWS has the line); and an offset() term met with new rows given as a matrix is refused, asking for a data frame, as predict.lm refuses a matrix, against evaluating the term in the matrix's named columns ("Refuse, asking for a data frame."). One wording gap was checked and brings no question: dec-B185 says the stored terms keep no environment, and the code gives them base R's, the nearest thing that evaluates; a column the formula needs and the new rows lack is refused by name on the predictor and the offset paths alike, a column named pi or T included, and a fit made inside a function carries no frame (run on 2026-10-07). The other calls are not marked. [dec-A135]

**The R fitting-surface slice: the other calls under the rulings**
Landing the third review's R fitting-surface findings, the orchestrator also settled that: under factors = "indicators" (dec-B155) a test factor that declares levels with no training rows but has no row at them is accepted, as lm drops unused levels before checking, and only a level some row carries is refused; the label rule for sampler column updates (dec-B156) covers a column coded from an ordered factor as well as an unordered one, and takes a sparseFactor beside a factor or character vector; predict on a fit kept with keepSampler = TRUE and keepTrees = FALSE is refused only with more than one chain, where 0.9-34 returned the first chain's values and leftover memory for the others and the development branch failed on an internal error, while a one-chain fit keeps predicting from its current trees as one draw; keepCall = FALSE leaves no call on the multinomial, ordinal, nbinom, hurdle and rbart_vi fits too (dec-B158), and a fit saved with the old NULL() placeholder reads as having none; a multithreaded rbart_vi given no seed draws each chain's seed from R's random stream, so set.seed reproduces a run at the same n.threads; and proposal.probs naming a move twice is refused, where the second value was dropped silently. The alternatives were refusing declared but unused levels, keeping numbers on ordered columns, refusing at one chain too, leaving those classes as they were, documenting that set.seed does not reproduce a multithreaded rbart_vi run, and keeping the first value. Multithreaded rbart_vi runs without a seed draw differently and take n.chains values from R's stream; a call that named a move twice errors. The keepSampler refusal is the maintainer's, on 2026-10-04, shown the 0.9-34 output: "Keep the refusal." So is the rbart_vi seeding, the same day, shown that 0.9-34 did not reproduce a multithreaded run under set.seed and left the caller's stream untouched: "keep 1.0-0's behavior." The other calls follow the rulings they extend. See also: [dec-B155], [dec-B156], [dec-B158], [dec-B159].
Record: merge 51bea192. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B375; covered by earlier rulings dec-A136; the keepSampler refusal and the rbart_vi seeding ruled 2026-10-04. [dec-A136]

**Ordinal predict gains an offset channel under the offset rulings**
An ordinal fit takes offset and offset.test and uses them, but its predict refused an offset and predicted the offset-free surface, so predict at the training rows disagreed with fitted(). The orchestrator read dec-B154 and dec-B184, which apply a fit's offset() terms and offset argument at predict, as covering ordinal without a ruling of its own: ordinal predict evaluates them on newdata and adds an offset given to it, as probit does. The alternative was refusing offset and offset.test on ordinal fits at fit time, which would keep true the help's statement that ordinal has no out-of-sample offset. The offset argument of ordinal predict goes from refusing every value to taking one, and the premise of dec-A114, that ordinal and hurdle fits have no out-of-sample offset, no longer holds for ordinal. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B154], [dec-B184], [dec-A114].
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B349. [dec-A137]

**The bridge fixes: three calls made landing them**
Landing the third review's bridge findings, the orchestrator settled that the sampler's run refuses run(0, 0), a run of no sweeps, with 0.9-34's message, where 1.0-0 returned NULL; that a state restored onto a live sampler with $setState must bring the sampler's own tree-store capacity, refused otherwise as before, while only re-creating a sampler after a save and load adopts the state's capacity, so a live $setState never leaves control@n.samples stale, though after a reload it can differ from a store that was resized through the C interface, which no control records; and that a warm start builds every donor tree on scratch trees before replacing any chain, so a refused install leaves the sampler untouched and its continuation bitwise, with the snapshot and restore kept as a backstop. The alternatives were treating run(0, 0) as a no-op, letting a live $setState adopt the capacity and rewrite n.samples, and the snapshot and restore alone, which left a refused install's fits different in the last bits. A host loop that called run(0, 0) errors; a host restoring a state of another capacity re-creates the sampler; every warm start builds its donor trees twice. Not yet ruled on.
Record: merge b28a75fa. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: a state whose kept store is another size installs what fits, under dec-B294, and a warm start salvages what it can, installTrees refusing whole unless forced, under dec-B295; run(0, 0) stays an error, as in 0.9-34 ("A. An error."). [dec-A138]

**The flat C API change keeps the version pair at 1/0**
Landing the flat C API's multinomial support (dec-B160), an ABI event, the orchestrator held the header's version pair at 1/0 and re-baked only the API hash and signature token, since no version has shipped and the header says the constants do not move before the first release; and made the flat run leave the k buffer unwritten on a sampler with no k hyperprior, as the header already said and the R run does, where it had written k. The alternatives were bumping the minor version, which the renamed struct field does not fit, and bumping the major, which every stub consumer's handshake refuses until rebuilt. A stale consumer binary passes the version handshake unless built with the exact-ABI flag; one that sized a single split-count set on a multinomial or BCF handle would overflow, and no consumer drives either through the flat API; a host that read k from the buffer under a fixed k finds it untouched, and neither consumer does. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B160], [dec-B163], [dec-B164].
Record: docs/plans/capi-multinomial.md. Marked: mine, 2026-10-07, shown the alternative of writing the fixed k on every draw: "Leave it untouched." [dec-A139]

**The nbinom log-mean slice: a gate rule, the prior centre under the row mask, the SBC arm's k and the baseline**
Planning and landing dec-B170, the orchestrator settled four points. The new mixing gate requires the two chains to agree on r by split-Rhat below 1.05; at r0 = 2 both chains hold r = 2 for every draw, where split-Rhat is undefined, so a check whose halves are all constant at one shared value passes and any other zero-variance case fails. The leaf prior's centre c, the log of the total count over the total exposure, is computed over all rows, masked ones included, as aft's response transform is, so a 0-weight row's count still moves it; the active-row pin test keeps the inactive rows' total count when it substitutes them. The SBC arm fixes k at 3 / 0.68 (an sd of f of 0.68) rather than drawing it under the shipped chi(1.5, 2), since the Polya-Gamma draw's cost grows with the counts and wide log-mean draws make it unbudgetable. The equivalence baseline equivalence-fdfc1fe4 records the nbinom scenario fresh and carries the other 54 from d23673b5, against which they compared bitwise. The alternatives, inferred when this entry was written and not weighed at the time, were failing every zero-variance case, which fails the correct sampler, c over the active rows recomputed at each mask change, the default k hyperprior in SBC, and a baseline recorded whole at one commit. A masked nbinom row is not the same as a dropped one; SBC does not check nbinom's k draw; the baseline mixes two recording commits, as its MANIFEST row states. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B170], [dec-B179], [dec-B183].
Record: docs/plans/nbinom-log-mean.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: what the response gives a prior is taken from the rows in the likelihood at creation and held, in every family, under dec-B296; the three testing calls are reshaped under dec-B301. [dec-A140]

**The ingestion and auxiliary slice: the calls under the rulings**
Landing the data-ingestion and auxiliary fixes, the orchestrator settled that xbart's per-fold sigma (dec-B176) runs the full estimate once on all rows, raising any refusal or fallback warning once and choosing the route: where it fell back to the marginal sd, each fold takes its own rows' marginal sd, otherwise each fold fits one linear model on a design built once per worker, and under a fixed residual scale no fold fits one; that pdbart and pd2bart take a factor predictor's levs as level names only, refusing numbers (dec-B175); that xbart takes a vector n.cuts under bart's rule (dec-B174), where it failed on one; and that the warning a dbartsData object gives for arguments passed beside it names each one, now subset, weights, factors and na.action as well, with bart and rbart_vi no longer adding their default factors and na.action to the call in that case. The alternatives, inferred when this entry was written and not weighed at the time, were running the estimator with its fallbacks in every fold, reading numbers as level positions, keeping xbart to one n.cuts, and leaving those four arguments ignored without a word. A fold whose own rows would have fallen back, or not, follows the all-rows route, and xbart pays one estimate more; a caller passing level codes to levs errors. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B174], [dec-B175], [dec-B176].
Record: merge 6b8a6932. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B350, dec-B382. [dec-A141]

**The oversize-allocation tests run only at home and outside AddressSanitizer**
The tinytest arms that ask for a tree store far past what a process can hold, to check that sampler creation and setControl raise an R error and keep the old store, run only at home and not when ASAN_OPTIONS is set: AddressSanitizer aborts on the deliberate request instead of letting it throw, and valgrind flags it as a silly argument, so CRAN's memory-check flavors would report the request itself. The alternative was running them everywhere. No automated check runs these arms: CRAN and CI run the suite as not at home, and only a developer running the test file directly checks that an allocation failure becomes an error; a sanitizer build run without ASAN_OPTIONS set would still abort on them. Not yet ruled on.
Record: commit f7ada5bb. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A142]

**The families slice: the calls on survival and multinomial outputs**
Implementing dec-B177, dec-B178 and dec-B180, the agents settled that a count-row multinomial fit's predictive draws come in the draws-first layout every other extract channel uses (draws x rows x K, a chain margin when chains are kept apart), not rows x K x draws, and are drawn as a sequence of binomials, the same distribution as rmultinom but a different random stream; that extract(sample = "test") on a count fit keeps one-trial category codes, as predict does; that survivalProbabilities takes its offset argument right after newdata and ahead of combineChains, as in predict, which moves combineChains one place, and refuses an offset given without newdata, where predict.lm ignores one; that a matrix-interface hazard fit records its offset per subject, so predict and survivalProbabilities re-evaluate it on new subjects; that a hazard subject with no time keeps one first-period row with a missing response, so na.action records it as it records a missing status; and that a hurdle.lognormal fit drops a missing response under the default na.action, as every other family does, where it was refused. The alternatives were the rows-first layout, per-row rmultinom, refusing test-row ppd on count fits, appending the argument last, ignoring an offset without newdata, replaying the person-period vector, refusing missing times outright, and keeping the hurdle refusal. A positional combineChains in survivalProbabilities now lands on offset, refused unless numeric; count ppd draws do not reproduce a per-row rmultinom stream. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B177], [dec-B178], [dec-B180].
Record: merge b5192199. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B342, dec-B379; revised under dec-B340; covered by earlier rulings dec-B177, dec-B180. [dec-A143]

**The families slice: refusals, weights and name lookup**
Fixing the review's families findings and a name-lookup defect found while landing, the orchestrator and agents settled that a binary family refuses a response with one class in every encoding (numeric, logical, factor, character, and under family = "auto" where it resolves to probit), with one message, and holds back the rescaling warning only where that refusal fires; that predictive draws on a probit fit treat weights as trial counts, as on a logistic fit, refusing a non-integer, checked on predict's ppd path only so a fit's stored 0/1 mask weights are untouched; that predict and survivalProbabilities refuse a non-numeric offset (logical, factor, character, difftime, list), which base R would coerce, an all-NA logical excepted, and a numeric data frame, multinomial's form for per-category offsets, which the check lets through on every family; that a family object is held to the settings its constructor takes, read from the constructors' arguments, so probit(sigma = ...), which earlier bartBT fits printed, is refused by name; and that offset, offset.test, test and weights expressions are evaluated as model.frame evaluates them, in the data and then the formula's environment, with offset.test falling back to the caller's frame and skipping a function value, so a wrapper's local t is not base::t. The alternatives were refusing only numeric single-class responses, a logistic-only weight check, coercing offsets as before, dropping stale settings silently, and the formula's environment alone. A single-level factor or character response that fit on the pre-release branch is refused, and 0.9-34 also failed on a one-level factor response, with an unrelated message; a non-numeric offset that was coerced is refused; a family object saved from a pre-release bartBT fit is refused. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B154], [dec-B184].
Record: merge b5192199. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B339, dec-B374, dec-B379; revised under dec-B333. [dec-A144]

**shape is spelled bare wherever the count family's r appears**
With r named shape and its reader overloaded (dec-B189, dec-B190), the orchestrator settled that the bare word is used everywhere else the parameter appears: extract(type = "shape"), the fit's shape and shape.raw components, summary's vars, the shape element of run's result, the saved state's block and the two fields of the shipped C header, and that the engine's own vocabulary moves to shape with them so one word names the parameter throughout. The alternative was a family-qualified spelling in some of those places, such as nbinom.shape, which would leave the reader the only bare one. The maintainer was told the bare spellings would follow the overloaded reader and asked to say if any should differ, and did not address it. A later family carrying a shape reports under the same names, so code reading a fit's shape must know the fit's family. The saved-state block is renamed without raising the format version, no serialized format having shipped. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B189], [dec-B190].
Record: docs/plans/nbinom-dispersion-name.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B348; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A145]

**The state rulings applied to the warm start and to four values the rulings did not name**
Planning dec-B195 and dec-B196, the orchestrator settled that a warm start follows the same rule as setState: the recipient keeps its own leaf prior and every value it holds fixed, and the donor's trees and the values the recipient draws seed it, so bart(leaf.prior = normal(k = 2), warm.start = fit) runs at 2 where a fit made at k = 4 had left it running at 4 with a model that says 2, and the manual's "Only its trees, sigma, and k carry over" gains "where this fit draws them". It also settled that a Gaussian-process lengthscale the caller supplied is model, kept by the sampler, and that a state is refused only when it has recorded draws and carries saved trees under lengthscales other than the ones the sampler was given, compared exactly, a saved draw being replayable only under the kernel it was drawn with, while any other state installs and runs under the sampler's lengthscale (dec-A148); that amplitudes on a forest created not to update them, and sigma on a family that pins it, are not installed, where a gaussian state had left a probit sampler at a sigma of 1.26; that a drawn k installs as the stored k and not as the stored spread, so under another anchor the chain starts one sweep at that anchor over k; and that a state has no block for a value held fixed, a recipient that draws a value and finds no block keeping the value it has, with no install refused for the missing block. The alternatives, per call, were leaving the warm start adopting the donor's model; a supplied lengthscale travelling with the state as it then did; installing the spread and deriving k from the recipient's anchor; and writing the fixed values into the state without installing them, or refusing a state that lacks a block the recipient draws. Told of the warm-start and lengthscale calls, the maintainer on 2026-10-02: "That makes sense I think." The other four were not put. See also: [dec-B195], [dec-B196], [dec-B197], [dec-A148].
Record: docs/plans/state-not-model.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B343, dec-B344; revised under dec-B384; covered by earlier rulings dec-B196, dec-B201. [dec-A146]

**The fit-stores-k calls under the rulings**
Planning dec-B192 to dec-B194, the orchestrator settled that a fit records its prior and its fixed values in two descriptors, leaf.prior, the sampler reader's list as the run ended, and fixed, the scalars the sampler held fixed, both read from the sampler and not the model, while the draw channels keep their layout, a fixed sigma or shape still repeated per draw inside the fit, since predict, the log-likelihood and bartCause read them per draw and bartCause tells a binary fit by a missing sigma; neither name starts with k, because fit$k matches a component by prefix and the harness takes a missing k to mean fixed. On a fit with several forests extract returns one named number per forest for k and for leaf.prior.sd, each forest's own total before its amplitude multiplies it, which on a forest without a basis is the response's sd whatever forest(sd = ) named, that argument setting the amplitude's scale there; a multinomial fit returns one number and a hurdle fit a list per part. summary's default leaf-scale row follows the naming on every class, under dec-A105's "report in a way that matches what a user specified", and its line of fixed values (dec-B199) also names a fixed Student-t df and the ordinal's first threshold, pinned at zero. A plot panel tracing a fixed sigma or shape is not drawn. rbart, whose chains end on different anchors and which is removed in 1.1-0, gains none of the types. The burn-in spread loses its name with first.sd; first.k stays. The alternatives, per call, were an anchor component named for k with every reader rewritten to exact matching; a fixed value stored as one number in the channel itself; refusing leaf.prior.sd on a fit with several forests, returning the forest(sd = ) values as named, or the contribution's own prior scale, which is infinite under a half-Cauchy amplitude; both leaf-scale rows, or one of them, on every fit; a flat trace or a titled value for a fixed panel; and the types on rbart. Landing it, three more: a sigma the family pins at 1 is not named on the line of fixed values; a quantity the model holds fixed but whose chains differ is returned as one value per chain, a case a warm start from a donor that drew it produced before dec-A146 landed and no longer does, since a warm start leaves alone a value this fit holds fixed; and the Student-t df joined summary's default parameters so that a fixed one is named unasked, which makes a sampled one a default row. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B192], [dec-B193], [dec-B194], [dec-B199], [dec-A105], [dec-B126], [dec-A146].
Record: docs/plans/fit-stores-k.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B347; revised under dec-B376; covered by earlier rulings dec-B192, dec-B253, dec-A105; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A147]

**The state-not-model calls the plan did not rule**
Implementing dec-B195 to dec-B197, dec-B200 and dec-A146, the agents settled seven things. The record of the sampler's response transform is the model attribute response.range, the engine's (min, max) pair, which one bridge entry hands the engine right after creation rather than a new creation argument; copy goes through it after its own first creation, and so does the private sampler samplePriorPredictive draws from, which the plan did not name, so prior draws after a swap without the scale update are made under the sampler's prior rather than one anchored to the data as it now stands. A drawn df or shape block that is present is still checked, a non-positive df or a shape that is not a positive whole number refused, while an absent block is kept, reading the plan's "requires a df or shape only of a sampler that draws one" beside its "no install is refused for a missing block". Whether a state's pair names response units is decided by the sampler's family, not by the pair: on a gaussian-style family a constant response's (c, c) is the transform spanning 1 upward from c, the window from c to c + 1, and converts like any other, where it had been read as scale-free. The shift a gp leaf or forests with amplitudes refuse is compared exactly, so a state whose shift differs from the sampler's by one ulp is refused there. A fixed sigma is not installed but is carried through the install's move of the transform from the chain's own value, the arithmetic the install used through the stored value, so a same-units install keeps its draws. A state is refused for a supplied lengthscale only when it has recorded draws and carries saved trees and lengthscales other than the sampler's. setSigma records a fixed sigma as fixed(sigma^2), fixed() naming a variance. The alternatives, per call, were the record as a creation argument; leaving samplePriorPredictive a first creation; taking any present value of a drawn quantity; reading a non-increasing pair as scale-free; a tolerance on the shift; leaving a fixed sigma's internal value at the old transform's; and refusing on saved trees whether or not any draw was recorded. See also: [dec-B195], [dec-B196], [dec-B197], [dec-B200], [dec-A146].

**pdbart's translation table and the notices it replaces**
Implementing dec-B205, the agents made the set of translated names every name bartBT takes and bart does not (power, base, sigdf and sigquant among them), less x.test and sampleronly, which pdbart sets itself and refuses; power, base and splitprobs become one tree.prior = cgm(...), refused beside a caller's tree.prior; proposalprobs goes into a copy of the caller's control; sigdf and sigquant reach bart under its consolidated names. pdbart holds back only the bart notices it replaces with its own, so a retired name it does not translate, such as resid.prior, still warns through bart. The alternatives were translating only bartBTOnlyFormals, and holding back every bart notice. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B205], [dec-B229].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377. [dec-A149]

**pdbart on a sampler passed in**
The agents had a dbartsSampler passed to pdbart or pd2bart take only type = "bart", since it carries no fit to transform through, refused a hazard sampler outright, since its rows are person-period rows and dec-B209 replaced that view (one is recognised by its control's hazard-periods attribute, its model reading probit), and served an aft sampler only with an explicit type = "bart", since "auto" on an aft fit means survival. The alternatives were serving samplers under "auto" as fits are, and refusing samplers entirely. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B209], [dec-B226], [dec-B227].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377. [dec-A150]

**pd2bart's two-predictor shortcut, narrowed**
pd2bart's shortcut, which predicts each grid point as a single row when a fit has exactly two predictors, applies only when every averaged row is identical once the two are set - no offset or a constant one - and type is not "ppd", whose rows each draw their own noise; then n.average.rows and average.weights have no effect and a warning names whichever was given, while newdata still supplies the grid, as dec-B216 requires. Otherwise the general route runs. The alternatives were the shortcut on every two-predictor fit, which ignores a varying offset and the averaging weights, and dropping it. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B211], [dec-B214].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377. [dec-A151]

**pdbart's subsample and averaging weights in detail**
n.average.rows is drawn once, without replacement, before the grid loop, from rows with a positive averaging weight, so every grid value averages the same rows and a sample cannot be all zero-weight; on survival fits it counts subjects. average.weights must be finite, non-negative and not all zero, has one entry per fit row before the zero-weight exclusion (one per newdata row with newdata), and an entry for a row the fit gives weight 0 is ignored. Rows a fit excludes with a 0 weight leave the default average, a change from 0.9-34, which averaged every row, and NEWS says so. The alternatives were sampling all rows and redrawing per grid value. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B214], [dec-B218], [dec-B228].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377; covered by earlier rulings dec-B214, dec-B228. [dec-A152]

**pdbart's refusals on survival options, and the hurdle result**
The agents refused plot.type = "curves" below three computed times and on a result with no times margin; refused times on a family without survival and on aft under a non-survival type; left a hazard fit's period column out of the default xind and refused xind = "period"; and on a hurdle fit averaged over every observation, carrying the two component samplers as fit, dropped under keepSampler = FALSE, with no copied draws. The alternatives were drawing curves from any number of times, ignoring times where it does not apply, and offering the period as a predictor as before. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B209], [dec-B224], [dec-B207].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377; covered by earlier rulings dec-B209. [dec-A153]

**pdbart slice 1: the implementer's calls**
A fit or sampler passed to pdbart takes only keepSampler (or keepsampler) in its ..., any other argument refused, since no fit is made; a one-chain fit's fd has no chain margin under combineChains = FALSE, as bart's own components have none; and pdbart and pd2bart share one once-per-session key for their defaults message, its registry row owned by pdbart. The alternatives were passing other arguments on to a refit, a length-one chain margin, and a key per function. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B206], [dec-B213].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A154]

**pdbart slice 2: the implementer's calls**
A sampler passed to pdbart refuses newdata, n.average.rows and average.weights, having no data to code; an n.average.rows at or above the rows available uses them all with no random draw; the sampled rows keep the fit's order, so a plain-vector offset stays aligned; rows whose averaging weight is 0 are dropped; the result gains a family component for its labels, worded where dec-B215 is silent as "logit scale", "log mean y", "log y where positive", "probability y is positive", "predicted y" and "residual sd of y"; pd2bart's panel titles read "Median, <scale>"; and the shortcut's warning is a plain warning, as error-style rule R15 (dec-B161) requires for one outside its classes. The alternatives were serving samplers through their stored data, always drawing, a random order, keeping zero-weight rows, and classed warnings. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B215], [dec-B218], [dec-B161].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377; covered by earlier rulings dec-B161, dec-B214. [dec-A155]

**pdbart slice 3: the implementer's calls**
With no events at all, the default time is the median of all observed times; pd2bart never takes its single-row shortcut on aft or hazard fits; times are sorted and de-duplicated; every result carries times, NULL off survival; the curves view draws medians only, the dependence view the plquants band; a matrix hazard fit's newdata is coded once, its offset evaluated on the coded rows; and the size check runs after a data call's fit, since the subjects, periods, draws and grid it counts are known only then, the refusal advising to fit once with bart(..., keepTrees = TRUE) and pass the fit. The alternatives were refusing a fit with no events, the shortcut on survival fits, keeping times as given, bands on the curves view, and an estimated count before fitting that would refuse calls the exact count admits. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B220], [dec-B221], [dec-B222], [dec-B224].
Record: docs/plans/pdbart-on-bart.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B377; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A156]

**The 2026-10-04 slices: calls under the rulings and the regressions fixed**
Landing input-guards, restore-empty-leaf and count-cap, the agents settled: an infinite case weight is refused by name on every entry, as NEWS already said, and posterior predictive weights on gaussian, Student-t and aft fits must be finite and non-negative; a per-column cut count below one is refused where the cap is checked; pdbart's burn-in sigma is kept. A state install merges leaves no row reaches on every path, warm start included (which had installed such leaves unchecked), restoring the response's own state first so a copy reproduces the original, as 0.9-34's restore merged them; no new signal tells the caller, a question left to the state-frame-prior design. The count cap of dec-B202 also bounds dbartsDrawLatents and dbartsWorkingResponse; a sampled negative-binomial shape cannot reach it, its grid ending at 50; an interrupted refresh that drew the shape is put back whole, and an interrupted multinomial sweep keeps the categories updated before the stop; draws made outside a run (setWeights, setResponse, setData, setState) are not interrupted, the ruling naming the in-sweep interrupt. treatSens' null treatment model calls pdbart with one chain, keeping its draw count and cost. The state-frame-prior design was split, its empty-leaf regression and quantile grid made their own items. The alternatives were leaving each as it was. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B202], [dec-B229].
Record: docs/plans/input-guards.md; docs/plans/restore-empty-leaf.md; docs/plans/count-cap.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B368, dec-B378; revised under dec-B372, dec-B387; covered by earlier rulings dec-B234; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A157]

**A control stored on a sampler states nothing about the next fit**
Under dec-B240 the control keeps mirrored copies of the tree count, the quantile rule and the binary flag until 1.1-0, written by the sampler so that code reading them still works. Planning the move, the orchestrator settled that such a copy never counts as a statement: a control taken from one fit and passed to a new fit brings its run settings and none of the three, so the new fit takes its tree count and quantile rule from its own arguments or the defaults. A value the caller states on a control, by naming it in dbartsControl or by assigning the slot, does count, for one release: it is used, with the once-per-session warning of dec-A02, or refused by name where the model, the data or the sampler states another, and never dropped without a message. Run on CRAN's 0.9-34 on 2026-10-05: dbarts given a fit's own control stops with an error whether called with a formula, with the fit's data object or with a bart2 fit's control, so no 0.9-x script carried a fit's tree count into a new fit this way; the caller's own control object used for a second fit worked and still does. On the branch before the move a fit's control did carry its count, 50 trees giving 50, which was never released. The alternatives were letting a copy speak always, or only when the fit's own count came from a control, each keeping for one release a behaviour 0.9-34 did not have. That holds at dbarts, bart and xbart. At the two places that take objects as given it is not the whole of 0.9-34: run there on 2026-10-05, a sampler built directly from one fit's control and another fit's model and data took the control's 200 trees and quantile cuts, and setControl given another fit's control resized to its count. At those two a copy that differs from what the model, the data or the sampler has is refused by name, saying where the setting now lives, so nothing is taken or dropped in silence and NEWS's statement under dec-B247 that setControl refuses another tree count stays true; a copy that agrees passes. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A02], [dec-A120], [dec-B239], [dec-B240].
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: revised under dec-B385; covered by earlier rulings dec-B247. [dec-A158]

**The multi-forest calibration anchor is derived data**
A multi-forest sampler scales each forest's prior through a calibration whose anchor is computed from the response when the sampler is first created and reused by every re-creation, so a copy or a reload builds on it and not on the response then in force. It rode the control's hidden forests attribute. dec-B239 sends the forests and their settings to the model object, but the anchor is not a setting: it is a number derived from one response, the same kind of value as the response range that dec-B232 moved from the model to the data object beside the split points of dec-B231. Planning the move, the orchestrator settled that the anchor goes to the data object with the range, so a model handed to another data set gets a fresh anchor, where on the model it would carry a number derived from one response to any data it is paired with. A sampler whose forests carry amplitudes refuses setData and every re-anchoring call, so the value does not change in a sampler's life either way. The alternative was the model object, inside the forest configuration. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B231], [dec-B232], [dec-B239].
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B378. [dec-A159]

**A Student-t fit redraws its latent scales when a row's weight leaves zero and when a state is installed under other weights**
Landing the rule that a leaf is empty only when no row reaches it (dec-B238), the orchestrator and agents settled four things the ruling did not name. A Student-t fit holds a latent scale for every row, and a row of zero weight keeps one drawn against an old fit, so setWeights redraws the scale of each row whose weight leaves zero, as setActiveRows redraws the latents of rows a mask switches back in; a mask lifted over a row whose weight is still zero draws nothing until the weight turns positive. A state carries only a digest of the weights it was stored under, so a Student-t state installed under other weights, by setState, a copy or a reload, has the scale of every row in the likelihood redrawn, where a test had pinned such an install between two positive weight vectors as changing nothing; installed under its own weights it draws nothing and the chain is the stored one. Drawing trees from the prior judges an empty leaf by membership, as the moves do, and so ignores weights. And a two-forest fit with no weight and no mask is not untouched, because its second forest starts multiplied by zero on the control rows: where the coefficients are drawn its draws shift and its posterior does not, and where they are held at (0, 1) every control row is weightless in that forest for the whole run and the posterior changes. The alternatives were leaving a stale scale in place, under which a larger sampler that changes weights every sweep is not exact (the transform of the scales failed a uniformity test at p 1.5e-60 before the redraw and passed after); a state that names its zero-weight rows, about 150 lines and a wider state, under which a restore under other weights would redraw only the rows that enter; and judging the prior draw by weight. Undoing a weight change by setState followed by setWeights leaves a Student-t sampler's scales drawn under the proposed weights for one sweep, and a sampler saved after a weight swap without storing its state reloads with redrawn scales; setState returns TRUE in both, which promises the trees and not the latents (dec-B234), and the manual gives the order that is exact. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B238], [dec-B251], [dec-B234].
Record: docs/plans/empty-leaf-membership.md. Marked: revised in part by the maintainer, 2026-10-06: a state names its zero-weight rows and a restore under other weights redraws only the rows that enter (dec-B277); the rest blank. [dec-A160]

**An interrupted run does what R's top level does, and a time limit stays an error**
Building dec-B256, the agents settled what the ruling left open. An interrupt no handler takes does what R's own does: it runs the function in options(interrupt = ) if one is set, otherwise prints the blank line and runs options(error = ), and then leaves by the first restart among browser, tryRestart and abort, so a fit started at a browser() prompt stays at that prompt; a handled one does none of it. Run on 2026-10-06 against a real SIGINT during Sys.sleep, 25 combinations of the two options and five ways of handling: the same blank lines, option calls and exit status in every one. The check that polls for an interrupt can also be left by an error, the one setTimeLimit raises, and that is re-raised as the error it is: read as an interrupt, as the first build had it, it escaped try() and left a parallel master waiting on its worker for good. The alternatives were invoking the abort restart alone, which drops a browser session and skips the options, and calling R's own interrupt entry, which is not documented API on every platform. Two things stay as they were and are queued: a run entry that takes a sweep callback turns a real signal into an error, and prior draws, predict and the latent refresh are not interruptible at all. stan4bart's test of an interrupted fit expects the condition's class. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B256].
Record: docs/plans/interrupt-is-interrupt.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B352, dec-B382; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A161]

**A basis formula is rebuilt at new rows wherever lm would rebuild it, and refuses nothing lm does not**
Building dec-B257's fix, the orchestrator and agents settled how far "as in lm" goes. A call with a training-rows method, scale(), poly(), ns() or bs(), predicts from the training rows when it is the whole right-hand side of a basis formula and also when it is an operand of arithmetic, parentheses or cbind() at the top of it, since model.frame gives each variable of a model formula its own treatment and ~ cbind(scale(w), scale(v)) is how two standardized columns are written; any other expression of the whole column, I(w - mean(w)) among them, is evaluated on the new rows, as lm evaluates it. The design had predict refuse an expression it could not rebuild; its test, the stored call evaluated on half the training rows, accepted or refused the same expression according to the order of the rows (a balanced column passed, an unbalanced one failed), so no refusal was added. With subset the centre and scale are those of the rows the fit used, where lm uses every row. Finding an operand's call evaluates the operand a second time at fit; R's generator is restored around it and its warning silenced, so a basis with a random operand draws what it drew before. The alternatives were rebuilding only a call that is the whole right-hand side, the refusal, and lm's centre under subset. The cost is that an expression lm gets wrong is wrong here in the same way and in silence; the help names which expressions are rebuilt and points to SafePrediction. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B257].
Record: docs/plans/basis-formula-terms.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: covered by earlier rulings dec-B270. [dec-A162]

**An NA thread count is refused with its cause**
The default n.threads on dbartsControl, xbart and rbart_vi is the guessed core count, which is NA on a system where the cores cannot be counted. An NA n.threads is refused with a message saying that guessNumCores returned NA because it could not count the cores and that n.threads must then be given; a value below one keeps the plain "must be a positive integer" message. The alternative, not taken, was leaving one message for NA and for a non-positive number. A user on such a system still has to pass n.threads, as before, but is now told why the default failed and what to do, where the old message read as if they had given a bad number. Not yet ruled on.
