Record: this register. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: an unstated thread count falls back to one thread without a message where the cores cannot be counted; a stated NA stays refused, under dec-B306. [dec-A163]

**The exhaustive-split cap is bounded at 30 in the bridge as well**
The control setting that sets how many present levels of a categorical predictor are enumerated exhaustively takes values from 2 to 30, 30 being the bound the control's own validity check already held, with the message naming the candidate count it implies (about 2^29 at the cap) and recommending 8 to 14. The bridge now refuses a value above 30 itself, for a control whose slot was written past the validity check, such as by assigning the slot on an existing object. The alternative, not taken, was trusting the control's check alone, under which a slot written past it reached the engine and asked it to enumerate an unbounded number of partitions. A user writing the slot directly and passing a value over 30 now gets an error at creation, not a sampler that appears to hang. Not yet ruled on.
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B374. [dec-A164]

**A state install keeps a pooled rule's missing bit and accepts a mask the plan refused**
Landing the rule that a state install drops a missing direction its column no longer routes, the agents departed from the plan in three places. A categorical rule over a pooled column (many levels) keeps its missing bit, as a forced predictor update leaves it, because rule equality compares the pooled words and clearing the bit on install alone would make a copy differ from its original; the rule routes nothing wrongly and tree moves leave its subtree alone until it is redrawn, and clearing it on both paths is queued. A categorical rule that sends every reachable level one way with no missing flag, on a column that never held a missing value, was refused as malformed and now installs and is merged, because a state does not record which columns held missing values and the build cannot tell it from a rule that split the missing value from every level. And the plan's promise that restored trees predict as the stored ones holds only for trees the filled predictors left no empty leaf in; the others match what a forced update gives. The alternatives were clearing the pooled bit on both paths, which changes draws after a pooled column is filled; refusing the mask as before; and promising identical prediction everywhere. The cost to a user is that a state with a genuinely malformed mask of that one shape is installed and merged, setState returning FALSE, where it was refused, and that a restored tree can predict differently from the stored one where a leaf was merged. Not yet ruled on.
Record: docs/plans/restore-missing-direction.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B378; covered by earlier rulings dec-B320. [dec-A165]

**A whole-matrix predictor replacement keeps a factor's declared levels**
When setPredictor replaces the whole predictor matrix with one that carries no level tables of its own, each factor column keeps the levels the sampler was made with, as a single-column update already did, so the number of levels is the declared count and not the largest code left in the new rows. The alternative, not taken and what shipped, rebuilt the stored matrix without them and counted levels from the codes present. A user whose new rows miss a factor's top level used to find the level count come up short and the sampler's own state refused by copy() and by a reload; now the level stays a declared level the trees may split on, and a replacement matrix that does carry level tables of its own is used as given, so a user who wants fewer levels must pass them. Not yet ruled on.
Record: this register. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B359. [dec-A166]

**A state stored in other response units makes setState return FALSE**
Under dec-B234, setState returns FALSE when any tree was changed on the way in. The agents counted a conversion of leaf values between response units as such a change, so a state stored under one response range and installed under another that implies different units returns FALSE, while two ranges that name the same units, such as a constant response's (c, c) and (c, c + 1), leave it TRUE and the install is bit for bit. The alternative, not taken, was treating a conversion as exact because it is a rescaling, the "to rounding" reading, under which only merges and dropped directions give FALSE. A caller that stores, proposes a change to the response and restores on rejection would otherwise take a converted chain for the stored one; the cost is that a caller who expects TRUE after a deliberate, harmless change of units sees FALSE and must read it as "the chain was rewritten", and that a stored leaf value of -0.0 comes back +0.0 even when the value stays TRUE. Latents re-derived under other weights leave it TRUE. Not yet ruled on.
Record: docs/plans/restore-status.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: a conversion between response units is a clean install and returns TRUE; an unforced setState leaves the sampler untouched where the state does not go in cleanly, under dec-B305. [dec-A167]

**split.probs is held to non-negative, finite entries with at least one positive**
A tree prior's split.probs is held to what sample() holds its prob: an entry that is negative or not finite is refused by name, and so is a specification with no positive entry, a single number included. Before this an all-zero vector raised R's "missing value where TRUE/FALSE needed", a vector mixing signs that summed to zero raised an error naming an internal slot, an infinite entry failed the same raw way, a single 0 or -1 was read as uniform, and a vector with no positive entry and at least one negative one was normalized and run, c(0, -1, 0) becoming (0, 1, 0), in released 0.9-34 as on the branch. The alternative, not taken, was refusing only the all-zero vector, leaving a negative weight to be turned into a probability by a negative sum. The cost is that a call released 0.9-34 ran, a specification whose entries are all zero or negative, is now an error; the manual never said such a vector was accepted, and NEWS does not report it. Not yet ruled on.
Record: docs/plans/single-forest-vars.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B338. [dec-A168]

**A column restriction on a single forest: the period column, an installed state's weights, and the direct door**
Building forest(vars = ) for a single declared forest, the agents made four calls the plan did not. On a discrete-time hazard fit the period column the package adds is always allowed on a single forest, whatever vars names, while the first of several forests takes vars as written, as it did before, so the two roads differ there and the help says so. A state installed into a restricted sampler under DART keeps whatever split probabilities it carried for excluded columns until DART's first update zeroes them, at most half the burn-in later; no draw reads them, but the reported variable probabilities show them until then. A model handed straight to new("dbartsSampler") or to setModel is held to the rule creation holds, that some allowed column has a positive split probability. And a sampler that views a subset of another's columns refuses a model carrying a restriction rather than reading past its columns. The alternatives were taking vars literally on a hazard fit, under which the hazard could not vary over periods unless the caller named a column they never supplied; zeroing the weights at install, which writes model into state; and leaving the direct door unchecked, under which such a forest split on its first allowed column only. The cost is the reporting lag under DART and the single against multi-forest difference on a hazard fit. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B241], [dec-B254].
Record: docs/plans/single-forest-vars.md; docs/design/single-forest-vars.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B379; revised under dec-B365. [dec-A169]

**setCutPoints takes a strictly increasing numeric grid, or the grid the column already holds**
Letting a changed cut grid be put back (dec-B231), the plan first had setCutPoints take any non-decreasing grid, since the sampler itself builds one with equal neighbours for a constant column. The first review showed such a grid does not survive a store and restore: a stored split names its cut by value and the rebuild puts it on the first index holding that value, so on a grid with every point tripled the next 30 draws moved by 0.569. The orchestrator took the narrow rule: a caller's grid is strictly increasing, and the one grid taken with equal neighbours is the grid the column holds at the call, bit for bit, which is all the undo needs; so setCutPoints(c(0.5, 0.5)) on a column holding another grid is refused, as it was, and the maintainer was told. The cost is that once the grid of a column whose own grid repeats a point has been changed, setCutPoints does not take the old one back and setState brings it. After the second review setCutPoints also takes only numeric vectors, refusing a character, a logical, a factor, a Date and NULL where it coerced them before, and takes a data frame as the list of its columns; released 0.9-34 coerced as well. A later setData derives the n.cuts points the sampler was created with, where it derived as many as the longest grid ever set, in 0.9-34 too; no NEWS line, the manual never having said which. The alternatives were the wide rule with the restore fixed first, and keeping the coercions. The restore's own defect is TODO repeated-cut-restore. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B231].
Record: docs/plans/cut-points-undo.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: a grid out of order is sorted, a repeated point and a non-numeric grid stay refused, and the restore's defect is fixed before the merge, under dec-B285. [dec-A170]

**The two forest arguments: what a held coefficient may sit on, where fixed lives, and a named sd**
Landing the first push of the written surface (dec-B266, dec-B269, dec-B271), the orchestrator made four calls its reviews and landing run raised. A held coefficient on a basis of one column is held at 0 on the tip, as update.amplitude = FALSE held it, so the forest drops out of the fit in silence; the multiplier slice makes that value 1 (not built), and until then amplitude = fixed() on a basis of one column is refused, at creation and by setForestBasis on a forest created with a basis. The maintainer was told and asked why 0; the alternative offered, building the held value ahead of the law, was left unanswered. fixed() has one public home, dbartsPriors, and resolves inside forests by the vocabulary interactions and blocks resolve by, so a caller's own variable named fixed wins; publishing it a second time under dbartsForests was the implementer's first form. The refusals of sd and amplitude on a model of one forest were reworded to say where the size goes. And the fitting function's normal(sd = ) takes a named single number as it always has, a number indexed out of a named vector keeping its name and two vignettes passing one, while forest(sd = ) refuses it under dec-B269 with a hint to unname() it; the plan had refused it at both, and the package build failed on the vignettes. Open for the maintainer: whether forest(sd = ) should take a named single number too. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B269], [dec-B271], [dec-B261].
Record: docs/plans/written-surface.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B353, dec-B374, dec-B380; covered by earlier rulings dec-B280. [dec-A171]

**The joint row update: numbers stay codes, differing level tables refuse, and labels for a numeric column refuse**
Fixing the joint row update for a factor (dec-B279), the orchestrator made three calls. Numbers given for a factor column stay what they were, the engine's category codes from 0, where setPredictor by column refuses a number; a caller moving a latent class inside a sampler holds codes, and the help now says that as.integer() of a factor counts from 1 and is not these. When the samplers in the list hold the column with different level tables, one vector is refused whatever its kind, labels or numbers, naming the first sampler that differs: the plan had refused labels only and told the caller to give numbers, which installs a different level in each sampler in silence; matching labels to each sampler's own levels would need the engine to take a vector per sampler and was not built. And a factor or text that is not numerals given for a numeric column is refused in the joint form, where it was read through its integer codes; the two column forms of setPredictor still read it so, and that stays in the backlog. The alternatives were refusing numbers for a factor column, as the column form does, and leaving numbers alone when the tables differ. Calls that passed numbers to samplers with differing level tables ran before and are refused now. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B279].
Record: docs/plans/factor-column-update-forms.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: numbers given for a factor column are refused, under dec-B286; the entry's other two calls are not marked. [dec-A172]

**The forests of a formula: when a forest's first argument is read, and what a removal takes**
Landing the second push of the written surface (dec-B267, dec-B268), the orchestrator made five calls its reviews raised, each where the push as planned fitted a different model without a message. A forest's first argument was kept as code and read when the sampler was built, so forests made in a loop all took the loop's last value, where the tip took each; forest() now evaluates the argument once, at the call, when it can, and keeps the value beside the code: code that names a predictor is that predictor's term, a predictor still hiding a caller's variable of the same name, and otherwise the value taken at the call is the selection. The same rule is written for the basis of the third push; the maintainer was told. The forest with no basis has the terms R gives the formula with that forest's call replaced by its contents and every forest with a basis deleted, so y ~ forest(.) - x3 + forest(x3, basis = ~ z) and y ~ . - x3 + forest(x3, basis = ~ z) are one model by construction, and a removal at the top never touches a forest that has a basis; a removal that names no plain term is passed by, as R passes it by, where the tip refused y ~ x1 + x2 + forest(x3, basis = ~ z) - x3. A forest written with no first argument is every predictor of the fit, and one given names by value beside a removal at the top is refused. In a list a term is matched to a predictor by its label as the fit holds it and one that matches none is refused, as are a bare numeral and a logical or factor given as a selection; names given by value in a formula bring their columns into the fit. Warnings from the argument are kept and raised when its value is used. The alternatives were the plan's capture at build, forcing nothing; refusing every removal beside a written first forest; and reading a list's terms by the text R writes for them. Costs: a forest keeps a value it may not use, data-sized when its terms name vectors in the caller's frame, and vars = x2[1:2] is refused when x2 is also a predictor. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B267], [dec-B268], [dec-B270].
Record: docs/plans/written-surface.md. Marked: in part by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: what a removal takes is kept as built, under dec-B287; the entry's other calls are not marked. [dec-A173]

**A state records its family, and another family's state is refused, a gaussian one included**
A saved state of one response family installed into a sampler of another: measured over twenty kinds of sampler, 15 of 380 ordered pairs left a sampler whose fits were not finite or whose next sweep did not return, a probit state into a logistic sampler among them. Every state now records the family that stored it, and setState, a copy and a reload refuse a state of another family by name before anything is touched, the message naming installTrees as the way to start one fit from another's trees. The orchestrator made three calls in it. A gaussian state, which holds no latents and installed into probit, logistic and aft samplers, is refused with every other pair: setState continues a chain, and a gaussian chain is not a probit one; the alternative kept a state with no latent block installable into any family, and one test pinned that. A state written before the record installs as it did, except that a Student-t, logistic or negative-binomial sampler refuses scales or Polya-Gamma variates that are not positive and finite; 13 of 784 such cross-family pairs that ran on the tip, all into weighted samplers, are refused by that floor. And a warm start is left taking any family's trees. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B254], [dec-B234].
Record: docs/plans/cross-family-state-install.md. Marked: revised by the maintainer, 2026-10-07: the family record and the refusal by family go, and the floor on precisions stays, under dec-B283. [dec-A174]

**The basis slice: every argument that holds rows is read once, and what a basis is cut to**
Landing the third push of the written surface (dec-B270, dec-B284), the orchestrator made five calls its four reviews raised, each where a build fitted other rows than the ones written, with no message. Every argument that holds rows, the first argument, data, test, subset, weights and offset, is evaluated once for a fit, the first argument before data, at every door, and everything downstream uses that value: subset = sample(n, 100) had cut a basis to other rows than the fit, on the tip too at most doors; data = d[sample(n), ] beside a subset had kept rows the subset excluded; and dbarts(x[i <- sample(n, 100), ], y[i]) had paired a response with other rows' predictors, the last two brought in by correction rounds. That one call now fits the first draw of the sample where the tip fitted the second, both right pairs. The values are handed on through an environment and not written into the inner call, so an error's stack does not print the data set. A level that subset empties is dropped where a basis is given as a value as it is where it is written as code, and a value of two levels left with one is refused in the words the code form is. cbind() in a basis stays refused, base::cbind being a matrix term in a model formula. And code evaluated in a frame of base R's own, the function lapply or Map applies, is taken as the value it has there, with a refusal by name for a basis handed on under one of mapply's own argument names. The alternatives were leaving R's rule that each use of an argument's expression evaluates it again, which is what lm does and what fitted other rows, and writing the values into the call. Costs: a caller who relied on two evaluations of one expression giving two draws gets one; pdbart on a kept fit still reads its stored call's data again. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B270], [dec-B284], [dec-A173].
Record: docs/plans/written-surface.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B351, dec-B380. [dec-A175]

**The response-scale rule is built in four slices, and the calls its plans make**
dec-B296 was designed as one slice; the design's critic found it behind dec-B303 and dec-B304, found its re-derivation unexamined and its claim of unchanged bits false on the prototype, and proposed four slices, which the orchestrator took on 2026-10-07: the survival fit's re-anchor read from observed times alone, where today each chain re-anchors to its own drawn censored times (run: two chains, shifts 1.872 and 2.098); creation only, the range, the count centre and the unit of a forest's sd over the rows in the likelihood at creation, with updateScale = TRUE on the two existing response setters reading the rows in and no new argument; the two new arguments on setWeights and setActiveRows; and dec-B304 with the slices that give derived data its record. The first two are planned. Calls in them, the orchestrator's and the planner's: with no row out every number keeps the base build's bits, a constraint and a gate; two exact arms are added that fail on the base build; the test for fewer than two distinct values (dec-B309) reads the count for a count fit, so rows that all hold one positive count take the fallback though their centre is defined; the fallback's warning is raised only by families that take something from the response, a probit or ordinal sampler under a mask of zeros being created in silence as today; in the fallback the starting sigma is the fit without the weights and its own warning is not also raised; a re-derivation through the two existing setters where the rows in hold fewer than two distinct values reads every row with no message, the refusal dec-B302 names waiting for the third slice, since stan4bart makes that call at every warm-up iteration; creation under a mask is R's install followed by the sampler's own re-derivation, for the count family only, a sampler made by hand keeping the every-row centre unless its caller does the same, which the help says; xbart warns once and a fold falls back in silence; and rbart_vi's rel.scale is left out, rbart_vi being deprecated. For the third slice, not planned: a re-derivation on a call that changes no response keeps the fitted function and restates the prior, the critic having measured the live fit moving by up to 15.07 with no data change where the leaves' internal numbers are kept; the new argument goes after updateState, so 0.9-34's positional setWeights(w, TRUE) keeps its meaning; and a sampler of several forests, whose unit cannot be re-derived, stands against dec-B303's updateBasisScale reading the rows in at its call. The alternatives were the one slice, a warning in every family, a second test for the count family, and the refusal now. Open for the maintainer: a hazard fit's periods are taken from the times of subjects at weight zero (133, 197 or 199 person-period rows for the same subjects), since ruled (dec-B315); the starting sigma is never re-derived, since ruled (dec-B314); the several-forests point. The rest is not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B296], [dec-B302], [dec-B303], [dec-B304], [dec-B309].
Record: docs/plans/response-scale-rows.md; docs/plans/aft-reanchor-observed-times.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B362, dec-B363, dec-B381; revised under dec-B364; covered by earlier rulings dec-B314, dec-B315, dec-B327; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A176]

**The leaf conversions: a covariate without spread, a constant centred exactly, and a single value off its centre**
Landing the first push of the leaf conversions, the orchestrator and its reviewer settled three things the plan did not. Where a linear leaf's covariate has no spread on either side of a conversion, at setData or a warm start, a live leaf is left as it is, the fit being its intercept before and after; with spread on one side only the slope goes to zero and the intercept keeps the fit; kept draws are rewritten by the formula in every case. The first build converted by the formula there and moved a live fit by 8523 against a fit sd of 1.46. A constant leaf covariate is centred at its value exactly: taken through the sum, the mean of a constant 0.1 over 150 rows missed it by rounding, the column got 2.5e-16 as its scale, and the base build predicted about 1e14 a little off the constant; the same constants now draw what a constant 1000 draws, and 20 fits on columns with spread are identical to the base. That changes what a fit on such a column draws, which the plan's first constraint had ruled out and was amended for. And a column that setPredictor moved to another single value is converted by the formula, as a column with spread is: the function of the covariate is kept exactly, but the data fixed that function at one point, and setData onto a widely spread column carries the live fit from about 5 either way to about 1500 until the sampler has run; the build before the slice kept the fit near, by a mark that had gone stale. The reviewer offered reading such a side as without spread, which a warm start cannot do from a state that does not record the value, or letting the formula stand with the help naming the case, and recommended the second for this push; the orchestrator took it and filed the first with the slice that moves the centre and scale to the rows in the likelihood (dec-B304). The alternatives were the formula everywhere, the rounding-sized scale as built, and a third code round now. The cost is that a caller who makes a placeholder column, sets it to another constant and then replaces the data sees the fit jump with only the help to say why. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B200], [dec-B233], [dec-B304].
Record: docs/plans/leaf-conversions.md, Landing note; root TODO, leaf-covariate-single-value-off-centre. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B328, dec-B358; revised under dec-B329. [dec-A177]

**The cut grid that repeats no point: the calls of its plan**
Reworking the plan for dec-B297 to dec-B300, the planner made calls the rulings did not. "The distinct points the values supply" is read as today's derivation with equal neighbours dropped, the uniform rule's n.cuts evenly spaced points and the quantile rule's midpoints, so a column of few values far apart, a 0/1 column among them, keeps its grid and its draws to the bit, and what moves is a column narrower than its points resolve or one finite value beside missing values; the alternative, one point per gap between distinct values under the uniform rule too, changes every fit with a discrete column. A refresh that changes a column's number of points keeps each split's position, as a refresh does today: a split past a shorter grid is merged when the update is forced, and when it is not the call returns FALSE with the column and its grid as they were, dec-B299's "never fails" and "never keeps the old grid" being read of a refresh that is accepted; the alternative moves the column's splits by value, which keeps more of them and needs the trees put back on a rollback. A column with every value missing gets one point at 0. A warm-start donor whose grid repeats a point is refused as a state is, where dec-B300 names setState, a copy and a reload; only a state from an earlier build of this branch can hold one, and it is refused, not converted, the format number staying. A stored split that falls outside its node's interval is merged and reported by the return value, not refused. dec-B285's exception for the grid a column already holds goes, nothing holding a repeat any more, and setPredictor's error for too few cut points with it. Two exact arms are added that a moved fit reaches, and no recorded baseline moves. The alternatives are named with each. Two of the calls were ruled otherwise on 2026-10-07: how the default rule counts a column's points (dec-B313) and what a refresh does to the splits on a column (dec-B311, dec-B312). The rest is not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B285], [dec-B297], [dec-B298], [dec-B299], [dec-B300], [dec-B305], [dec-B311], [dec-B313].
Record: docs/plans/repeated-cut-restore.md, Calls made in planning. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B378; revised under dec-B311, dec-B313; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A178]

**Defaults by a forest's kind: the calls of its first push, and what a carried control does**
Building and landing the first push of dec-B274, the agents and the orchestrator settled what the plan did not. The control a sampler holds shows its first forest's count and carries, unseen, the count a new fit should take for its forest with no basis; given to a new fit as it is, it hands on the carried count, and a count the caller writes into it stands as stated, the first build having put the carried count back over it without a message (written 20, held 75). A written count equal to the one the control shows cannot be told from no edit, and the carried count wins there. bartBT records what its own caller stated, as bart does, so a model whose every forest has a basis is created through it when nothing is stated, with 50 and 50 trees under dec-B274 where the build before gave 200 and 50, and its ntree, power, base, splitprobs and k are refused there under their own names; the alternative was refusing a data object with bases at bartBT. A tree prior, leaf prior or k written as NULL counts as named on such a model, NULL being the default's own value; of power and base both named, power is the one refused; print reads the engine while the sampler is live and the record after a reload; setControl takes the control a sampler was created under where its first forest has a basis. Two refusals are new against the build before and are left for the control migration: a control taken from a bart() or bartBT() fit, given to dbarts() for a model whose every forest has a basis, is refused as naming a count though its caller named none, those functions building their control by a call that names it; and setControl given an all-basis sampler's control on another sampler is refused as changing the count. Both are loud, and a fresh dbartsControl() works. The refusal's remedy, to state the count on a forest, cannot be written at bartBT or at bart() given a data object, and is reworded in the second push. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B274], [dec-B239], [dec-B240], [dec-A158].
Record: docs/plans/forest-defaults-by-kind.md, Landing note; root TODO, control-carried-count-edges. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B380; revised under dec-B385; covered by earlier rulings dec-B247; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A179]

**The gp refusals and the survival re-anchor: the calls made building them**
Building the last step of the leaf conversions and the survival re-anchor as one push, the implementer and the orchestrator settled what the plans left. A sampler with Gaussian-process leaves "holds kept draws" where a run has recorded one, so one created to keep trees and not yet run, or run as burn-in alone, is served; the guard runs after the refusal a sampler of several forests already gives, and in setData after the refusals already there. Its cost, found in review: a burn-in loop that re-anchors and then keeps a draw at each pass, setOffset(updateScale = TRUE) followed by run(0, 1), was served before and stops at its second pass; the same loop run as burn-in sweeps is served, and the help says so, the message keeping its planned text. The refusal of a range re-derivation on such a sampler is the orchestrator's call of 2026-10-06, taken because a Gaussian-process kept draw cannot be rewritten into another range, and is not dec-B237's, which rules the standardization; the design note had cited the ruling for both. For the survival fit: the range is read from the observed times at creation and at every re-derivation, a censored row's censoring time among them, where each chain had re-anchored to its own drawn times (two chains at (2.003, 5.536) and (2.032, 5.594) against (1.383, 4.401) from the observed times, in the review's run); the routine that takes a range is handed the vector to read, so that the slice changing the rows edits one place; no virtual changed; and a re-derivation straight after setResponse, a status change or a row brought back by the mask is in the changed class, each of those redrawing censored rows without a sweep, the plan's first wording having called the first of them neutral. The alternatives were refusing only where a kept draw would move, which needs a preview of the transform, a message naming the burn-in remedy, and the survival fit computing its own range. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B237], [dec-B200], [dec-B296], [dec-A176].
Record: docs/plans/leaf-conversions.md and docs/plans/aft-reanchor-observed-times.md, Landing notes. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B330, dec-B357; covered by earlier rulings dec-B237; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A180]

**Selecting a forest by its label: the calls of its push**
Building dec-B276, the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the plan did not. A vector of strings given to a method that takes one forest is refused as needing a single number or a single label; a class that is neither a number nor a string is refused by its class name, complex, raw, dates and version numbers among them, which the build before took as numbers; matching is exact, with no partial matching and no folding of case. In the two lists given forest by forest, setLeafPrior(forests = ) and predict(bases = ), a name may be the forest's label or forest<i> at its own position, the second of which the build before refused; a name that is another forest's label is refused there even where it also spells this position's forest<i>, the first build having taken it and written the wrong forest. Labels are recorded only for a model of several forests, so a sampler of one forest, one declared as a list of one named forest, and a multinomial sampler take positions alone, as before. And where every forest has a basis, the refusal of a count, tree prior, leaf prior, interactions or blocks given to bartBT, or to bart() with a data object, ends "leave it out, or fit with dbarts()", the orchestrator's ruling on the first push's review, the remedy of stating it on a forest not being writable at those doors. The alternatives were coercing other classes as before, partial matching as `$` does, and reading a list by position alone. A caller who passed a date or a complex number as a forest's position is now refused. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B276], [dec-B274], [dec-A179].
Record: docs/plans/forest-defaults-by-kind.md, Landing note; docs/plans/small-r-batch.md for the two wordings. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B316, dec-B374, dec-B380. [dec-A181]

**The small R batch: where the thread fallback is read, a NULL prior argument, and two refusals**
Building dec-B306 and dec-B310 with two backlog items, the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. The fallback to one thread is applied in the bodies of dbartsControl, bart, rbart_vi and xbart and not in their defaults, so that the usage shows exported names only; it therefore reads whether a count was stated, and a wrapper that passes on its own defaulted count, NA where the cores cannot be counted, is refused where one that hands on its dots gets one thread. The first build put an internal function in the four defaults; the second lost the fallback at bart() given a control, which the review caught before landing. A stated NA is told what the count must be and that leaving it out gives the default; a control whose slot is NA is refused at dbarts() and bart() in words naming the slot, and so is refused beside a stated count, a call that ran before and that only a slot assignment can make. A NULL power or base is read as the default by the builder every bart door reaches, by rbart_vi, and by cgm() and dart(), the last two so that the remedy the retired spelling's warning names works; on a model whose every forest has a basis a NULL stays refused as naming the plain forest's prior (dec-A179). forceUpdate = NA at setPredictor is refused by name before anything changes, where the whole-matrix form had installed the predictors before stopping. The news names the four doors that refused an uncounted machine in 0.9-34 and says nothing of the rest, the release returning NULL from a forced update too. The alternatives were the helper in the defaults, a NULL refused by name, and leaving the two raw errors. bartCause's bcf computes its own count from guessNumCores() and states it, so the ruling does not reach it (root TODO, bartcause-thread-default). Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B306], [dec-B310], [dec-A179], [dec-A163].
Record: docs/plans/small-r-batch.md, Calls made in planning and Landing note. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B332, dec-B374, dec-B380; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A182]

**The cut grid that repeats no point: the calls of building it and of its fix round**
Building dec-B297 to dec-B300 with dec-B311 and dec-B312, the implementer, its reviewers and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. Under "position" a split at place i of n, counted from 0, goes to floor((2 i + 1) m / (2 n)) on a grid of m points, the point under the centre of its share of the old grid; two splits that land on one point are then placed as the move by value places them, and an interval left empty is merged. An unforced refresh is refused, the sampler as it was, where after the move a split's interval or a leaf would be empty or a monotone tree out of order; a split moved to another point of its interval is taken. A forced refresh and setCutPoints merge an emptied interval weighing each leaf by the rows it held, as the merge of an empty leaf does, while setData's move keeps weighing by the last sweep's statistics, so that a sampler and its copy draw alike. A refresh that leaves a column's grid as it was runs the release's path. The engine takes the rule beside its flag and three facade virtuals gain the argument; the bridge reads it as a code in the argument that carried the logical, so neither predictor entry changes arity and the flat C header does not move. The logical's reader lives with the tombstones and only turns a logical into its word with the warning. Three readings of dec-B312 stand as the orchestrator's: an explicit FALSE warns as TRUE does, a word is matched as match.arg matches, and setCutPoints stays forced. 0.9-34 also took 1, 0, "TRUE" and "T" for updateCutPoints, read as a logical; they are refused now with the three words and no warning, dec-B312 naming a logical only. On review, two lines of the R tests can never fail, a split being read back off its grid, and a build keeping the index on a shorter grid at setCutPoints passes every R test; the C++ tests catch it. The alternatives were a position kept by index on a grid of another length, and the merge weighing leaves by node statistics everywhere. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B311], [dec-B312], [dec-B297], [dec-A178].
Record: docs/plans/repeated-cut-restore.md, Calls made in building, Calls made in the fix round and Landing note. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B317, dec-B319, dec-B345, dec-B378; covered by earlier rulings dec-B311; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-A183]

**The check-shape fixes: the calls made building them**
Building dec-B301 with the weekly run of the exact gates, the implementer, three reviewers and the orchestrator settled what the plan did not. The count model's mixing gate takes 4000 draws a chain in full mode and 2000 in quick, its full mode passing a correct sampler in 160 of 160 runs over fresh engine streams on its six pinned datasets, where at 2000 one dataset failed about one stream in 100; the longer chains catch a shape moved once in 20 sweeps 1 time in 12 where 2000 caught it 7 times, which the header says. Its truth-in-set check reads a 99.9% set per run and a 95% set over the full mode's six runs, allowing two misses, and its coverage band is sized from a correct sampler's spread across datasets. The probit-k arm ranks k over 99 kept draws so that each of 20 bins holds five values, runs 600 replications inside a 180-minute job, and flags k on its chi-square p beside its band. Each weekly gate has its own time limit, so one that hangs does not take the rest. The by-hand calibration step and its reading are in benchmarks/README.md. The alternatives were swapping the slow pinned dataset, a looser set check, and the arm at fewer replications. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B301], [dec-A132].
Record: docs/plans/check-shape-fixes.md, Calls made in planning and Landing note. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: revised under dec-B371. [dec-A184]

**The small R-surface rulings of 2026-10-08: the calls made building them**
Building the eleven R-surface rulings of 2026-10-08 (dec-B330, dec-B333, dec-B340, dec-B341, dec-B346, dec-B359, dec-B360, dec-B367, dec-B370, dec-B372, dec-B374, dec-B376), the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. A fit stores its training offset as offset, and a hazard fit with a test grid its test offset as offset.test, since a fit without trees cannot otherwise replace its own offset. predict with no newdata refuses weights and bases by name; rbart_vi's predict is untouched, needing a group.by. A weight of 0 draws its predictive at weight 1 on test-sample weights too. A one-class hazard fit keeps its refusal, the ruling naming probit and logistic. A held forest coefficient is recorded as 1 per forest, named forest<i>. survivalProbabilities on a hazard fit given an offset and no newdata shifts the stored test grid on the latent scale where the fit stored one and replays the trees where it did not, as it does with no offset: the training draws stop at each subject's event period, so the stored-draws premise of dec-B340 holds only with a grid. The starting sigma of a sparse indicator design comes from Matrix's sparse QR on unit-norm columns, entered in batches so the QR never sees more columns than rows, a column whose pivot vanishes dropped and the QR redone, Matrix's structural rank warning muffled; it matches lm.fit's on the dense design to 1e-16 over more than 60 designs. A caller's own sparse columns keep the marginal-sd fallback and its warning. A factor at density exactly sparseDensityThreshold is built sparse. A numeric matrix is refused on a design with a factor column also by the sampler's predict and predictForests and by test = at creation. The starting-sigma change moves the equivalence scenario wideFactorIndicators, re-recorded. The alternatives were requiring trees for an offset, letting weights and bases through, refusing the hazard offset without a grid, and densifying the design for the sigma. The cost is two new fit elements and a sparse least-squares path the package maintains. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B340], [dec-B341], [dec-B370], [dec-B372], [dec-B376].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, Calls made in the build and Landing note. Marked: blank. [dec-A185]

**treatSens's null treatment model on several chains: the calls made building it**
Building dec-B387 as dec-B395 left it on treatSens's dbarts-1.0 branch (4a4e1e3, 4ab0fc9), the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. The null model's chain count is read from dbarts's bart default rather than written as 4, so it follows dbarts; its threads are min(nthreads, chains), or treatSens's core guess capped at the chains when nthreads is not given, so treatSens's nthreads caps the null fit too; the draws are not trimmed to nsim, since every reader averages over them; the tests that pin the thread cap stop at the null fit's call and the default-thread test mocks the core guess to two, so no test runs more than two processes (the maintainer: "treatSens will go to CRAN at some point - don't make permanent decisions based on its current status"). The alternatives were a fixed chain count, a separate thread argument and trimming to nsim. The cost is that the null fit's chain count moves whenever dbarts's default does. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B387], [dec-B395].
Record: treatSens dbarts-1.0 branch, commits 4a4e1e3, 4ab0fc9 and babfaa6. Marked: blank. [dec-A186]

**The engine rulings of 2026-10-08: the calls made building them**
Building dec-B329, dec-B365, dec-B366, dec-B386, dec-B391 and dec-B356 with dec-B369, dec-B392 and dec-B393, the planner, a blind critic, the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. Where a leaf covariate holds one value on both sides of setData or a warm start, centres differing included, a live leaf stays as built (dec-B329's both-sides clause), while a saved draw takes the formula so it stays the function it was. n.perturb.cuts takes a positive whole number or Inf, stored as a double, an integer written to the slot taken as its value; anything at or above the cut cap is clamped to 65533 in the bridge, exact because the window is clipped to the node's interval, so Inf means any cut in the interval; 0, negatives, NA and fractions are refused by name. setControl installs a changed width, like a changed mixture, on the mean forest only. A constant response keeps (c, c) as its recorded pair, the c - 0.5 to c + 0.5 window applied inside the range-0 handling, an empty response following the same rule. setLeafPrior keeps the spread through a new engine primitive, Chain::scaleDrawnK, which the install surface's state-install-keeps-spread will reuse. The leaf-statistics cache prices each entry's crossproduct, so at a binding budget fewer entries are cached. The alternatives were the formula on both-sides-constant live leaves, refusing widths past the cap, recording the window, and keeping k across setLeafPrior. The cost is a double-typed count setting and a slower cache at a binding budget. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B329], [dec-B366], [dec-B386], [dec-B391], [dec-B393].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-engine.md, Open calls and Landing note. Marked: blank. [dec-A187]

**copy() after an n.samples edit and with no stored state: the calls made building them**
Fixing copy-after-nsamples-edit and building dec-B396 (5982c39a, 2985ff24), the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the ruling did not. A copy installs its state as a reload does, taking the state's saved-tree capacity when n.samples was edited after the store, the saved trees kept rather than truncated or reset. With no stored state, copy() reads the sampler's current state from the engine for the copy alone: the read draws nothing, the source's state field stays empty, and the source runs bitwise as if never copied. The state install shared by the reload, $setState and copy() is one function, installStateOnto(), shown bitwise unchanged on every path. The alternatives were refusing the copy after an edit and caching the read state on the source. The cost is one engine state read per copy of such a sampler. A gp-leaf copy continuing its source only approximately, older than this change, is TODO gp-copy-continuation. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B396].
Record: R/dbarts.R copy() and installStateOnto(); inst/tinytest/test-copy-nsamples-edit.R. Marked: blank. [dec-A188]

**The tinytest suite's stray warnings: the calls made removing them**
Clearing suite-stray-warnings (686bc0fc, 2ea3ad78; 278 warnings outside expectations to none), the implementer, its reviewer and the orchestrator settled how each warning goes. A test that exercises a warning path pins it with a strict expect_warning and a message pattern, so a second warning inside the same call fails; one that warns only through its setup changes the setup: sparse fits pass sigest (sd(y), the fallback's own value, where a draw-dependent expectation needs it), BayesTree-spelled calls go to bartBT directly, test matrices are named, the cgm prior replaces the power shorthand, and rm() names only objects that exist. The alternatives were pinning every warning where it fires, which keeps the incidental paths running but ties each test to messages it does not test. The cost is coverage carried by fewer files: the bart() forwarding shim is pinned only in test-front-door.R, the shorthand warnings only in the surface tests, and predicting from an unnamed matrix against named predictors only in test-data-compatibility.R. Whether a plain fit on a sparse x should warn at all is TODO sparse-starting-sigma. Not yet ruled on.
Record: commits 686bc0fc and 2ea3ad78. Marked: approved by the maintainer, 2026-10-09 ("Approved."). [dec-A189]

**The probit rescaling step: the calls made building it**
Building dec-B371 as dec-B397 left it (no switch), the planner, a blind critic, two implementers, the reviewer and the orchestrator settled what the rulings did not. A forest's cached fits are scaled in place with the leaves while the accumulated factor stays within [1/2, 2] and re-derived from the leaves past that, every rebuild resetting the factor; the relative rounding gap does not compound, and the per-sweep cost on x86 measured 1.000 to 1.027 of the step-free sampler. The step is the only change to the kernel, so the five equivalence scenarios that fit one probit forest with a drawn k were re-recorded although two moved past |z| 4 (chik 4.44, bart2probit 4.91): long chains, 60,000 draws on 8 chains a side for bart2probit, put k.mean at 1.816 with the step and 1.811 without against the old baseline's 1.936, so the old baseline's short chains had not converged in k, and the exact gate is the oracle. The engine keeps an internal toggle for its own tests only, with no bridge or R setter, hidden or not; the tinytest pairs that compared the step on and off went with the switch, replaced by tests/cpp checks that the step acts on every chain, declines without a draw where it does not apply and leaves an empty leaf alone, and by the re-recorded mover list as the record of which fits take it; the exact gate's step-off arm and the mixing script's off arm went too, their evidence kept in the design note. The probit-k calibration arm stays at thin 1000: with the step every dataset keeps 50 or more effective draws of its 99, and at thin 300 8% fall under 50. k's mixing under logistic and negative binomial was measured before 1.0-0, as the slice's plan required: logistic is slow where probit was, and its fix goes to the maintainer. The alternatives were a switch (dec-B394, withdrawn by dec-B397), re-deriving the cached fits every sweep (the slice's first build, costlier at small n), a test-only bridge setter, and thin 300. The cost is that the step's effect can no longer be re-measured from R, and that rbart_vi's binary fit, which takes the step through its offset path, has no test or baseline of its own. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B371], [dec-B394], [dec-B397].
Record: docs/plans/probit-k-scale-move.md, Landing note; docs/design/probit-k-scale-move.md; docs/design/k-mixing-pg-families.md. Marked: revised in part by the maintainer, 2026-10-09: the probit-k calibration arm runs at thin 700 after a 10,000-sweep burn (fewest effective draws 49 to 55 of 99 on the census by two estimates, about 60% of thin 1000's cost), "use the reduction on the thinning for now but otherwise approved. We'll revisit everything k-related as we gather the evidence."; the rest approved. [dec-A190]

**The order of the engine slices after 2026-10-09's rulings**
Shown the order gp-copy-continuation, the width-weighted default cut rule (dec-B406, dec-B409), the k-internal plan carrying dec-B418's removal of install conversion (dec-B416 to dec-B418), setstate-force-update in its two parts, response-scale-rows, then the logistic scale move (dec-B404) and whatever the sampler survey (dec-B415) recommends, against setstate-force-update first, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Up to you. I don't care how this lands." The orchestrator's call: the order shown, so that the k-internal slice removes the conversion and k-rescaling steps before setState and response-scale-rows would build or test them, and a new k sampler is written once against k's final meaning. The alternative was setState first, building steps the k-internal slice deletes. The cost is that setState and response-scale-rows wait behind two slices, all before the merge. See also: [dec-B416], [dec-B418].
Record: docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md, open call 4. Marked: blank. [dec-A191]

**gp leaves in observation order: the calls made building it**
Building the fix for gp-copy-continuation (c40d2f97..f9674313), the orchestrator, the implementer and the reviewer settled what no ruling did. Every gp computation on a leaf under the size cap reads the leaf's members sorted by observation index, skipping the sort when a span is already in order, and kernel-cache eviction keeps an entry on bottom-ness and member count with the lookup re-checking the sorted members; draws change for gp fits, not their distribution. The re-record's oracle was the gp, gp-weighted and gp-mixed SBC arms (no arm flagged on two seeds) beside the permutation argument, and bench-sampler's compare was waived since none of its arms builds a gp leaf. The speed A/B timed both builds from one restored state, a cold first sweep and two warm ones, since diverging chains swung the plan's paired ratios by 30 percent; it found cap 256 about 4 percent faster warm and cap 64 between 1.00 and 1.02 pooled across two runs on a loaded laptop, with four cells above the plan's 1.03 in the noisier run; the slice landed on that, its x86 rerun owed (TODO gp-speed-x86). Two test blocks moved their gp cap so the fallback share sits clear of the 25 percent that reports it (test-mutate-then-serialize.R 100 to 50; test-gp-leaves.R's data-handle block 100 to 50, its warning pinned by count). The plan's blind critique was skipped, against the process for a change to draws; the review ran eleven mutants instead. The alternatives were holding the landing for the x86 rerun, and raising the test caps (seven times slower). The cost is a possible cap-64 slowdown of a few percent until measured, and a plan no second reader critiqued. See also: dec-A188.
Record: docs/plans/gp-copy-continuation.md, Landing note. Marked: blank. [dec-A192]

**sigest beside a fixed scale: the calls made building the agreeing case**
Building dec-B413 (60397152, b334fa89), the implementer settled what the ruling did not. The agreeing sigest's message prints once per session, not on every call, since the two loops outside CRAN that make the call would otherwise repeat it every iteration; the accepted call is a no-op, so a silent repeat hides nothing. Agreement is within 4 machine epsilons relative to the square root of the fixed variance: sqrt is correctly rounded, so a hand-typed root such as 0.3 beside fixed(0.09) is off by an ulp or two, and anything looser would accept a different scale. rbart_vi and bartBT take no family and cannot reach a fixed scale. The alternatives were a message on every call and exact equality. The cost is that a user who misses the first message is not told again. See also: dec-B413.
Record: R/family.R, refuseSigestUnderFixedPrior; inst/tinytest/test-sigest-fixed-agree.R. Marked: blank. [dec-A193]

## B. Decisions with maintainer evidence

**Missing predictors are modelled, not refused**
A missing predictor value no longer stops a fit: the sampler sends the missing cases down one side of a split, chosen as part of the rule, so the missingness itself can carry signal. The alternative, refusing any design with missing values, survives as a strictness setting named "error". A user fitting a design with missing predictors gets a fit rather than an error, and both the number of rows used and the fitted values differ from 0.9-x. The maintainer set the name and the default to "incorporate" and kept "error" as the strictness escape. At the tip that escape is reachable only by writing the slot on the model object, since no front-door argument spells it; the modern front door instead takes an na.action argument that drops rows with a missing response, and the BayesTree-style door drops any row with a missing value. See also: [dec-B108], [dec-B75].
Record: docs/design/mia-missingness.md. Marked: mine; superseded in part by dec-B108: na.action replaced the front-door spelling, so the escape is slot-only and bart no longer rejects NAs. [dec-B01]

**The swap tree move ships switched off**
The tree kernel keeps the swap proposal, which exchanges split rules between a node and its parent, but its default probability is zero and its share of the proposal mass goes to the birth and death moves. The BayesTree-style door, bartBT, keeps BayesTree's own mixture, with swap at 0.1. The alternative, and the first ruling, was deleting the move from the kernel outright; that removal was then partly reversed. Every fit's draws differ from 0.9-x, and outside bartBT the mixture of proposals is one of the reasons; a user of the modern doors fitting a single tree who wants swap back has to set it. The maintainer first ruled that "The swap move is removed from the MCMC kernel before 1.0", then that "The removal is PARTIALLY reversed", which leaves the move present at zero weight, and later asked that bartBT keep BayesTree's mixture.
Record: docs/design/swap-removal.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B02]

**The change move's acceptance ratio is repaired**
The change move, which redraws the split rule at an internal node, was missing the proposal-density term in its acceptance ratio; the repair uses restricted proposals with explicitly counted ratios for ordinal variables and draws from the prior for categorical ones. The alternatives were other variants measured against each other, and the maintainer picked this hybrid. Every 1.0-0 fit therefore differs from every dbarts and BayesTree fit since the package began, with no switch back to the old behaviour. The hybrid "is now THE change move".
Record: docs/design/change-move-balance.md; docs/plans/archive/change-move-fix.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B03]

**chi() means the degrees of freedom it names**
The chi hyperprior on the leaf-scale parameter k drew from a distribution with twice the shape its argument named; the constructor now samples the stated degrees of freedom, and the binary default is relabelled so the prior it names is the prior it draws. The alternative was relabelling the argument to match the old behaviour, a documentation fix rather than a code fix; the maintainer picked the code fix. A user who wrote an explicit chi with 1.25 degrees of freedom in 0.9-x gets a different prior now, while the relabelled default draws the same values it always did.
Record: docs/plans/correctness-audit.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B04]

**bart and bart2 were to differ by intent**
The package was to keep two entry points that differ permanently: bart reproducing BayesTree's defaults and bart2 carrying the current recommendations, with the same functionality behind both. No alternative was weighed. The same data would give different answers depending on which name a user called. That is no longer the shape: a later ruling makes bart itself the modern front door and moves the BayesTree-style names and defaults to a new function. See also: [dec-B75].
Record: docs/plans/archive/bart2-argument-consolidation.md. Marked: superseded by dec-B75. [dec-B05]

**setResponse keeps its argument order**
The sampler's setResponse method keeps the argument order it shipped with in 0.9-34, and warns once per session when the second argument is supplied positionally. That order matches the offset setter's, which is the reason it was kept; reordering it was the alternative. An embedding loop written against 0.9-x that passes the second argument positionally silently changes meaning after that single warning. The maintainer ruled to "Keep setResponse's argument order ... and warn once per session when the second argument is supplied positionally."
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B06]

**One argument order for every predict method**
Every predict method in the package takes its arguments in the same order: the fitted object, the new data, the kind of prediction, then anything further. The alternative, rejected, was leaving the orders as they were and only refusing unrecognized argument names. A 0.9-x positional call silently changes meaning wherever the order moved, so a user who relied on position has to check. The maintainer chose the single order.
Record: docs/plans/prerc-surface-freeze.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B07]

**Fractional counts are refused, not truncated**
Wherever a caller can write a count, a column index or a group selector by hand, a fractional value is refused by name rather than silently truncated. No alternative was weighed; the ruling was a directive to finish the rule everywhere. A 0.9-x script that computes a count and passes it without rounding now errors. A later ruling narrows the rule to counts and selectors only, never to weights. See also: [dec-B103].
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md. Marked: superseded by dec-B103. [dec-B08]

**Unknown arguments warn; one is refused**
Each predict method refuses an argument named value by name, and any other unrecognized name in the dots produces a warning rather than an error, through a check the package implements itself. The alternatives were erroring on any unknown name or ignoring them silently; the maintainer chose warning, "grounded in R standards", including when the name arrives through a subclass's own method dispatch. A user whose wrapper forwards its own dots into a dbarts call sees a warning, and code that relied on the 0.9-x deprecation warning for value now gets an error. The ruling named base R's chkDots for the job; the package's own utility source states why it does not use it.
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md; docs/plans/archive/bart2-argument-consolidation.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B09]

**The modern front door stops accepting dots**
The modern front door takes no dots channel: an argument it does not name is refused, so every control knob has to be a named argument of its own. The alternatives ranged from keeping dots as a pass-through to deleting them outright, and the maintainer picked refusal with a stated sunset toward deletion. Any control field not promoted to a named argument was therefore unreachable from that entry point. That rule no longer holds: the front door and the cross-validation entry both take a control object again, so control settings are reachable without one argument apiece. See also: [dec-B116].
Record: docs/plans/archive/bart2-argument-consolidation.md. Marked: mine; dec-B116 reverses the formals-only rule, giving bart and xbart control = dbartsControl(). [dec-B10]

**xbart refuses a three-element n.burn**
The cross-validation entry point refuses a burn-in vector longer than two elements by name, where 0.9-34 accepted three and ignored the third. No alternative was weighed. A user who copies the documented 0.9-x default gets an error. The maintainer's later ruling on cross-validation, three options put, kept the refusal as a tombstone until 1.1-0: "go ahead with your recommendation". See also: [dec-B77].
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md; this register, for the ruling in dec-B77. Marked: not mine. [dec-B11]

**The cross-validated k grid for binary fits**
Cross-validation over the leaf-scale parameter k for a binary outcome was to use a grid of fixed values, matching the gaussian arm, rather than the hyperprior the modern default fits. The alternative was leaving the binary axis on its own semantics. The cross-validated k then no longer matches what the front door fits by default, and 0.9-x results on that axis are not comparable. A later ruling reopens it: the grid now accepts fixed values and an entry that leaves k modelled. See also: [dec-B102].
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md. Marked: superseded by dec-B102. [dec-B12]

**Weighted binary fits mean counts, not scaled latents**
The weighted probit path is gone rather than carried into the new engine, because scaling the latent draws by one over the square root of the weight is not a coherent weighted likelihood. Weights on a logistic binary fit are supported under the common reading of a weight as a number of identical observations, restricted to positive integers. A 0.9-x weighted probit call now errors, and the only migration is an integer-count logistic fit. The maintainer's words on the removal: it "is not a coherent weighted-likelihood model".
Record: docs/design/core-generalization.md; docs/design/weighted-logistic.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B13]

**No approximate sampling in this release**
Every draw in 1.0 stays exact: an approximate Polya-Gamma draw, the data augmentation that would make a logistic likelihood conjugate under arbitrary real weights, is declined. The alternative was accepting that approximation to unlock the models behind it. A real-valued negative-binomial dispersion and arbitrary real weights on a binary fit therefore cannot be fit in 1.0-0, and both wait on that one unlock. The same ruling kept the hurdle model's sampler-only mode refused, and struck, as reopening a settled point, a proposal to remove the second, redundant sampler creation that ordinal and negative-binomial fits perform, about 3 milliseconds a fit.
Record: docs/plans/release-candidate-review.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B14]

**Heavy tails and overdispersion on capped grids**
A Student-t residual law estimates its degrees of freedom on a capped grid with a floor of 3, and negative-binomial dispersion is drawn on a capped grid of integers. The alternative for dispersion was a continuous parameter, which needs an approximate draw; the record lays out both forks and records the maintainer's pick: "RESOLVED (VD 2026-07-18): fork (A) - integer dispersion, fully exact". For the degrees of freedom the record credits a software and literature survey with settling the forks and quotes no maintainer pick of the grid or its floor. A user cannot have the degrees of freedom estimated below 3, which is where the residual variance stops existing, though a value below 3 supplied outright is accepted and fit; a non-integer or very large dispersion has no such escape.
Record: docs/design/negative-binomial.md, for the dispersion pick; docs/design/robust-errors.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B15]

**Monotone leaves target the exact posterior**
Leaves constrained to be monotone in a predictor sample the exact target, with a constrained joint marginal at the seam where a tree move changes which constraints apply. The alternatives traded that exactness for a simpler move at the seam. The cost is 600 to 1000 lines of engine code and an adaptive quadrature that dominates the run time of a monotone fit. The record has the maintainer resolving for the exact shape and for proceeding with the monotone work at that point.
Record: docs/design/monotone.md. Marked: not mine; superseded in part by dec-B144: the move's conditional cone masses matched the whole tree's normalizer only at root births, so under "leaf" the move now carries the whole tree's normalizer; under "joint" it carries none. [dec-B16]

**The variance forest rides the weight channel**
A second forest modelling the residual variance enters the sampler through the observation weight channel, as a separately typed forest that may be absent. The alternative was giving it a channel of its own. Because the weight channel is then taken, a variance forest is refused for every family and model composition that already owns weights. The record has the maintainer resolving for this route and for implementing it at that point.
Record: docs/design/heteroscedastic.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B17]

**The causal model's prognostic scale is half-Cauchy**
In the two-forest causal parameterization, which fits a prognostic forest and a treatment-effect forest, the prognostic forest's amplitude gets a half-Cauchy prior. The alternative was deferring the scale and fixing the amplitude at one. The record put the question "ship it with the b0/b1 expansion, or defer at a = 1?" and records the answer: "Questions 3 and 4 (VD, 2026-07-07): ship the half-Cauchy prognostic scalar a - parity with bcf is the goal". The R-side default is family-aware while the engine's own default is not, so the two disagree by design; no entry point, R or C, reaches the engine's default, so no user sees the plainer one today.
Record: docs/design/bcf.md, under its open questions and their resolutions. Marked: not mine. [dec-B18]

**Causal forests live in bartCause, not dbarts**
dbarts exports no causal-forest entry point: the causal fit class and the causal argument vocabulary move to bartCause, and what stays in dbarts is the engine vocabulary for models with more than one forest. The alternative, a public creation route inside dbarts, existed during the pre-release and was withdrawn before it shipped. A dbarts user cannot fit a causal forest by name. The maintainer: "I don't think bcf belongs in the dbarts function."
Record: docs/design/bcf.md; docs/plans/archive/bcf-public-surface.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B19]

**An ordered factor now fits an ordinal model**
An ordered-factor response is detected by its class and fit as an ordinal model, identified by fixing the scale, with the cutpoints updated one at a time by a Cowles-style Metropolis step. The alternatives were requiring the user to name the ordinal family and inferring it from the level names. The record lays out each fork and records the maintainer's picks: "RESOLVED (VD 2026-07-18): scheme A.", "RESOLVED (VD 2026-07-18): as recommended - Cowles-style marginal MH", and "RESOLVED (VD 2026-07-18): auto-dispatch with announcement", with "VD's condition - detection by a concrete class, not level-name inference". A user whose 0.9-x code passed an ordered factor got a continuous fit on the integer level codes and now gets a different model class, announced at the call. The BayesTree-style door refuses such a response by name rather than changing the model under the user.
Record: docs/design/ordinal.md, for the forks and picks; docs/plans/archive/ordinal-outcomes.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B20]

**Latent draws are not readable for multinomial fits**
The sampler's latent accessor refuses with a message when the fit is multinomial, instead of returning the augmentation draws. The alternative was returning them. A host embedding a multinomial sampler inside a larger model cannot read those latents. The maintainer's criterion was usefulness: "latents are not persisted absent a compelling use case."
Record: docs/design/multinomial-mutation-arc.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B21]

**Gaussian-process leaves ship with no users**
The Gaussian-process leaf model, in which a leaf holds a smooth function rather than a constant, stays in 1.0-0 although nothing outside the package uses it. The alternative was cutting it before the release. Keeping it freezes 773 lines of engine code, a factorization whose cost is cubic in leaf size, and a manual page into the release. The maintainer ruled to keep it while flagging that whether it belongs in 1.0 is a conversation still owed.
Record: docs/plans/pre-review-cleanup.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B22]

**Single-precision residual storage is optional**
The sampler can hold residuals in single precision, asked for by a storage argument set to "single", with double precision still the default. The alternative was not building it. A user who asks for it gets different numbers back, which the manual page has to say, and the knob is valid for one combination of leaf model and family and refused for the rest. The maintainer directed: "Build it, keep it optional, think about other ways to optionally decrease storage sizes at the same time." The cost is one more full instantiation of the engine stack.
Record: docs/design/reduced-precision-storage.md; docs/plans/archive/bart2-argument-consolidation.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B23]

**Observation indices narrow to 32 bits**
The engine stores the indices it gathers observations through as 32-bit integers by default, and the narrowing preserves every draw bit for bit. No alternative was weighed. The number of observations is capped just under four billion, and every support-library signature that takes an index changed with it. The record quotes the maintainer's directive for the storage work, "Build it, keep it optional, think about other ways to optionally decrease storage sizes at the same time.", which does not choose this narrowing. The maintainer marked the entry his in the marking pass of 2026-09-08.
Record: docs/design/reduced-precision-storage.md; the maintainer's marking pass, commit 5fb09fae. Marked: mine. [dec-B24]

**No eight-bit predictor codes**
Quantized predictor values stay in 16-bit codes: an eight-bit layer for the hot data and per-column code widths are not pursued. The alternative was a standalone phase of work to build them. Nothing a user sees changes, and the memory-bound regime at large n keeps the wider codes. The record leaves the go or no-go open and then records the maintainer's answer: "Go/no-go (VD, 2026-07-07): NO standalone phase 2."
Record: docs/plans/archive/hot-layer-u8.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B25]

**The support library's thread managers are cut**
The two thread managers in the support library and the threaded wrappers around the moment calculations are removed from the tree, after being archived on a branch. The alternative was keeping them against a future scheme that threads within a single chain. About 2470 lines of within-chain reduction machinery leave, so such a scheme would start over. Nothing a user sees changes. The record lists every fork a pre-review audit put to the maintainer with its ruling; this one reads "ARCHIVE ..., then CUT from bartcore".
Record: docs/plans/pre-review-cleanup.md, its rulings table. Marked: not mine. [dec-B26]

**The response is still scaled by its range**
A continuous response is still shifted and scaled to the interval from minus one half to one half before fitting, rather than standardized by its standard deviation. The alternative was standardization, which is less sensitive to a single extreme response value. Range scaling keeps that sensitivity and the internal-scale bookkeeping it forces, and was kept for compatibility with the package's lineage. The maintainer signed off to "keep, document", with a setter added on the sampler for the response.
Record: docs/plans/archive/range-scaling.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B27]

**Categorical rules report directions, not a mask**
The tree reader returns a missing value in the split-value column for every categorical rule and gives the per-level directions instead, dropping the raw bit-mask value that narrow categorical rules used to carry. The alternative was keeping two vocabularies, one for narrow masks and one for wide ones. A 0.9-x script that read the value column for a narrow categorical rule now gets NA there. The maintainer signed off to "unify".
Record: docs/plans/archive/flat-format-v2.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B29]

**family moves from control to model**
The response family is a slot on the model object rather than on the control object. No alternative was weighed. Any consumer that reads the family off the control object breaks, and the layout freezes at 1.0-0. The maintainer signed off to "move".
Record: docs/plans/archive/family-on-model.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B30]

**Saved sampler state carries a format version**
A saved sampler state records the format it was written in and is refused when that does not match the running package, with no shim to migrate an old one. The alternative was such a shim. A state written by any pre-release build cannot be loaded. The maintainer scoped the policy down on the ground that nothing from this line of work has been released, so there is no cross-version guarantee apparatus and nothing for binary formats. A later ruling adds the one recognition that matters, a 0.9-x fit refused by name with a message. See also: [dec-B109].
Record: docs/plans/archive/state-format-policy.md. Marked: not mine; superseded by dec-B109. [dec-B31]

**Restoring a state is semantic, not bitwise**
Loading a saved sampler state restores the model's meaning rather than its bits: the residual standard deviation rides the original response scale and fitted values are rebuilt by resumming the trees. The alternative was a bitwise restore. A consumer cannot check reproducibility by requiring bitwise equality across a save and a load. The maintainer signed off to "drop to semantic restore".
Record: docs/plans/archive/state-continuation.md. Marked: not mine. [dec-B32]

**A fit does not store its sampler state**
Trees are not kept by default, a fit does not capture sampler state on its own, and predicting from a reloaded fit errors with a message naming what to call first. The alternatives were flipping the default to keep trees, rejected, and the eager state capture that was built and then reverted. A user who saves a fit and reloads it hits that error unless the state was stored before saving. The maintainer overruled the eager capture because the resulting bloat, roughly 2.8 times the size of the fitted values, is not acceptable as a default, and accepted the requirement to touch the state as a known cost. A later ruling restates this with the manual wording and a fallback. See also: [dec-B104].
Record: docs/plans/archive/package-review-remediation.md; docs/plans/prerc-surface-freeze.md. Marked: superseded by dec-B104. [dec-B33]

**The data object's x slot is the source**
The x slot on the data object accepts any type and means the predictors as they were handed in, not a maintained view of the engine's quantized state. The alternative was keeping it a matrix that mirrors what the engine holds. The S4 slot no longer states the contract, so a consumer that assumes a matrix is unguarded. The maintainer approved the reconciliation conditional on the model being a collection of mutations and explicitly "NOT a maintained public view of the engine's quantized state".
Record: docs/plans/archive/data-ownership-3-mutation.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B35]

**Predictor matrices are borrowed, never copied**
The engine borrows a predictor matrix read-only while the sampler is being constructed, and never afterwards; every later path that needs the raw values gets them handed back at call time. The alternative, copying the raw values into the sampler, was rejected. Mutation, cut-point selection and tree replay all then require the R layer to supply the values again, which is work the R layer has to do. The maintainer rejected the copy and set both the construction-only borrow and the call-time supply.
Record: docs/design/data-ownership.md. Marked: mine. [dec-B36]

**sparseFactor ships under a Matrix-style name**
The package exports a constructor for a sparse factor predictor, named to follow the Matrix package's own naming convention so that it reads familiarly to users of sparse data. No alternative spelling was weighed once the convention was chosen. The cost is one more generic-sounding name at the top level of the package. The maintainer marked the entry his in the marking pass of 2026-09-08.
Record: docs/design/data-ownership.md; the maintainer's marking pass, commit 5fb09fae. Marked: mine. [dec-B37]

**Probability vectors snap to sum to one**
Split and proposal probability vectors that sum to one within the square root of machine epsilon are snapped to exactly one. The alternative was the tighter fixed tolerance the released package uses. This tolerance is looser, so nothing that validated under 0.9-x is refused now, and some input that would have been refused is accepted. The maintainer asked for "the default tolerances from almost-equal type functions" and accepted that the change moves which user input is admitted.
