It seems inefficient from a mixing perspective to have so many possible proposals that don't change anything." BayesTree's default rule spaces its points evenly whatever the values, which is where the release's comes from; the BART package gives one point for each gap under either rule (run: 1 point and 9 for a 0/1 column and one of ten values); bartMachine draws a threshold from the distinct values of the rows in the node; bcf takes midpoints where a column has fewer than 8 distinct values (sources read). On mixing: a change of threshold among the 100 points of a 0/1 column is proposed, taken and moves no row, and after one split on the column both children still count it as splittable, so a second is proposed and refused for an empty side. The maintainer: "Use option B, One point per gap when a column has fewer distinct values than n.cuts; evenly spaced otherwise." The rule: under the default rule a column with fewer distinct values than n.cuts gets one cut point in each gap between neighbouring values, and any other column its evenly spaced points. The cost is that every default fit with a binary or few-valued numeric column draws differently from 0.9-34, and that a gap's chance of being split no longer follows its width; how far a fit moves was not measured. Revises dec-A178 in part. Not built; a slice of its own after the one that removes repeated points, since it moves recorded baselines that one leaves alone. See also: [dec-A178], [dec-B297], [dec-B299].
Record: root TODO, default-rule-per-gap. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-07. [dec-B313]

**The residual prior gets a setter and a scale of its own, and the starting estimate of sigma is never re-estimated**
A gaussian fit takes a rough residual sd from a linear fit at creation, sigest, which places the prior on sigma through its quantile and starts the chain. No call re-estimates it, setData included, as in 0.9-34, whose setData "Preserves the n.cuts and sigma slots"; dec-B296 names the response's scale and the count centre and not this, and the plan held it for the sampler's life as its own call (dec-A176). A response in other units then keeps a sigma prior where the first put it, and a sampler created on every row and masked afterwards kept 50.07 where the rows left in give 0.949 (the plan's run). The orchestrator offered holding it, re-estimating it under updateScale = TRUE, and an argument of its own, recommending the last. The maintainer: "But you wouldn't re-run a linear regression, would you?" No: the regression stands in at creation for not knowing sigma, is slow inside a loop, and a caller who changed the units or the rows knows more than it does; stan4bart asks for a re-derived scale on a schedule through warm-up and would pay for one each time. Offered a method that restates the estimate with a number: "But it's really just used to scale the prior, right? So why not speak to that more directly?" Offered the prior taking its scale directly, restated through setModel: "Well no, the argument is for there to be prior settors which allow for manipulating those parameters, which aren't there already?" They are not: setLeafPrior is the one setter for a single prior, setModel replaces the whole model and reaches the residual prior's df and quantile only (run on 2026-10-07: it takes a changed one), and the prior has no scale to set, its scale being the quantile applied to an estimate every call preserves. Shown a setter in setLeafPrior's idiom, sampler$setSigmaPrior(chisq(df = 3, scale = 1.2)), with chisq() taking a scale as well as a quantile, and asked whether that was the shape meant and whether the name served, the maintainer on 2026-10-07: "Yes, and if you want you can have an argument that indicates whether or not the updated scale is on absolute or relative to the response scale." The rule: the sampler gets a setter for the residual prior; chisq() takes scale beside quant, the two not given together; sigest stays an aid at creation for the quantile form and nothing regresses afterwards; updateScale = TRUE moves the range alone. An argument saying whether a stated scale is absolute or relative to the response's scale is allowed and left to the design, where a relative one would follow a re-derived range as a leaf prior stated by k does. Elsewhere: the BART package takes the prior's scale directly as lambda, sigest then unused, and stochtree a shape and scale; bartMachine and BayesTree take a quantile only (sources read, the first run). The cost is one method and one argument, and that a caller changing units restates the prior beside the call that moves the range unless the scale is relative. Not built; design first. Settles the second of dec-A176's open points. See also: [dec-A176], [dec-B296], [dec-B231], [dec-B233].
Record: root TODO, sigma-prior-setter. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-07. [dec-B314]

**A hazard fit's periods come from the subjects in the likelihood at creation**
A hazard fit splits time into periods at the distinct observed times and gives each subject a row for each period it was at risk in. The times were every subject's, a subject at weight 0 among them, which dec-B296 does not name and the plan for it left to the maintainer (dec-A176). Run on 2026-10-07, 40 subjects with two at weight 0 holding times nobody else has, 3.5 and 20: the fit has 8 periods and 181 rows where the same fit with the two dropped by subset has 6 and 146, 23 of the added rows falling to subjects in the fit, and a period in which none of them can have an event. Only the probit link reaches the case, the logistic one refusing a zero weight. The orchestrator recommended the subjects in the likelihood, and then, asked "What do other packages do, and what do we do elsewhere?", moved to every subject: survfit keeps such a subject's time on its axis, glm keeps a factor level seen only in a row at weight 0 as a column with no coefficient, the package's cut points come from every row, and the period is a predictor column; coxph and survreg refuse a zero weight, and the BART package's survival fit takes no weights (run, the last read from its arguments). The maintainer: "What about other models within dbarts? Didn't we have a ruling about counts for log(counts) and setting offsets in one class?" That is dec-B296, which puts what a fit takes from the response in one class, the count centre, the response's range, the accelerated failure time fit's range and the unit of a forest's sd, over the rows in the likelihood at creation; the periods are built from the response, the accelerated failure time fit on the same response is already in the class, and the ruling's test, that a mask which never changes is the same as deleting the rows, is failed by every subject's times and passed by glm's column, which is inert. The orchestrator returned to its first recommendation, and the maintainer on 2026-10-07: "Go ahead and use your revised recommendation." The rule: a hazard fit's default periods are the distinct observed times of the subjects in the likelihood when the fit is created; stated breaks are as they were. Two things are the orchestrator's and not ruled: a subject at weight 0 is expanded on that grid with its time in the interval it falls in, as stated breaks place any time, and one whose time is past the last period is at risk throughout with no event. The alternative was every subject's times, under which weight 0 keeps a subject's own time on the grid and a caller wanting it gone uses subset. The cost is that a subject at weight 0 has no period of its own, and that a caller wanting one grid across fits under different weights must state it. The family is new in 1.0-0, so no released behaviour changes. Not built. Settles the first of dec-A176's open points. See also: [dec-B296], [dec-A176], [dec-B302].
Record: root TODO, hazard-periods-rows-in. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-07. [dec-B315]

**A forest's label is matched exactly**
dec-B276 has a string given as a forest be its label, and the build matched it exactly, with no abbreviation and no folding of case, as the implementer's and the orchestrator's call (dec-A181). Run on 2026-10-07 with forests labelled "dose", "age" and "Treat": "dose" selects the first, and "do" and "treat" are refused with the three labels listed. In base R a name is matched exactly by the double-bracket subscript and by a matrix's dimnames, a list's "do" giving NULL and a matrix's an error, and partially only by the dollar operator (run). Shown exact matching, recommended, against taking a unique abbreviation as match.arg does, which suits a fixed vocabulary such as monotone()'s direction words (dec-B288) and under which a label added later can make working code ambiguous, the maintainer on 2026-10-07: "Exact." The rule: a label selects its forest only when it is given whole and in its own case. The cost is that a long label has no shorthand but the forest's position. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A181; its others, the classes refused and the names a list given forest by forest may carry, are not ruled. See also: [dec-A181], [dec-B276], [dec-B288].
Record: docs/plans/forest-defaults-by-kind.md, Landing note. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-07. [dec-B316]

**An explicit updateCutPoints = FALSE warns as TRUE does**
dec-B312 takes a logical updateCutPoints until 1.1-0 with a warning once per session, TRUE as "position" and FALSE as "none", and the build warned on FALSE too, as the orchestrator's reading. In 0.9-34 FALSE was the default of setPredictor(x, column, forceUpdate, updateCutPoints = FALSE) (read from the release), so code written for it may spell it out meaning nothing by it. Shown warning on FALSE, recommended, against taking it silently until 1.1-0 and then refusing it, and against keeping it for good as a second spelling of "none", the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Warn on FALSE too, just the first time it is called. Until removal in 1.1-0." The rule: a logical updateCutPoints of either value warns the first time in a session and is read as its word; in 1.1-0 a logical is refused. The cost is one warning a session for code that restated the old default. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A183. See also: [dec-B312], [dec-A183].
Record: docs/plans/repeated-cut-restore.md, Calls made in building. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B317]

**A state carrying more kept draws than the sampler keeps is clean**
A sampler created with keepTrees = TRUE holds its kept tree draws, and its state carries them. dec-B305 named as not clean "a store of kept draws larger than the sampler's", in the rule its entry wrote and not in the maintainer's words, which define clean as "leaving a sampler in a coherent state with regards to its model". Run on 2026-10-08: a state of 10 kept draws restored into a sampler keeping 4, 4 into one keeping 10, and 4 into one keeping none are each refused on the branch with "state is not consistent with this sampler", and a state with none into a sampler keeping 4 installs, TRUE; 0.9-34 takes the first two and returns NULL, and crashes R on the third case it was given, a state with none into a sampler keeping draws. Shown the store as clean, installing the chain and the newest draws that fit and returning TRUE, recommended, against needing force and against the refusal as built, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Option A, clean." The rule: the kept draws are output, not model or chain; setState installs a state's chain whatever its store, keeps the newest of its draws that fit the sampler's store, leaves the rest of the store empty where it holds fewer (dec-B294: "Install what fits."), and returns TRUE for that. The cost is that draws a smaller store cannot hold are dropped with no signal. Not built; the install surface's first slice. Revises dec-B305 in part. See also: [dec-B305], [dec-B294], [dec-B283].
Record: root TODO, state-store-size-install and setstate-force-update. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B318]

**setCutPoints stays forced**
setCutPoints replaces a column's grid and merges a split whose interval empties, returning NULL invisibly; 0.9-34 did the same, with no forceUpdate argument and help reading "Forces the change by pruning any leaves that end up empty". setPredictor and setState have an unforced form returning TRUE or FALSE (dec-B310). Keeping setCutPoints forced was the orchestrator's reading in dec-B312. Asked whether a call that sets a grid belongs inside a loop and what other packages do: a predictor that is itself drawn, a latent trait or an imputed covariate, needs a grid that follows it, which setPredictor's refresh gives, deriving the grid from the new values up to n.cuts each time, with an unforced form (dec-B298); BayesTree and the BART package fix the grid at creation and bartMachine draws a threshold from the values in the node (sources read for dec-B313). Run on 2026-10-08, a column going from 5 distinct values to 3 and then 8: under the quantile rule a refresh by position or by value gives 4, 2 and 7 points and no refresh 4 throughout, and under the default rule 100 throughout, so a grid is never left trimmed, where 0.9-34's refresh kept a grid's length. Shown always forced, recommended, against an unforced default and an unforced form on request, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "OK, keep it forced and make sure its well documented." The rule: setCutPoints has no unforced form and returns NULL invisibly; its help says so, that it sets a grid, and that a grid following changing values is setPredictor's refresh. The cost is that a caller cannot ask for a new grid only where nothing merges. Built; its help amended with this entry. Confirms that call of dec-A183. See also: [dec-B312], [dec-B310], [dec-B298], [dec-A183].
Record: man/dbartsSampler-class.Rd, Value. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B319]

**A split keeps its direction for missing values when no row is missing**
Rows with missing predictor values are kept in 1.0-0, where 0.9-34 dropped them, and each split on a column that had missing values records which way they go. A state stored while a column had missing values may be restored into the sampler after they were filled, or the column filled after it, so that no row is missing when the trees come back. Run on 2026-10-08, 60 missing values filled by a forced setPredictor and the stored state restored: on a numeric column and a factor of 5 levels the directions are dropped and setState reports the install as not exact, and on a factor of 90 levels they are kept and it reports it exact; on the numeric column 4 of 116 nodes are merged, splits that had only missing rows on one side. The install surface's design had the dropped direction need force; its critique offered keeping the direction on every kind of column. Asked what case is meant, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "there are some states that derive from data that correspond to a vision of the world and are essential properties of samplers. If you're going to impute, you should do so before the sampler is created so that no future predictors can be missing. That said, if there's a missingness mechanism and at some point no data are simulated as missing on an update, that can be a valid sampler." Shown keeping the direction as clean, recommended, against dropping it under force and dropping it as clean, the maintainer: "Yes, keep the direction, clean." The rule: whether a column can be missing is a property of the sampler, set when a missing value is first seen in it (dec-B321); a split on such a column keeps its direction for missing values whether or not a row is missing at the time, and setState installing it into a sampler whose column holds no missing value now is clean and returns TRUE. A split left with an empty side is merged and is not clean, as dec-B305 has it. The alternatives were dropping the direction, needing force, under which restoring the state before the predictor fails unforced, and dropping it as clean, under which missing values put back go down a split by no recorded direction. The cost is engine work so that every kind of column holds the direction; a forced setPredictor that fills every missing value should keep the directions too, which is to be checked in the design. Not built; the install surface's first slice. See also: [dec-B305], [dec-B318], [dec-A165].
Record: root TODO, setstate-force-update. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08; revised in part by dec-B321. [dec-B320]

**A column can be missing from the first missing value it is given, and ever after**
dec-B320 keeps a split's direction for missing values once it exists. The converse: a sampler created with no missing value in a column and given one later by setPredictor. Run on 2026-10-08: the branch takes it, unforced returning TRUE and forced NULL, and the sampler runs, while its predict refuses a missing value in such a column ("a split rule learns a route for NA only on a column that had missing values when the trees were grown"); 0.9-34 took it and sent missing values down an arbitrary branch. Read from the engine: a split's direction is drawn with probability one half when the split is proposed, on a column that has missing values at the time; it is cleared when the column has none; and when missing values first enter a column every split already on it sends them left, a fixed default. Shown refusing such a value, recommended, against taking it, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "If there are no missings in training data, no proposal mechanism can generate trees with them but that's just inefficiency, not degeneracy." and "what's a principled stance on NAs? Only at creation, first time seen and then ever after, switch on and off with content of a column?" A direction no missing row has reached has its prior, one half, as its conditional, so in law every split has one; taking it at creation only refuses a valid operation, switching with the column's content loses what an exact restore needs (dec-B320), and the first time seen and ever after is that law stored only where it can matter, provided the first missing value draws each existing split's direction from its prior. Directions drawn for every split from creation are the same law, at the cost of random draws in every fit that never sees a missing value, which would move every recorded baseline. The maintainer: "OK, first time seen, then ever after. That seems to me to correspond to the *possibility* of missingness. We assume missingness isn't possible until we have evidence that it is, and then ever-after we operate under that evidence." The rule: a column can be missing once a missing value has been given to it, at creation or by a later setPredictor, and stays so for the sampler's life; when the first one arrives each split already on the column draws its direction with probability one half; no direction is dropped afterwards, whatever the column holds. The alternatives were refusing a missing value in a column created complete and the build's switch with the column's content and its default to the left. The cost is engine work: a column's flag that missing values are possible no longer follows its content, and the draws at first sight consume random numbers in a call that today consumes none. setData, which replaces the whole data set, is not covered. Whether predict should answer for a missing value in a column the fit never saw missing, by the directions' prior, is a separate question. Not built. Revises dec-B320 in part. See also: [dec-B320], [dec-A112], [dec-B305].
Record: root TODO, missingness-first-seen. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B321]

**predict refuses a missing value where the fit never had one, under dec-B321**
dec-A112 refuses a missing value in a column that had none in training, at predict and in test data given at fit time, na.pass returning NA for those rows; the maintainer marked it on 2026-09-29. Under dec-B321 a split's direction for missing values exists in law at its prior of one half, so predict could answer for such a row, each kept draw sending it one way with probability one half at each split on the column, the posterior mean averaging last; the answer ignores where the value probably lies. Shown keeping the refusal, recommended, against answering from the prior directions and against doing so on request, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "I think we should continue to refuse if the learned sampler was in a world where missingness wasn't a possibility. There's no evidence where missing values should be sent." The rule: a missing value in a column that could not be missing in the sampler that grew the trees, in the sense of dec-B321, is refused at predict and in test data as dec-A112 has it, na.pass returning NA for those rows; a column that became missable by a later setPredictor is not refused. The alternatives were the prior directions by default and on request. The cost is that a user with such rows drops or imputes them. As built for a column never missing; the column made missable later follows dec-B321's slice. Confirms dec-A112. See also: [dec-A112], [dec-B321].
Record: root TODO, missingness-first-seen. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-09-29. [dec-B322]

**A smaller recipient of a warm start takes the donor trees that carry its fit**
dec-B247 left a warm start from a donor of another tree count to be built ("maybe we can add only partially populating trees from another model in the warm-starts to handle the big -> small, small -> big scenarios"); run on 2026-10-08, the branch refuses both directions as not shape-compatible, and 0.9-34's setControl, resizing a sampler, kept the first trees. A larger recipient takes every donor tree with the rest single leaves at 0, so its starting fit is the donor's; that was stated, not asked. For a smaller one the install surface's critic measured, 24 paired runs a cell to sweep 300, the root mean squared error to the truth of the m trees whose fitted values vary most over the recipient's rows against the first m: on a strong signal, 50 to 20, 0.76 better at sweep 1, 0.13 at 50 and even from 200; on a weak signal, 200 to 50, 0.15 worse at sweep 1 and even from 50; on a slowly mixing additive signal, 200 to 50, 0.67 better at sweep 1 and 0.07 better over sweeps 201 to 300. Shown the most varying trees, recommended, against the first m, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Sure, option B." The rule: a warm start into fewer trees takes the donor's trees whose fitted values vary most over the recipient's rows, in the donor's order and with their values as they are; into more trees it takes them all and starts the rest as single leaves at 0. The alternative was the first m, an arbitrary subset since a tree's place in a forest means nothing. The cost is a rule that takes a sentence to state and a slower start on a weak signal. Not built. See also: [dec-B247], [dec-B295].
Record: root TODO, warm-start-tree-count. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B323]

**A warm start takes the trees, and a part only one side has does not transfer**
Run on 2026-10-08: a warm start from a plain fit into a DART fit, and from a DART fit into a plain one, is refused ("DART state transfers only between two DART fits"), DART into DART is taken, and a donor or recipient alone in carrying a variance forest is refused as not shape-compatible. dec-B254 names a new sampler, "warm-started where that helps", as the route between models of different structure, DART against the ordinary tree prior among them. Shown taking the trees with such parts left behind, recommended, against keeping the refusals, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Take the trees; a part only one side has does not transfer." The rule: the trees transfer; DART's weights transfer only between two DART fits, a DART recipient of a plain donor starting its weights as a new fit does and drawing them from the trees at its first sweep; a variance forest transfers only where both have one, a recipient's own starting as a new fit's and a donor's left behind. The alternative was the refusals. The cost is that the part only the recipient has starts cold. Not built. See also: [dec-B254], [dec-B295].
Record: root TODO, warm-start-salvage. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B324]

**A warm start between kinds of leaf takes what translates**
Run on 2026-10-08: a constant-leaf donor into a linear-leaf or gp-leaf fit is refused with "malformed leaf parameters in bartcore state", and a linear-leaf donor into a constant-leaf fit is taken with its slopes dropped and no word. dec-B295 lists a donor of another kind of leaf as one that may stay refused, and asks that a warm start "try to get as much information out of the fit it can". Shown refusing every such start with a message naming the kinds, against taking what translates, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Take what translates." The rule: a constant donor seeds a linear recipient exactly, each leaf's value its intercept with slopes at 0, so the starting fit is the donor's; a linear donor seeds a constant recipient by each leaf's value at its covariates' centre, which bart takes as its best effort and an unforced installTrees reports by FALSE, the sampler as it was; a gp leaf on either side is refused, the message naming the two kinds. The alternative was refusing all of them. The cost is the conversion work and its tests. Not built. See also: [dec-B295], [dec-B200].
Record: root TODO, warm-start-salvage. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B325]

**bart says under verbose when a warm start was taken in part**
Under dec-B323 to dec-B325 a warm start may take some of a donor's trees, leave its DART weights or variance forest behind, or reduce linear leaves to their value at the centre; bart has no return value to report it through and its verbose output says nothing of a warm start (run on 2026-10-08). Shown saying nothing, one line under verbose = TRUE, recommended, and a warning, against dec-B234's "Return a status, no warning", the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "One line under verbose = TRUE, alongside the setup bart already prints." The rule: with verbose = TRUE, bart prints one line among its setup lines saying what a warm start took, such as "warm start: 20 of 50 donor trees taken; DART weights started fresh", and nothing otherwise. The alternatives were silence and a warning. The cost is one line of verbose output. Not built. See also: [dec-B323], [dec-B324], [dec-B325], [dec-B234].
Record: root TODO, warm-start-salvage. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B326]

**A re-derivation asked of a weight setter acts as the response setters' does**
Under dec-B296 the response's scale comes from the rows in the likelihood at creation and is then held; the third slice of its plan gives setWeights and setActiveRows an updateScale argument, FALSE by default and after updateState so that 0.9-34's positional setWeights(w, TRUE) keeps its meaning, asking for the scale to be derived again from the rows now in. A tree's leaf values are stored on the internal scale, so a re-derivation changes what they mean on the response's. Run on 2026-10-08, setResponse with the response tripled and updateScale = TRUE: the live fit follows the new units, three times as large, and predict on the kept draws is unchanged, leaf-conversions having converted them. The design's critic, on a prototype, masked rows near 115 among responses from -7 to 12 and re-derived with the live leaves kept: the live fit moved by up to 15.07 with no data change, its error to the truth 1.16 before and 8.54 after, recovered at the next sweep. The orchestrator recommended converting the live leaves so that the fit stays and only the prior is restated. Asked "If data go in and go out, why not add an updateScale to weight setters as well?", and told that the slice does, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "If they set it to true they're asking for the internal scale to be updated, so we should treat it the same as `setResponse`." The rule: setWeights(updateScale = TRUE) and setActiveRows(updateScale = TRUE) derive the response's scale from the rows in and treat the live chain and the kept draws as setResponse(updateScale = TRUE) does, the live leaves keeping their internal numbers and the kept draws converted; FALSE, the default, holds the scale. The alternative was converting the live leaves. The cost is that a caller who asks for the re-derivation sees the live fit move to the new scale until the next sweep, as at the response setters. Not built; the plan's third slice. Settles that open point of dec-A176. See also: [dec-B296], [dec-B303], [dec-A176].
Record: docs/plans/response-scale-rows.md; root TODO, response-scale-rows. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B327]

**A linear leaf's slope that no data informed is converted by the formula**
A linear leaf fits an intercept and a slope in its covariate; where the covariate has held one value on every row, the data fix only the leaf's value there and the slope is a draw from its prior. dec-A177 left converting such a slope by the formula when setData or a warm start brings a covariate that varies, for a column setPredictor had moved to another single value, and filed reading that side as without spread. Run on 2026-10-08: a sampler created with the column at 0, set to 3 and run 50 sweeps, fit from -3.80 to 5.65; setData with the column spread at sd 10 carries the live fit to -25.5 to 34.9, and one sweep later it is at -9.22 to 8.21. The orchestrator first recommended setting such slopes to zero with the intercept carrying the fit. Asked "Do both options correspond to legimitate statistical models?": only the formula does, holding the parameters, a valid draw, across a change of data while restating their representation, which is the move a larger sampler that changes data needs; zero replaces a draw with a fixed value, as sending every new missing value left did (dec-B321), and is a start for a new chain only. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Use option A. As built: convert by the formula." The rule: a slope on a column that held one value is converted by the formula like any other, and the fit may move until the sampler runs, which the help says. The alternative was the slope set to zero. Confirms that call of dec-A177; the rounding-sized scale of a column constant to the eye only stays filed. See also: [dec-A177], [dec-B321], [dec-B200].
Record: root TODO, leaf-covariate-single-value-off-centre. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B328]

**A linear leaf's slope is converted by the formula onto or off a single-valued column too**
dec-A177's first call, made at the leaf conversions' landing, sets a live slope to zero where a linear leaf's covariate holds one value at its centre on one side of setData or a warm start: off such a column each leaf keeps its value, onto one it takes its function's value at the new constant. Run on 2026-10-08: a sampler created with the column at 0, run 150 sweeps, then given the column spread at sd 10: the fit moves by at most 1.35e-14. By dec-B328's reasoning that is a fixed value in place of a draw, the case differing from dec-B328's only in that setPredictor never moved the column, a single-valued column taking a scale of 1 by convention in both. Shown converting by the formula in both directions, recommended, against keeping the zeroing, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Convert by the formula here too, in both directions." The rule: at setData and a warm start a live slope is converted by the formula whether the covariate holds one value before the call, after it, or neither; where it holds one value at its centre on both sides and nothing is to convert, the leaf stays as it is. The alternative was the zeroing as built. The cost is that a fit on a placeholder column can move when the column first spreads, until the sampler runs, as under dec-B328. Not built. Revises dec-A177 in part. See also: [dec-B328], [dec-A177], [dec-B321].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-engine.md, leaf-slope-formula-single-value. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B329]

**A gp sampler holding saved draws refuses a re-derived scale, naming the practice it serves**
A saved draw of a gp leaf is a set of kernel weights, a zero-mean function with no constant term, so it can take the stretch of a change of the response's units and not its shift; constant and linear leaves take both, their parameter holding a constant. The leaf conversions refuse updateScale = TRUE on a gp sampler that holds saved draws, the orchestrator's call of 2026-10-06 (dec-A180), at the cost that a loop re-anchoring and then saving a draw stops at its second pass. Asked first "Is it possible to flip keepTrees on after burn-in, or are they not saved during burn-in?": burn-in sweeps never save, sample sweeps save only under keepTrees, and keepTrees can be turned on after burn-in by setControl (run on 2026-10-08: ten draws saved and predicted after it) or dbarts_sampler_setTreeStorage, which stan4bart uses, storage off and the scale re-derived every few iterations in warm-up, storage on and the scale held for sampling. The maintainer: "the goal of updating the scale is to allow it to vary while things are settling and then fix it to produce valid MCMC draws. Whatever we do for gp-leaves should support that practice. I'm not sure if there are valid reasons to allow updating the scale after burn-in." The orchestrator's view: none within one fit, the scale setting the prior, so that draws on either side of a change come from different posteriors, and a scale re-derived from quantities being drawn making the prior depend on them; new data or a changed model are new targets with their own burn-in. Asked whether gp leaves are under-specified: no, the shift has nowhere to go because the model's leaves are zero-mean; a shift stored with each saved draw would take it for every kind of leaf, and under the practice no saved draw needs converting. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "OK, let's use that then. Option A: refuse, with the message naming the practice." The rule: a gp sampler holding saved draws refuses updateScale = TRUE outright, whatever the new values, and the message says to re-derive the scale during burn-in, before draws are saved, or to make a new sampler. The alternatives were refusing only where the centre moves and a shift stored with each saved draw. The cost is no re-anchor once a gp draw is saved. The refusal as built; its message to be reworded. Confirms that call of dec-A180. See also: [dec-A180], [dec-B237], [dec-B327].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, gp-reanchor-message. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B330]

**Constant and linear leaves keep converting saved draws at a re-derived scale**
dec-B330 has a gp sampler holding saved draws refuse updateScale = TRUE, the scale being re-derived during burn-in before draws are saved. Constant and linear leaves take it, their saved draws converted so that predict on them does not move. Run on 2026-10-08: 0.9-34 took it and moved the saved draws' predictions without a word (by -5 after an offset of 5); turning keepTrees off and on by setControl clears the saved draws; no consumer re-derives from R, and stan4bart re-derives in warm-up with storage off. The orchestrator recommended refusing for every kind of leaf while draws are saved, since draws on either side of the change come from different posteriors. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Eh, you can keep converting. Let's not be too paternalistic." The rule: constant and linear leaves convert saved draws at a re-derived scale, as built; only gp leaves refuse. The alternative was the refusal for every kind. The cost is that re-deriving after draws are saved passes without a word for most fits. As built. See also: [dec-B330], [dec-A180].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B331]

**A thread count stated as NA is refused**
dec-B306 ruled "Fall back to one thread, silently." where n.threads is not stated and the cores cannot be counted; its rule text kept a stated NA refused, the orchestrator's reading. Run on 2026-10-08: bart(x, y, n.threads = NA) is refused with "'n.threads' must be a positive integer, not NA; leave it out to take the default"; parallel's mclapply(mc.cores = NA) and makeCluster(NA) and data.table's setDTthreads(NA) refuse it too. A wrapper that forwards guessNumCores(), which returns NA where the cores cannot be counted, is refused there; bartCause's bcf does so (root TODO, bartcause-thread-default). Shown the refusal, recommended, against reading a stated NA as unknown and taking one thread, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Refuse a stated NA, as built." The rule: a stated n.threads of NA is refused at every door, its message saying to leave the argument out. The alternative was one thread for a stated NA. The cost is that a wrapper forwarding guessNumCores() handles NA itself. As built. Confirms that reading of dec-B306 and dec-A182. See also: [dec-B306], [dec-A182], [dec-A163].
Record: root TODO, bartcause-thread-default. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B332]

**A binary fit whose response holds one class is fitted, with a warning**
A probit or logistic fit models the chance that a 0/1 outcome is 1; a subgroup, a fold of a loop or a rare event can leave the response with one class, where the model is still defined, its prior proper, and pushes the probability toward 0 or 1 as far as the prior lets it. Run on 2026-10-08: the branch refuses it ("family \"probit\" requires a response with both classes; the response has a single class"), logistic alike, the orchestrator's call in dec-A144; 0.9-34 errored on every form with unrelated messages; glm(family = binomial) fits an all-0 response in silence. dec-B209: "we just shouldn't prevent legimitate models because they sometimes break. What's actually good for users in this case?" Shown the refusal, a fit with one warning, recommended, and a silent fit, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Fit, with one warning that the response holds one class." The rule: a probit or logistic fit whose response holds one class is fitted, with one warning saying so. The alternatives were the refusal as built and glm's silence. The cost is a fit the prior drives, and tests of the latent sampler at one class. Not built. Revises dec-A144 in part. See also: [dec-A144], [dec-B209].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, binary-one-class-fit. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B333]

**A control copied from a fit sets no tree count, and says so where its copy differs**
dec-B239 moves model settings off the control ("move them to the model and data objects"), with a copy kept on it for old code until 1.1-0; the migration is not built. Run on 2026-10-08: dbarts(y ~ x1 + x2, d, control = fit$fit$control) takes the old fit's 50 trees though the caller named no count, and where every forest has a basis the same call is refused as naming one; 0.9-34 errored on such a call ("cannot replicate NULL to a non-zero length"). Shown a copied control never counting, as the migration's plan has it, recommended, against its count always applying, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "As planned for the migration. We might warn if there's a difference in tree sizes and indicate that the new value isn't be used." The rule: a control taken from a fit sets no tree count for the next; a count the caller writes on a control counts for one release with the rename warning; where a copied control's count differs from the count the fit takes, a warning says so and that the control's is not used. The alternative was the carried count as built. The cost is that a branch user who relied on the carried count gets the default, told by the warning. Not built; with the control migration. See also: [dec-B239], [dec-B240], [dec-A158], [dec-A179].
Record: root TODO, control-carried-count-edges. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B334]

**A sampler assembled from another fit's control takes the model's count, with the same warning**
new("dbartsSampler", control, model, data) assembles a sampler from parts. Run on 2026-10-08: a control from a 200-tree fit beside the model of a 50-tree fit gives 200 trees, the control's copy winning, as in 0.9-34; the migration's plan refused a differing copy by name. Shown the refusal, the model's count with dec-B334's warning, recommended, and the control's copy, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "B. The model's count wins, with the same warning as for a copied control." The rule: a sampler assembled by hand takes the model's tree count, and a control whose copy differs draws dec-B334's warning. The alternatives were the refusal and the control's count. The cost is that a hand-built call meaning the control's count gets the model's, with a warning. Not built; with the control migration. See also: [dec-B334], [dec-B239], [dec-B247], [dec-A158].
Record: root TODO, control-carried-count-edges. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B335]

**NA for an argument no release had is refused, the message saying to use NULL**
dec-B140 ("Go ahead, those look good.") makes NULL the public spelling of not given: an NA where 0.9-34 had the argument warns for one release and is read as NULL. Run on 2026-10-08: an NA for an argument new in 1.0-0, dbarts(x, y, sigest = NA), seed = NA on dbarts and dbartsControl, xbart(sigest = NA), is refused ("NA is a missing value; NULL is the spelling of not given"), where 0.9-34 gave an unused-argument error for each; sigma = NA and rngSeed = NA, 0.9-34's spellings, warn and are taken. Shown the refusal, recommended, against a one-release warning for spellings no release had, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "I don't care for \"NULL is the spelling of not given\" - that's awkward. Just say \"use NULL instead\". Otherwise, use option A." The rule: such an NA is refused, the message ending "NA is a missing value; use NULL instead", followed where the argument's NULL means something more particular by what it gives (dart's rho: "which gives the default, the number of predictors"). The alternative was the warning. No call that ran in 0.9-34 breaks. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A118. See also: [dec-B140], [dec-A118].
Record: R/tombstones.R, refuseNAForNull. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B336]

**rbart_vi's group.by is found in the data first, then where the call was written**
rbart_vi fits BART with a random intercept per group named by group.by. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: a group.by vector that is not a column of data is found where the call was written, directly and through a wrapper's dots (4 groups); 0.9-34 grouped by the data's first column, there the response, without a word (80 groups). Shown that rule, lm's for weights, recommended, against a wrapper's own variable before a data column of the same name, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Data first, then where the call was written, as built and as lm." The rule: group.by and group.by.test are looked up in the data first and then where the call was written. The alternative departed from lm. NEWS says 0.9-34 grouped by the first column. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A122. See also: [dec-A122].
Record: inst/NEWS.Rd. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B337]

**split.probs holds to sample()'s rules**
split.probs weights how often each predictor is picked for a split. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: on the branch c(0, -1) is refused ("must be non-negative and finite") and c(0, 0) is refused ("must give at least one column a positive probability"); 0.9-34 ran c(0, -1), normalized to (0, 1) by a negative sum, and failed on c(0, 0) with "missing value where TRUE/FALSE needed"; sample(prob = ) refuses negative, non-finite and all-zero weights. Shown that refusal with a line in NEWS, recommended, against refusing only the all-zero vector, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Refuse negative, non-finite and all-zero, as built and as sample()." The rule: split.probs with a negative or non-finite entry, or with no positive entry, is refused. The alternative left a negative weight a probability. The cost is that the negative-weight call 0.9-34 ran errors, which NEWS now says. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A168. See also: [dec-A168].
Record: inst/NEWS.Rd. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B338]

**An offset that is not numeric is refused**
An offset is a known shift on the model's linear scale, and predict takes one for the new rows. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: predict(fit, nd, offset = rep(TRUE, 3)) and a factor offset are refused ("'offset' must be numeric"), as fitting refuses them, an all-NA logical and a numeric data frame being taken; 0.9-34 coerced both and returned predictions; predict.lm coerces too. Shown the refusal, recommended, against coercing as predict.lm does, a factor then becoming its level codes, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Refuse, as built." The rule: an offset that is not numeric is refused when fitting and at predict. The cost is that a 0.9-34 call passing a logical offset errors, which NEWS now says. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A144. See also: [dec-A144].
Record: inst/NEWS.Rd. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B339]

**survivalProbabilities applies an offset given without newdata to the training rows**
survivalProbabilities(fit, times, newdata, offset) returns survival curves at the training rows when newdata is left out, read from the draws the fit stored, and at newdata by replaying the saved trees, which needs keepTrees = TRUE (run on 2026-10-08: without saved trees predict stops with "refit with keepTrees = TRUE"). The branch refuses an offset given without newdata ("'offset' is for the rows of 'newdata'; the training rows use the fit's own offset"), the orchestrator's call in dec-A143; survival is new in 1.0-0. The orchestrator recommended the refusal; asked "Is it more efficient to not have to pass in the training data again?": yes, a new offset at the training rows is each stored draw less the fit's offset plus the new one, exact, with no trees replayed and none needed. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Use C: apply it to the training rows." The rule: an offset given without newdata replaces the fit's offset at the training rows, one value or one per training row, read off the stored draws. The alternatives were the refusal and ignoring it. The cost is one more meaning for offset, documented, and a length check against the training rows. Not built. Revises dec-A143 in part. See also: [dec-A143].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, survival-offset-training-rows. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B340]

**predict with no newdata predicts the training rows from the stored draws**
predict.lm and predict.glm with no newdata return the training rows' fitted values, glm's taking an offset there. Run on 2026-10-08: predict(fit) on a bart fit with no newdata stops with "newdata cannot be NULL", with saved trees or without, and without them first with "refit with keepTrees = TRUE"; 0.9-34 stopped with "argument \"newdata\" is missing"; the training rows' draws are stored, read by extract(fit, sample = "train") and fitted(). After dec-B340, survivalProbabilities reads them off the stored draws, an offset replacing the fit's. Shown keeping newdata required against predicting the training rows from the stored draws, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "With no newdata, predict the training rows from the stored draws." The rule: predict on a fit with no newdata returns every type at the training rows from the stored draws, needing no saved trees, and an offset given there replaces the fit's as in dec-B340. The alternative was the refusal as built and in 0.9-34. The cost is a second road to what extract gives, and each type worked from the stored draws. Not built. See also: [dec-B340], [dec-A143].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, predict-training-rows. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B341]

**survivalProbabilities takes offset after newdata, as predict does**
survivalProbabilities(fit, times, newdata, offset, combineChains, ...) puts offset after newdata, as predict does. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: survivalProbabilities(fit, 1, nd, FALSE) puts FALSE on offset and is refused ("'offset' must be numeric"), so a combineChains given by position is refused, not misread; survival is new in 1.0-0. Shown that order, recommended, against offset last, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. offset after newdata, as built and as predict." The rule: one order of these arguments across the fit's methods. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A143. See also: [dec-A143], [dec-B340].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B342]

**A warm start keeps the new fit's prior**
bart(warm.start = oldFit) starts a fit from an earlier fit's trees. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: bart(leaf.prior = normal(k = 2), warm.start = f4), f4 fitted at k = 4, runs at k = 2, where the build before ran at 4; 0.9-34 had no warm start. The maintainer had said of it on 2026-10-02 "That makes sense I think.", unmarked. Shown the donor giving its trees, and sigma and k only where the new fit draws them, recommended, against the new fit adopting the donor's prior and fixed values, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. The donor gives its trees, and sigma and k only where the new fit draws them (as built)." The rule: a warm start installs the donor's trees, and its sigma and k only where the new fit draws them; the new fit's prior and fixed values stand, as a state never changes the model (dec-B195). The alternative overrode the call's own prior in silence. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A146. See also: [dec-A146], [dec-B195].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-02. [dec-B343]

**A gp leaf's lengthscale stays the sampler's at setState**
A gp leaf's kernel has a lengthscale, given or derived, and a saved gp draw replays only under the kernel it was drawn with. Run on 2026-10-08, a state from a sampler at lengthscale 0.5 restored into one at 0.2: with saved draws it is refused ("state holds saved gp draws made under other lengthscales than the ones this sampler was given; a saved draw replays only under the kernel it was drawn with"); without, it installs, TRUE, and the sampler runs at 0.2. gp leaves are new in 1.0-0; the maintainer had said of it on 2026-10-02 "That makes sense I think.", unmarked. Shown that rule, recommended, against the lengthscale travelling with the state and against installing and dropping the mismatched draws, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. As built." The rule: the lengthscale is the sampler's model; a state installs under it, and one whose saved draws were made under another is refused. The alternatives changed the model in silence or lost saved draws in silence. The cost is that such a state cannot be restored into that sampler. Built. Confirms those calls of dec-A146 and dec-A148. See also: [dec-A146], [dec-A148], [dec-B195], [dec-B237].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-02. [dec-B344]

**updateCutPoints takes a word or a logical, nothing else**
dec-B312 makes updateCutPoints one of "none", "position" and "value" and takes a logical with a warning once a session until 1.1-0 (dec-B317). Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: TRUE warns and is taken, 1 and "T" are refused ('must be one of "none", "position", "value"'); 0.9-34 read 1, TRUE and "T" as a logical and took them. The orchestrator recommended, weakly, taking whatever 0.9-34 read as a logical with the same warning, by the rule that a spelling 0.9-34 took warns for a release (dec-B140). The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Refuse anything but a word or a logical, as built." The rule: updateCutPoints is a word or a logical; a number or a string that is not one of the words is refused. The alternative carried 1, 0, "TRUE" and "T" for a release. The cost is that 0.9-34 code writing one of those breaks now. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A183. See also: [dec-B312], [dec-B317], [dec-A183].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B345]

**A basis swapped to one with no rows on it is taken, numeric or factor**
A forest multiplied by a basis, a treatment indicator say, describes an effect on some rows only, and setForestBasis swaps the basis between sweeps for a larger sampler that imputes treatment or group membership. Run on 2026-10-08, one model coded two ways: a numeric 0/1 basis swapped to all zeros is refused ("a 'basis' column of all zeros contributes nothing to a forest"), and a factor basis swapped so one level has no row is taken and runs on; at creation a zero column is refused and an empty factor level dropped (dec-B308), as dec-C14 records; 0.9-34 had no such forests. Shown the split as built, taking both on a swap, recommended, and refusing both, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "B. Take both on a swap; creation still refuses a zero column." The rule: setForestBasis takes a numeric basis column of all zeros as it takes a factor level no row holds, the forest's part on it drawn under its prior until a row returns, as a part no data inform is inefficiency and not degeneracy (dec-B321); at creation a zero column stays refused. The alternatives were the split and refusing both. The cost is that a mistaken zero column on a swap is not caught. Not built. Revises dec-C14 in part. See also: [dec-C14], [dec-B308], [dec-B321].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-r.md, basis-swap-zero-column. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B346]

**summary does not name the latent scale a binary or ordinal family fixes**
dec-B199 ("Option 1.") has summary name each fixed parameter under its table, citing summary.glm's "(Dispersion parameter for binomial family taken to be 1)"; a gaussian fit with sigma = fixed(1) prints "(Fixed, not sampled: sigma = 1, k = 2)". Run on 2026-10-08: a probit summary shows its k row and no such line; 0.9-34 printed none. The orchestrator recommended, weakly, naming the latent sd of 1 on every binary and ordinal fit, after the glm line. The maintainer: "glm would print it? Is that because it's possible to set it a dispersion to something other than 1? My intuition is that, since it isn't a parameter, it's just not relevant." In glm the dispersion scales every standard error, quasibinomial estimates it (run: "taken to be 1.033531") and summary.glm takes a dispersion argument; the latent sd of a probit, logistic or ordinal fit is what identifies the model's scale, set by nothing and estimated by nothing. The orchestrator turned to leaving it off; the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Leave a family-fixed sigma off, as built." The rule: summary names parameters the user fixed and not a scale the family defines. The alternative was the line on every binary and ordinal summary. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A147. See also: [dec-B199], [dec-A147].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B347]

**The count family's dispersion is shape wherever it is reported**
dec-B189 names the negative-binomial r shape and dec-B190 overloads its reader getShape across families; neither names the other places it is reported. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: the fit component, extract(type = "shape"), the summary row, run()'s result, the saved state and the C header all say shape; the family is new in 1.0-0. Shown the bare word, recommended, against nbinom.shape in the reported places, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. The bare word everywhere, as built." The rule: the parameter is shape wherever it is reported, the family telling which. The alternative gave one parameter two spellings. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A145. See also: [dec-B189], [dec-B190], [dec-A145].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B348]

**Ordinal predict takes an offset**
An ordinal fit takes offset and offset.test when fitting; its predict refused one, so predict at the training rows disagreed with fitted(). Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: predict(fit, newdata, offset = o) returns draws by rows by categories, evaluating the fit's offset expression on newdata where none is given, as predict.lm does (dec-B184); ordinal fits are new in 1.0-0. Shown taking it, as probit does, recommended, against refusing offsets on ordinal fits at fit time, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Take it, as probit does (as built)." The rule: ordinal predict takes an offset as every other family's does. The alternative removed a feature the fit has. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A137. See also: [dec-A137], [dec-B184], [dec-B154].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B349]

**pdbart takes a factor's levs by level name only**
pdbart and pd2bart plot a fit as one predictor varies at the values levs gives; a factor predictor is plotted as a factor (dec-B175), where 0.9-34 expanded it into indicator columns and refused its name in xind. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: levs = list(c("1", "10")) plots those levels; levs = list(1:2) stops, "'levs' for factor predictor 'g' must name its levels"; a name absent from training stops and lists it. Shown names only, recommended, as predict's newdata matches a factor by label, against reading numbers as level positions, which goes wrong in silence where the labels are numerals, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Level names only (as built)." The rule: a factor's levs are level names, a character vector or a factor; a number is refused. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A141. See also: [dec-A141], [dec-B175].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B350]

**An argument that holds rows is evaluated once per fit**
A fit can use its rows in more than one place, the trees' design and a leaf basis (linear or gp leaves, new in 1.0-0); while each place evaluated the arguments that pick rows for itself, a random expression gave each place other rows, with no message. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: a subset whose expression returns rows 1:100 on its first call and 101:200 after is evaluated once on the branch, with and without leaf.prior = linear("a"), and once on 0.9-34, which had one use; lm evaluates subset once too, in model.frame. Shown once per fit, recommended, against evaluating at each use, which fits mismatched rows, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Once per fit (as built)." The rule: the first argument, data, test, subset, weights and offset are each evaluated once for a fit and that value is used everywhere; pdbart on a kept fit reads its stored call again, as update() does. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A175. See also: [dec-A175], [dec-B270], [dec-B284].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B351]

**An interrupt no handler takes does what R's top level does**
Under dec-B256 a user's interrupt during a run raises R's interrupt condition. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: a real SIGINT two seconds into a fit reaches a tryCatch interrupt handler at once on the branch, and on 0.9-34 only once the fit has finished; setTimeLimit's error stays an error, caught by try(), on both. Shown copying R's top level, recommended, against invoking the abort restart alone, which drops a browser() session and skips the options, and calling R's own interrupt entry, not documented API on every platform, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Copy R's top level (as built)." The rule: an unhandled interrupt runs options(interrupt = ) if set, else prints the blank line and runs options(error = ), then leaves by the first of the browser, tryRestart and abort restarts; a handled one does none of it; a time limit is re-raised as the error it is. What is still not polled is queued as interrupt-gaps. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A161. See also: [dec-A161], [dec-B256].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B352]

**A held coefficient on a one-column numeric basis is refused until the held multiplier is built**
forest(x1, basis = z, amplitude = fixed()) held the coefficient at 0 on the tip, as update.amplitude = FALSE had, so the forest dropped out of the fit in silence; the planned multiplier slice (forest-multiplier) gives it 1. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: on a numeric z the call stops, naming the zero and pointing to a factor for a column of two values; on factor(z) it fits. Shown refusing until then, recommended, against holding at 0 and building the held value 1 ahead of the slice, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "You can refuse until the feature is built." The maintainer also asked that fixed() take a value other than 1 and that the slice not be called a law; the held value is brought separately. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A171. See also: [dec-A171], [dec-B253], [dec-B271].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B353]

**A held coefficient is 1; the basis carries any coding, and fixed() on a forest takes no other value**
A forest with a basis is one forest whose output each row multiplies by its basis row times the coefficients, m = B a; bartCause's bcf gives its treatment forest the basis cbind(1 - z, z) with coefficients (b0, b1). Asked under dec-B353, the maintainer: "My intuition was that different fixed values make sense for forests with bases, but do they? Is 1 or 0 enough for consumers? What's the good design here?" Held, the multiplier is a fixed number per row, so any held vector on any basis is the one numeric column B a held at 1: a scale on no basis or one column is sd times that scale (dec-B253), a coding such as (-1/2, 1/2) is the column z - 0.5, and several columns held at any values are their combination. bartCause's bcf(update.b = FALSE) holds (0, 1), the original bcf model, and stan4bart passes no basis. Shown that design, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Yes, let's do that." The rule: amplitude = fixed() holds the coefficient at 1 and the multiplier is what the basis says, 1 with no basis, the column as given for one numeric column, 0 for a two-level factor's reference level and 1 for the other, as R's default coding; another coding is written as a numeric column; fixed() with a value other than 1 is refused on a forest, pointing to the basis for a coding and to sd for a size; more than two levels or several columns held stay refused. The engine's holding by forest position goes. The alternatives were per-level values in fixed() on a factor basis and any value anywhere, each a second spelling of what the basis or sd says. Not built; the forest-multiplier slice builds it. See also: [dec-B253], [dec-B271], [dec-B353], [dec-A171].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B354]

**setData and setCutPoints reset a monotone tree they put out of order, as a forced update does**
New predictor values or a new cut grid can empty leaves and merge them beside neighbours they did not border, leaving a monotone tree's leaves out of order; dec-B278 keeps a forced setPredictor's reset of such a tree to all-zero leaves, which the next sweep draws, and refuses the unforced one. setData and setCutPoints take no force flag and always complete (setCutPoints always forced, dec-B319). Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: the monotone test file passes on the tip, 215 results, among them a hand-built tree reset after a forced update and a flattened column whose merged trees are reset, the fit monotone throughout. A warm start is no longer under this call: dec-B295 collapses a donor tree at a split it cannot hold. Shown the reset, recommended, against refusing those calls on a monotone sampler, which breaks the embedded use, and drawing the reset trees' leaves at the call, a draw in a call that draws nothing, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Reset, as a forced update does (as built)." The rule: setData and setCutPoints reset a monotone tree they leave out of order to all-zero leaves, drawing nothing. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A129. See also: [dec-A129], [dec-B278], [dec-B295], [dec-B319].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B355]

**Switching a drawn leaf prior between its k and sd spellings keeps the spread**
The spread in force is k.scale / k, and k.scale depends on the spelling: the data's scale under normal(k = ), twice the named scale under normal(sd = invchi(scale = s)). setLeafPrior kept the engine's k across a switch, so the spread jumped by the ratio of the two k.scale values, as its help said. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: after 200 sweeps under normal(k = chi()) with spread 2.67, a switch to normal(sd = invchi(scale = 2.67)) left k at 4.00 and halved the spread to 1.34, a prior centred on the spread in force moving it. The leaves were drawn under the spread; k is that spread against a k.scale the spelling defines, so keeping k moved the state as a side effect of a model change. Shown keeping k, as built under dec-B201, against keeping the spread by re-expressing k against the new k.scale, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "B. Keep the spread." The rule: a switch between the k and sd spellings of a drawn leaf prior keeps each chain's spread in force, k becoming k_old x k.scale_new / k.scale_old; k stays the engine's k as reported. Not ruled: a changed invchi() scale within the sd spelling, which moves k.scale by the same mechanism. Not built. Revises that call of dec-A121. See also: [dec-A121], [dec-B201], [dec-B254].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B356]

**A survival fit's response range comes from its observed times**
An aft fit's leaf prior takes its centre and scale from the range of log time; a censored row's true time is later than its censoring time and each chain draws it. Each chain had re-anchored to its own drawn times; the build reads the observed times, a censored row at its censoring time, at creation and at every re-derivation. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: 200 rows, 137 censored, two chains: every chain has centre 0.441 and scale 2.69, the observed log-time range (-0.904, 1.786), at creation and after setOffset(updateScale = TRUE). BART's abart does the same, log(times) on every row, once. Shown observed times, recommended, under dec-B296, against each chain's drawn times, under which chains would target different posteriors, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Observed times (as built)." The rule: a survival fit's range is read from the observed times of the rows in the likelihood, a censored row at its censoring time, one prior for every chain; under heavy censoring it understates the true times' spread. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A180. See also: [dec-A180], [dec-B296], [dec-B327].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B357]

**A leaf covariate with one value is centred at that value exactly**
Linear leaves standardize each leaf covariate by a centre and a spread; a column of one value has spread 0 and is handled as constant. The centre had been taken as sum / n, which misses the value by rounding (150 rows of 0.1), giving a spread of 2.5e-16 and predictions just off the value near 1e14. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: sum(c) / 150 differs from 0.1 where mean(c) does not; a fit with linear("c") on that column predicts at 0.1, 0.1001 and 0.2 within (0.42, 0.67). Shown exact centring, recommended, against the rounded mean, a numerical defect, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Exact (as built)." The rule: a single-valued leaf covariate is centred at its value exactly. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A177. See also: [dec-A177], [dec-B328], [dec-B329].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B358]

**setPredictor takes a data frame whole; a numeric matrix only where every column is numeric**
A factor predictor keeps its declared levels (dec-A115). setPredictor(x) with no column replaces every predictor; given a numeric matrix carrying a factor column as integer codes, the only practical source being as.matrix(sampler$data@x), it had rebuilt the levels from the codes present, and copy() then refused the sampler's own state; the fix kept the declared levels (dec-A166). Run by the triage on 2026-10-08 on the branch, on the commit before the fix and on 0.9-34: the fix holds; setPredictor(df, column = c("a", "f")) matches labels and keeps the levels on both branch builds, droplevels() or not; setPredictor(df) with no column fails on all three, "'list' object cannot be coerced to type 'double'"; the column form refuses a number for a factor column where the whole form took a matrix of codes. bairrtt updates one column at a time; treatSens only the test set. Asked what a whole matrix meant, the maintainer: "It's a real pain-in-the-ass to carry mixed data in a matrix. If you were referring to data frames, I'd get it." Shown keeping the declared levels on the matrix route and, as the next question, a data frame taken whole with a numeric matrix refused on a sampler with a factor column, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Yeah, matrices should only work when matrices were given as an input (which, not may not be enforceable from the shape of the original input, I'm not sure, but is obvious from the column types)." The rule: setPredictor with no column takes a data frame, coded by label as the column form is; a numeric matrix is taken only where every predictor column is numeric, judged by the column types, and refused by name on a sampler with a factor column. Not ruled: setTestPredictor and predict, which also take a matrix of codes on a factor fit. Not built. Confirms dec-A166 where it applies. See also: [dec-A166], [dec-A115].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B359]

**setTestPredictor and predict take a numeric matrix only where every column is numeric**
dec-B359 named setPredictor. Run on 2026-10-08 on the branch: sampler$setTestPredictor(as.matrix(sampler$data@x)[1:10, ]) is taken on a sampler with a factor column, and predict(fit, cbind(a = 0.5, f = 2)) on a bart(y ~ a + f) fit is taken, reading 2 as f's second level; predict.lm refuses a matrix newdata ("'data' must be a data.frame, not a matrix or an array"). Shown the same rule for those routes, recommended, against leaving them taking codes, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Apply the same rule to setTestPredictor and predict." The rule: every route that takes new predictors, setPredictor, setTestPredictor and predict, takes a numeric matrix only where every predictor column is numeric, judged by the column types, and refuses it by name on a fit with a factor column, pointing to a data frame. Not built. See also: [dec-B359].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B360]

**samplePriorPredictive draws under the prior the sampler runs under**
samplePriorPredictive (new in 1.0-0) draws from a private sampler built from the sampler's model and data; built fresh, that private sampler had anchored its prior to the response as it then stood, so after setResponse(updateScale = FALSE) it described a prior the sampler was not running. The build hands it the sampler's recorded transform. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: prior draws with mean 0.53 and sd 0.54 at creation stay there after setResponse(10 y + 50, updateScale = FALSE) and move to 55.2 and 5.38 after updateScale = TRUE. The maintainer asked whether it is a real concern, the usual call being samplePriorPredictive(sampler) on a sampler left as made; it arises only where code swaps the response without the scale update, an embedded loop, and then asks for prior draws. Shown the sampler's prior, recommended, against a prior re-derived from the current response, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "I think "A. Draw from the prior the sampler runs under (as built)." is correct, but is this a real concern?" The rule: samplePriorPredictive draws under the prior the sampler runs under. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A148. See also: [dec-A148], [dec-B296], [dec-B327].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B361]

**A sampler of several forests keeps refusing a re-derived response scale in 1.0-0**
Each forest's size, forest(sd = ), is stated against the response scale fixed at creation, and the engine cannot restate those sizes on a new scale, so setResponse and setOffset with updateScale = TRUE refuse a sampler of several forests; several forests are new in 1.0-0. dec-B303's setForestBasis(updateBasisScale = TRUE) reads a basis column's scale over the rows in at its call, so after a weight change it and the response scale span different rows. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: on y ~ forest(x1) + forest(x1, basis = z), setResponse(2 * y, updateScale = TRUE) stops, naming forest amplitudes and updateScale = FALSE; updateScale = FALSE is taken. bartCause's bcf fits once; stan4bart runs one forest. Shown keeping the refusal for 1.0-0, recommended, against building the re-derivation now, engine work in code the forest slices rewrite, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Keep the refusal for 1.0-0." The rule: in 1.0-0 a sampler of several forests refuses updateScale = TRUE, the help saying the response and a re-derived basis scale may span different rows; re-derivation on several forests is for after the release. Confirms that call of dec-A176. See also: [dec-A176], [dec-B303], [dec-B265].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B362]

**A sampler built by hand states its creation mask with setActiveRows(mask, updateScale = TRUE)**
A count fit's leaf-prior centre comes from the rows in the likelihood at creation (dec-B296); its 0/1 weights become a mask. The response-scale plan has dbarts() re-derive after installing the mask, while a sampler built by hand from dbartsSpec and new("dbartsSampler", ...) is created with every row in and keeps that centre unless its caller makes one more call, which the plan gave as setOffset(offset, updateScale = TRUE). Not built; the plan and the consumers read on 2026-10-08: stan4bart builds by hand and fits no count model under a mask. The maintainer asked whether the by-hand route has a way to say the same thing and how a loop whose mask changes would be written: under dec-B327, setActiveRows gains updateScale (the plan's slice C), so setActiveRows(spec$active, updateScale = TRUE) once after creation gives the sampler dbarts() makes, and in a loop setActiveRows(mask, updateScale = i <= n.burn) re-derives in burn-in and holds after. Shown that as the documented call, recommended, against creation taking the mask, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "We can do A', as long as the help is correctly updated when the feature is implemented." The rule: a sampler made by hand is created with every row in; dbartsSpec's help gives setActiveRows(spec$active, updateScale = TRUE) as the call that makes it dbarts()'s, and shows the loop form; the setOffset route is not documented. The help is updated when slice C is built. Not built. Confirms that call of dec-A176, with the documented call revised. See also: [dec-A176], [dec-B296], [dec-B327].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B363]

**A re-derivation where the rows in hold fewer than two distinct values keeps the scale in force, with one warning**
Under dec-B296 updateScale = TRUE re-derives the prior from the rows in at its call; dec-B302 creates a sampler with no row in from every row, with a warning, its caller a mixture whose component starts empty, and its entry recorded the design's call that a re-derivation with no row in is refused, which the plan deferred to slice C, reading every row in silence until then. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08 on the branch, the rows-in rule not built: two clusters at 0 and 20, re-derived with all rows in, rows 1 to 50 in and none in, give centre 10.20 and scale 24.22 each time. A mixture component re-deriving in burn-in can lose all its rows, or all but one value, mid-run. Shown refusing, keeping the scale in force with one warning per sampler, recommended, and every row with a warning as at creation, which jumps a component to the whole data's scale, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "B. Keep the scale in force." The rule: setResponse, setOffset, setWeights and setActiveRows with updateScale = TRUE, where the rows in hold fewer than two distinct values (none in, or one value), complete, keep the prior in force and warn once per sampler; creation keeps dec-B302's every-row fallback. Not built. Revises the re-derivation call recorded in dec-B302 and that call of dec-A176. See also: [dec-B302], [dec-B296], [dec-B309], [dec-A176].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B364]

**A hazard fit's period column is allowed on every forest**
A discrete-time hazard fit adds a period column the caller never supplies, so the trees can let the hazard change over time. As built a single forest always allows it whatever its vars, while each of several forests (reachable through dbarts(forests = list(...)); the formula interface refuses a basis forest on a hazard fit) takes vars literally, so one not naming period is constant over time. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: the forest-vars tests pass on the tip (171 results), a single forest restricted to a and b splitting on period and the first of two forests so restricted not; BART's surv.pre.bart always puts time in the first column. Shown the build, period on every forest, recommended, and vars literal everywhere, which fits a hazard constant over time in silence, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "B. Always allow period, on every forest." The rule: every forest of a hazard fit may split on period whatever its vars; a forest constant over time has no spelling in 1.0-0 and may gain an explicit one later. Not built for several forests. Revises that call of dec-A169. See also: [dec-A169].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B365]

**Of three fixed engine limits, the cut cap stays, the perturb width is exposed and the leaf-covariate cap goes**
dec-A46 kept three limits as compile-time constants: 65533 cuts a predictor, the 16-bit code's room with one value for missing; the perturb move's window of one grid position; and at most 8 leaf covariates for linear and gp leaves. Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: linear() on 8 columns is taken and on 9 stops ("at most 8 leaf covariates are supported"); n.cuts = 70000 stops at the cap of 65533. Read: the perturb move is exposed as proposal.probs' perturb, default 0, its benefit trial killed at width 1 (docs/design/perturb-move.md); its width was kept a constant because acceptance fell with width, effective sample size showed no gain, and OpenBT's 10 percent and Pratola's 85 percent were never compared, a reason that holds as well for proposal.probs, which is exposed. The cap of 8 sized a stack scratch when linear leaves were added (f2893296); nothing measured chose it, and the code's own note finds neither cost nor rank deficiency binding there. The maintainer asked "Why isn't the perturb move's step exposed the way other proposal mechanism parameters are?" and "is there a good reason why 8 was chosen?", and of the cut cap: "For the cut points, I think that's reasonable." Shown exposing the width or keeping it, and removing, raising or keeping the cap, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Expose it in dbartsControl beside proposal.probs, default 1." and "Remove the cap by sizing the buffer at creation." The rule: the cut cap stays fixed and refused above, as built; the perturb window is a dbartsControl setting beside proposal.probs, a whole number of grid positions, default 1, which changes no draw; linear and gp leaves take any number of covariates, their scratch sized at creation.
