Not built for the last two. Revises those calls of dec-A46. See also: [dec-A46], [dec-B91].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B366]

**hazard(max.rows = ) keeps its default of ten million rows, and the help and the refusal state the memory a row costs**
A discrete-time hazard fit expands each subject into a row per period at risk; with the default grid of every distinct event time the expansion grows about with the square of the subjects, and an expansion over max.rows is refused, naming hazard(breaks = ) and hazard(max.rows = ). Run by the triage on 2026-10-08: 300 subjects expand to 45150 rows, refused at max.rows = 1000 with that message; peak memory at 75 trees measured about 210 bytes a row shared plus about 630 a row for each chain, one stored value a row a tree, so ten million rows take about 8.5 GB on one chain and 27 GB on bart()'s four. BART's surv.bart has no cap. The maintainer asked whether a gigabyte, the orchestrator's unmeasured estimate, is modest and a higher default makes sense; shown the measurement and keeping 1e7 with the cost stated, recommended, lowering it to 2e6, and raising it, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Use your recommendation for this issue." The rule: max.rows defaults to 1e7 and is settable; the help and the refusal give the cost, about 0.85 KB a row a chain at 75 trees and growing with the trees. Not built for the stated cost. Confirms that call of dec-A22. See also: [dec-A22].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B367]

**An infinite case weight is refused by name**
Run by the triage on 2026-10-08 with one weight of Inf in fifty: the branch stops, "'weights' must all be finite", on every entry that takes weights; 0.9-34 stopped, "unable to obtain a starting estimate of sigma; provide one instead", a message that points away from the cause; lm stops, "NA/NaN/Inf in 'x'". Shown refusing by name, recommended, against 0.9-34's indirect failure, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. Refuse by name (as built)." The rule: an infinite case weight is refused, naming the weights. Built. Confirms that call of dec-A157. See also: [dec-A157].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B368]

**A changed hyperprior scale on a drawn leaf spread keeps the spread at the call**
dec-B356 kept the spread across a switch between the k and sd spellings and left open a changed invchi() scale within the sd spelling, which moves k.scale (twice the scale) by the same mechanism; under the k spelling k.scale is the data's scale and a changed chi() leaves the spread alone. Run on 2026-10-08 on the branch: after 200 sweeps under normal(sd = invchi(scale = 2)), setLeafPrior(normal(sd = invchi(scale = 4))) doubled the spread at the call (2.46 to 4.93), while chi(1.5, 2) to chi(1.5, 4) left it at 2.95. The maintainer asked whether the change does not express that the spread should change; the prior changes, and the spread is drawn under it from the next sweep. Run: 2000 posterior draws give a spread of median 2.85 (90 percent 2.06 to 4.54) under scale 2 and 3.24 (2.32 to 4.91) under scale 4; as built a chain at 3.47 jumped to 6.94 at the call and came back over about ten sweeps (5.03, 4.65, 3.66, 2.91 at sweeps 1, 5, 10, 30). Shown keeping the spread at the call against the jump, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "OK, then A. Keep the spread at the call." The rule: a setLeafPrior that changes a drawn leaf spread's hyperprior, its spelling or its scale, keeps each chain's spread in force at the call, k re-expressed against the new k.scale; the new prior acts from the next draw. A fixed sd stated sets the spread. Not built. Extends dec-B356. See also: [dec-B356], [dec-A121], [dec-B254].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B369]

**An indicator expansion is built sparse column by column under sparseDensityThreshold, and the storage changes no result or return type**
Where a factor is expanded into one indicator column a level (bartBT's data-frame path, factors = "indicators", makeModelMatrixFromDataFrame), R builds the expansion sparse past an internal 100 levels when Matrix is installed (dec-A26 said by sparseDensityThreshold, at no cost). Run by the independent assessment and rerun by the orchestrator on 2026-10-08, a 150-level factor: bartBT(df, y) starts sigma at 1.418 with a warning about sparse columns the user never gave, where 0.9-34 gave the linear-model 0.296; makeModelMatrixFromDataFrame returns a dbartsMixedMatrix, not a matrix, so rowSums and %*% fail, where 0.9-34 returned a 400 x 138 matrix; both depend on whether Matrix is installed. The engine already stores a column of a sparse design sparse only at or below sparseDensityThreshold (0.2) of nonzero entries. bartXViz, maple and riAFTBART on CRAN call makeModelMatrixFromDataFrame. The maintainer, having taken the question to be about factors kept as factors: "If they're being blown up into indicators, I think your revised proposal makes sense." The rule: R builds an indicator column sparse or dense by the same sparseDensityThreshold the engine uses, in place of a level count; the starting sigma takes the same linear-model estimate whichever the storage; makeModelMatrixFromDataFrame returns a plain matrix unless the caller supplied a sparse column, as its help says. Not built. Revises dec-A26's storage call. See also: [dec-A26], [dec-B100].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B370]

**k's slow mixing under probit is fixed in the sampler, by a move that changes the fit's scale in one step**
The probit-k calibration arm (dec-B301) put k's ranks in excess at both ends (end bins 48 and 52 of 30, chi-square p 0.012 and 0.013). An investigator traced it to slow mixing; the maintainer: "We've been tricked by this before, where we think we know why a diagnostic is off but there is actually an underlying root cause we're not measuring. Have the finding verified." An independent verifier on 2026-10-08 found it holds: chains started at the drawn truth show no drift over 9080 replications (+0.007 in log k, se 0.004, against +0.135 for the half-shape error), and on a separated toy with a known answer the small-k mass (0.103) is exact from exact starts while free chains reach it too rarely; but a correct sampler gives end bins averaging 47 against 30 and a median chi-square p of 0.004, so the arm's flag fires about one run in three. The maintainer asked "If we start at truth, is it a proper SBC?" (no: it tests that a sweep leaves the posterior invariant, as Geweke's test does, and a chain that never moves passes) and "step back a bit, this seems a little bad. What's a good fix here?" k and the leaves form a funnel and probit's data augmentation mixes slowly in overall scale, near separation most; every binary fit draws k by default, as 0.9-34's bart2 did. Shown adding one cheap move a sweep that changes the fit's scale in one step, interweaving on k (Yu and Meng 2011) or a probit parameter expansion (Liu and Wu 1999), chosen by a measured comparison, built before 1.0-0 with an exact gate, the arm meanwhile read as expected to flag, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Go ahead with your proposal." The rule: before 1.0-0 the sampler gains a scale move for k under probit, designed by comparing the two forms on k's autocorrelation time and small-k visitation; the probit-k arm keeps its flag and becomes an ordinary SBC again once it lands; until then the arm is read by its end bins (benchmarks/README.md). Not built. Revises dec-A184's call that the arm flags k on its chi-square p. See also: [dec-B301], [dec-A184].
Record: this register; docs/design/probit-k-calibration.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B371]

**A row at weight 0 draws its posterior predictive as an ordinary observation, at weight 1**
On gaussian, Student-t and aft fits case weights are variance weights, a row's noise sd sigma / sqrt(w), and a predictive draw follows its row's weight, where 0.9-34 used sigma at every row; a weight of 0, the documented way to hold a row out, gave an infinite sd and NA draws with only R's "NAs produced" (dec-A157 required predictive weights finite and non-negative). Found by the independent assessment and rerun on 2026-10-08: weights cycling 0, 1, 16, extract(fit, "ppd") gives a spread of NA, 0.585 and 0.147 on the branch and 0.358 at every weight on 0.9-34. predict.lm predicts at weight 1 unless told otherwise. Shown drawing a weight-0 row as an ordinary observation, with an explicit predictive weight of 0 refused by name, recommended, against NA with a message and refusing weight 0 wherever ppd is asked, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "A. A row at weight 0 draws as an ordinary observation, weight 1." The rule: in extract(fit, "ppd") and fitted(type = "ppd") a training row at weight 0 draws at weight 1; predict(type = "ppd", weights = ) refuses a weight of 0 by name, the message saying to pass 1 for an ordinary observation; the help states both. Not built. Revises that call of dec-A157. See also: [dec-A157], [dec-B296].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B372]

**Minor calls, group 2 (refusals and their messages), approved; setCategoryTestOffset loses updateState**
Group 2, each refusing input that is a mistake or changing a message: the test-data setters take no updateState, a call passing it getting R's own "unused argument", as on 0.9-34 (dec-A118); NaN is refused, never read as not given, bartBT keeping BayesTree's reading of NA and NaN for sigest as the one exception (dec-A118); setState names the monotone cause of a refusal (dec-A129); a categoricalExhaustiveCap slot written above 30 is refused where the engine receives it as dbartsControl already refuses it (dec-A164); a forest given as a Date, complex, raw or a vector of labels is refused by class, and two all-basis refusals end "leave it out, or fit with dbarts()" (dec-A181); a control whose n.threads slot is NA is refused naming the slot, and setPredictor(forceUpdate = NA) is refused before anything changes (dec-A182); a family object with a setting its constructor lacks is refused by name (dec-A144); an unfound constructor's hint names the list its argument reads (dec-C09); the one-forest refusals of sd and amplitude say where the size goes (dec-A171). The maintainer asked of the first "is it even an argument? It doesn't seem like it should be, so the error should be a normal one handled by R" (it is not, and the error is R's) and for background on the cap. setCategoryTestOffset, new in 1.0-0, alone of the test setters takes updateState and stores the state; test data are not sampler state. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "The rest seem fine to me." and, shown removing it there: "Those seem fine to me." The rule: as built, and setCategoryTestOffset takes no updateState. Built but for that removal. Confirms those calls of dec-A118, dec-A129, dec-A164, dec-A181, dec-A182, dec-A144, dec-C09 and dec-A171.

**Minor calls, group 3 (fixes of things that failed), approved as built**
Group 3, each fixing a call that errored or misbehaved: k = chi() through a wrapper's dots fits, where 0.9-34 could not find chi (dec-C11); bart called through another name forwards its arguments, where 0.9-34's bart2 failed inside a function (dec-C13); setControl stores a control only after the engine accepts it, a defect 0.9-34 had (dec-A129); the truncated-normal draw reports a stalled tail as NaN and takes a uniform proposal on a narrow far-tail interval, draws changing only there, in ordinal fits and monotone leaves (dec-A129); proposal.probs naming a move twice is refused where 0.9-34 dropped one; a test factor declaring levels unused in training is accepted under factors = "indicators", as lm; keepCall = FALSE leaves no call on the fits that packaged their own results; the column-update label rule covers ordered factors and sparseFactor (dec-A136); retired resid.prior and split.probs resolve where written, and bartBT names bart(family = "ordinal") for an ordered response of three or more levels (dec-A122). **Minor calls, group 4 (what a fit carries and summary shows), approved; a held value lives only in fit$fixed**
Group 4: a fit's new fields, leaf.prior and fixed, do not start with k, since $ matches by prefix, and fit$k on a fixed-k fit is NULL as on 0.9-34; plot draws no panel for a held sigma or shape; rbart gains no k or leaf.prior.sd types; first.sd goes with sd and first.k stays (dec-B192); summary's Student-t df row and the ordinal first threshold named fixed at 0; k.scale under sd = invchi(df, 0) is the data's scale; an NA spread is refused wherever a leaf prior is given (dec-A124); setLeafPrior serves DART samplers (dec-A121); all dec-A147 but where named. Run on 2026-10-08: under gaussian(sigma = fixed(1)) the fit held 1 three ways, fit$sigma and fit$first.sigma as vectors of 1s and fit$fixed$sigma, where a held k has fit$k NULL and its value in fit$fixed$k. fit$fixed lists each parameter the package can draw or hold, when the fit held it: sigma (gaussian, aft), shape (nbinom), k (per forest), resid.df (Student-t); settings never drawn (power, base, a gp lengthscale) live with the priors, and constants a family fixes are not listed (dec-B347); a forest coefficient held by amplitude = fixed() was missing. No fit stores the leaf sd: k and first.k, or fixed$k, with k.scale in fit$leaf.prior, and extract(fit, "leaf.prior.sd") computing k.scale / k. The maintainer asked "I don't know that 2. makes sense", how first.sd differs from first.sigma (the leaf prior's spread against the residual sd), why fit$fixed and whether it holds every pinned parameter, and whether the leaf sd is stored beside k (no); then on 2026-10-08: "Oh, ok. Approved." The rule: as built, except that a held sigma or shape is stored only in fit$fixed, fit$sigma, fit$first.sigma and their shape counterparts absent, as for a held k, the package's readers (plot, summary, predictive draws, the log-likelihood) reading the held value there; and fit$fixed carries a held forest coefficient, per forest. Not built for those two. Revises dec-A147's call that a fixed sigma or shape stays repeated per draw; confirms the rest of those calls of dec-A147, dec-A124 and dec-A121. bartCause tells a binary fit by a missing sigma and must read fit$fixed for a held one. See also: [dec-B192], [dec-B199], [dec-B347], [dec-B354].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B376]

**Review and gates follow what a change can break**
The maintainer asked on 2026-10-08 whether implementation is efficient with its tests and checks: "I feel as if progress has slown down and I wonder if that's because we've become unnecessarily cautious." Read from the record: about six landings on 2026-10-06 and ten on 2026-10-07, two on 2026-10-08, a day given to decisions; engine reviews found real defects (the cut grid's two blocking findings, the check-shape mixing gate's false failures, the assessors' A26 and weight-0 draws), while a 270-line slice with no package code took three opus reviews and a 160-stream measurement, every fix round took a fresh review, the local landing battery repeated what CI runs, each code push waited for the previous commit's CI because a push cancelled it, and each landing touched about six records. Shown three tiers by what a change can break, batching of small rulings, a per-commit CI concurrency group on bartcore and fewer records a landing, with a measurement of the last twelve landings to check against afterwards, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Go ahead. Do the measurement too." The rule: docs/plans/README.md, Process by risk: a change to draws keeps a plan with a blind critique, one opus review with mutants whose reviewer also checks the fix round unless the fixes change engine behaviour, and the full local battery; an R-surface change takes a short plan or none, one sonnet review and the touched tests locally, CI running the rest; docs, text, workflows and records take no review; small rulings are batched; a landing records the ledger entry, the plan and TODO, the indexes and design Status lines brought up to date in batches; on bartcore each commit is its own CI concurrency group, so an R-surface slice may land while an engine slice's CI runs. Built (the contract and the six per-push workflows).
Record: docs/plans/README.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B383]

**A restored state keeps its leaf spread: k is re-expressed against the recipient's k.scale**
A state carries a drawn k; installed into a sampler whose k.scale differs (another response's units) it installed as the stored k, so the chain started one sweep with the spread jumped by the ratio of the two k.scale values (dec-A146, a call the agents made and did not put). The independent coverage check of 2026-10-08 found dec-B201, cited as covering it, names the reader's field and what k is reported as, not what an install carries, while dec-B356 and dec-B369 keep the spread when k.scale changes. Shown keeping the state's spread on install, k re-expressed against the recipient's k.scale, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "3. Use your recommendation." The rule: setState, a copy and a reload install a drawn leaf scale as the spread in force in the state, k becoming k_state x k.scale_recipient / k.scale_state. Not built. Revises that call of dec-A146. See also: [dec-A146], [dec-B201], [dec-B356], [dec-B369].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B384]

**setControl given a control whose tree count differs takes it, keeps the sampler's count and warns**
A control copied from another sampler or fit, given to setControl, was refused as changing the tree count (dec-A158, dec-A179). dec-B247 refuses a stated change of n.trees on a live sampler; dec-B334 has a copied control state no count, with a warning where its count is not used, naming new fits and dec-B335 new("dbartsSampler"), not setControl. Shown treating setControl as dec-B334 does, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "4. Use your recommendation." The rule: setControl given a control whose count differs from the sampler's, where the count was carried rather than written, takes the control, keeps the sampler's count and warns that the control's count is not used; a count the caller wrote stays refused under dec-B247. Not built; with the control migration. Revises those calls of dec-A158 and dec-A179. See also: [dec-B247], [dec-B334], [dec-B335].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B385]

**A constant response's window is c - 0.5 to c + 0.5**
The engine maps the response onto [-0.5, 0.5] by (y - min) / range - 0.5; for a constant response the range is 0, set to 1 with min left at c, so the value lands at -0.5 and the window is c to c + 1, as in 0.9-34's bartFit.cpp; a stored (c, c) pair is read the same way on install (dec-A148). Run on 2026-10-08: a sampler on a response constant at 5 draws prior fits with mean 5.52, the leaf prior centred half a unit off the data. The maintainer asked "Why c to c + 1? Why not c - 0.5 to c + 0.5?"; shown the symmetric window at creation and on reading a stored pair, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Use your recommendations for 7, 9, and 10." The rule: a constant response's transform is the window c - 0.5 to c + 0.5, at creation, at a re-derivation and when a stored (c, c) is read. Changes the draws of fits on a constant response only, which already warn. Not built. Revises that call of dec-A148. See also: [dec-A148], [dec-A167].
Record: this register. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B386]

**treatSens takes the default chain count, keeping its total draws**
treatSens's lockstep branch calls pdbart, and cibart builds its control, with n.chains = 1, to keep 0.9-34's draw count and cost, the 1.0-0 default being four chains (dec-A157, a consumer call). The maintainer: "Seems like multiple threading might be nice. Any reason for this?" Shown the default chain count with n.samples = ceiling(nsim / n.chains) per chain and threads by default, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Use your recommendations for 7, 9, and 10." The rule: treatSens's null treatment model (pdbart) and cibart run the default chains on threads, each chain taking ceiling(nsim / n.chains) kept draws so the total is kept; burn-in runs per chain. Not built; on treatSens's dbarts-1.0 branch. Revises that call of dec-A157.

The triage's 83 settled and 27 internal calls were checked independently on 2026-10-08: 76 settled calls covered by the maintainer's rulings as cited, 23 internal calls invisible to users and to the packages that call dbarts. Of the rest: dec-A157's merged-install signal is decided by dec-B305 and dec-A147's per-forest leaf.prior.sd shape by dec-B275 (not built); dec-A146's drawn k on install went to dec-B384, dec-A158's and dec-A179's setControl count to dec-B385, a constant response's window to dec-B386 and treatSens's chains to dec-B387; xbart's sd-grid details (dec-A121), a held forest coefficient not carried by a state (dec-A146), pdbart and pd2bart sharing one once-a-session notice (dec-A154), and NEWS naming the four functions, dbartsControl, bart, rbart_vi and xbart, that refused to run in 0.9-34 where the cores could not be counted (dec-A182) are approved as built. The maintainer on 2026-10-08: "THe rest are fine." and "Use your recommendations for 7, 9, and 10." The triage of the unmarked agent entries is closed; marking follows.

**The repo holds what has value shared or under version control; scratch holds the temporary and the near-term**
Ledger Record lines and some TODO items cited untracked scratch/ paths, agent reports and measurements, which dangle for anyone but the orchestrator. Asked whether tracked text may cite them, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "The repo should be for things that have value in being shared, or value in being version controlled. That's a bit subjective, so you'll have to use your judgement, but if it is just for temporary files or things that are going to be done in the very near future, scratch is fine." The rule: evidence a lasting record rests on moves into the repo (the k calibration finding became docs/design/probit-k-calibration.md); a one-off review aid whose findings the record already states stays in scratch and is not cited from the ledger; tracked text may point at scratch only for work to be done shortly (a TODO probe). Applied the same day to the ledger, TODO and the process contract.

**The binary leaf-scale default is revisited after the merge to main and before 1.0-0, once k's scale move lands**
dec-A07 kept chi(1.5, 2) as the binary leaf-scale default (confirmed 2026-09-14) with "A revisit stays scheduled for after the mixing research, since the sampled k's own non-convergence leaves the optimum's location resolved only to about a factor of two in scale." dec-B371 schedules the fix for k's slow mixing under probit. Shown rerunning the September study on converged chains once that move lands, before 1.0-0, against after the release, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Hmm, I was actually thinking about mixing beyond just k. BART trees are too sticky and we need to figure out what to do. We can address maybe A07 however. And we should do that before 1.0-0 but after the main cutover." The rule: after the merge to main and before 1.0-0, with the k move in place, the binary hyperprior study (docs/plans/binary-hyperprior.md) is rerun on converged chains and the default brought back to the maintainer with the numbers. The wider mixing research the maintainer names, trees too sticky, stays in TODO tree-mixing-proposals, which also holds dec-A10's revisit of the rejection-sampled starting forest. Not built. See also: [dec-A07], [dec-A10], [dec-B371].
