**The perturb window is n.perturb.cuts**
dec-B366 makes the perturb move's window a dbartsControl setting beside proposal.probs, a whole number of grid positions, default 1; its name was left open. The setting counts cut positions, the unit n.cuts names. Shown n.perturb.cuts (the n. prefix the maintainer takes for counts, dec-B218, dec-B220), recommended, against perturb.width (proposal.probs' dotted style) and perturbWidth (the engine's name, in the camelCase of the threshold settings), the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "n.perturb.cuts". The rule: dbartsControl(n.perturb.cuts = 1L), the most cut positions a perturb proposal moves a split either way. The cost of the alternatives was a count without the n. prefix; the cost of this one is a possible reading as how many cuts are perturbed, which the help closes. Not built. Extends dec-B366. See also: [dec-B366], [dec-B218], [dec-B220].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-engine.md, perturb-width-control. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B391]

**A fixed sd turned drawn keeps the sd in force**
dec-B369 keeps each chain's spread at a setLeafPrior that changes a drawn leaf spread's invchi() scale; a fixed sd made drawn was not shown. As built, setLeafPrior keeps the engine's k, and a fixed sd is stored as k = 2 against k.scale = twice the sd, so the chain starts at the new prior's named scale. Run on 2026-10-08 on the branch: after 200 sweeps under normal(sd = 0.5), setLeafPrior(normal(sd = invchi(3, 2))) moved the spread from 0.50 to 2.00 at the call. The maintainer: "Changing the named value within the sd spelling should be solved by the user's intent. They're trying to actively set the sd of the leaf prior, so both of their values should be interpretted as such." Shown starting at the new named scale (R1, as built) against keeping the sd in force until the next draw, which comes from the new prior (R2, recommended, dec-B369's rule), the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "I agree with R2." The rule: within the sd spelling, a setLeafPrior from a fixed sd into a drawn one keeps each chain's sd in force at the call, k re-expressed against the new k.scale; the new prior acts from the next draw. A switch between the k and sd spellings out of a fixed prior is not ruled here. Not built. Extends dec-B369. See also: [dec-B369], [dec-B356].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-engine.md, leaf-prior-spelling-keeps-spread. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B392]

**Every setLeafPrior switch keeps the sd in force into a drawn prior**
dec-B356 and dec-B392 left open a switch between the k and sd spellings out of a fixed prior into a drawn one. As built, setLeafPrior keeps the engine's k, so the sd jumps by the ratio of the two k.scale values. Run on 2026-10-08 on the branch: fixed k = 2 to sd = invchi(3, 1) moved the sd from 3.08 to 1.00 at the call; fixed sd = 0.5 to k = chi(1.5, 2) from 0.50 to 3.08. The maintainer: "if a person installs a fixed value of k, they clearly mean it to be interpretted literally so that should also reset the k.scale", "Treat drawn k priors and fixed k priors the same - reset the k scales. The person is trying to put a distribution on k itself and otherwise the interpretation is off." and "I don't see changing the prior as changing the state / the active parameter. For that, you'd need a setK() function." Shown keeping the sd in force with k re-expressed against the new k.scale (i, recommended) against keeping k's number (ii, as built), the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Yes, keep until the next draw." The rule: k.scale always belongs to the new prior's spelling (the data's scale under k, twice the named value under sd); into a fixed prior the stated value is taken literally; into a drawn prior each chain's sd in force stays, k re-expressed against the new k.scale, and the new prior acts from the next draw. Changing the prior never moves the state; no setter for k exists. Not built. Extends dec-B356 and dec-B392. See also: [dec-B356], [dec-B369], [dec-B392].
Record: docs/plans/small-rulings-1008-engine.md, leaf-prior-spelling-keeps-spread. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B393]

**The probit rescaling move's switch is probitRescaleForest, for now**
dec-B371's scale move for k under probit (docs/plans/probit-k-scale-move.md) is taken by default wherever it applies (probit, one forest, constant leaves, k drawn) and the design proposed a dbartsControl switch to turn it off, treeScale = c("auto", "never"). The maintainer: "treeScale doesn't describe what the parameter does. And auto / never aren't entirely clear." Shown, with the move explained, a logical switch under rescaleForest (recommended), probitRescale, fitScaleMove or parameterExpansion, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "Call it `probitRescaleForest` for now. What does the feature cost in runtime? If it's basically always beneficial we can consider making it the only option." The rule: the switch is a logical dbartsControl(probitRescaleForest = TRUE), FALSE turning the move off; whether a switch exists at all is revisited with the move's measured cost. Not built. See also: [dec-B371].
Record: docs/plans/probit-k-scale-move.md, Open calls. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B394]

**cibart keeps one chain until treatSens rewrites its driver**
dec-B387 has treatSens's null treatment model and cibart run the default chains on threads. Building it on treatSens's dbarts-1.0 branch found that cibart cannot: its compiled driver is a Gibbs loop that runs the BART sampler one draw at a time and redraws the confounder given each draw, with buffers sized for one chain's fit (src/sensitivityAnalysis.cpp, src/bartTreatmentModel.cpp), so more chains would overrun them. Shown keeping cibart at one chain, as in the 0.9-x releases, with the null model alone gaining chains (recommended), against running the whole driver once per chain in parallel with its own confounder state, a rewrite of the driver, the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "treatSens isn't on CRAN and it's on its schedule, but yes, probably post 1.0". The rule: cibart keeps n.chains = 1 on the dbarts-1.0 branch; independent parallel chains for cibart are treatSens's work after dbarts 1.0-0, on its own schedule. Not built. Revises dec-B387 in part. See also: [dec-B387], [dec-A157].
Record: root TODO, treatsens-cibart-chains; treatSens dbarts-1.0 branch, 4a4e1e3. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B395]

**copy() takes the sampler's current state when none is stored**
copy() installs the sampler's stored state, so a sampler made with dbartsControl(updateState = FALSE), the setting the help recommends inside a larger Gibbs loop, has none stored and its copy starts from fresh trees. Run on 2026-10-08 on the branch: a seeded sampler run 50 sweeps, copied, and both run 20 more differed by up to 2.97 under updateState = FALSE and by 1.4e-14 under TRUE; 0.9-34 gave 2.26 and 1.9e-14. Shown keeping that behaviour, documented, against copy() storing the sampler's current state at the moment of copying when none is stored (recommended: one store per copy, nothing per sweep), the maintainer on 2026-10-08: "copy() saves the sampler's current state at the moment of copying when none is saved." The rule: copy() of a sampler with no stored state first stores its current state, so every copy continues its source whatever updateState is. Copying such a sampler draws differently than before. Not built. See also: [dec-B384].
Record: R/dbarts.R copy() and man/dbartsSampler-class.Rd, landed at 2985ff24. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-08. [dec-B396]

**The probit rescaling move has no switch**
dec-B394 named the move's switch probitRescaleForest for now and left open whether a switch should exist, pending the move's measured cost. Built with the forest's cached fits scaled in place and re-derived from the leaves only when the accumulated factor leaves [1/2, 2], the move costs 0 to 3 percent of a sweep on an idle four-core x86 box (step on over off, same build, data and chain state, n 500 to 20000, 75 and 200 trees), where re-deriving every sweep cost 9 to 14. Its benefit on the probit-k arm's 100 datasets: effective draws of k per second about ninefold at the median, the 99th percentile of k's autocorrelation time from about 23,000 sweeps to about 800, worse than today on 4. Shown no switch (recommended: 1.0-0 already changes draws throughout, the move is exact and nearly free, and a shipped switch is hard to remove) against keeping probitRescaleForest = TRUE, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Use Option A. No switch." The rule: the move runs wherever it applies (probit, one forest, constant leaves, k drawn), with no R-level control; the engine keeps an internal toggle for its own tests. Not built. Revises dec-B394. See also: [dec-B394], [dec-B371].
Record: docs/plans/probit-k-scale-move.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B397]

**A constraint a state's tree breaks, under a forced setState: repaired**
A stored state can hold a tree the receiving sampler does not allow: a split on a column the forest may not use, a split breaking an interactions() limit, or a monotone tree out of its cone, from another sampler or from a constraint changed after the store. Each is an error today in both forms. Shown keeping the error (about 20 lines; force then cannot force) against repairing (about 350 lines, mostly the collapse routine warm-start-salvage needs anyway: unforced returns FALSE with the sampler untouched; forced collapses the first offending split on each path from the root into one leaf at the weighted mean of the leaves beneath it and reseeds an out-of-cone monotone tree, as a warm start does), recommended repair from dec-B305's "'cram' a state in there and patch up whatever we could" and dec-B295, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Repair, as we do elsewhere." Not built. See also: [dec-B305], [dec-B295].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, H1. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B398]

**setData keeps which columns can be missing**
dec-B321 makes a column missable from its first missing value, at creation or by setPredictor, for the sampler's life ("We assume missingness isn't possible until we have evidence that it is, and then ever-after we operate under that evidence"); it does not name setData. Under dec-B378 a categorical rule sending every level one way is malformed on a column never missing. Run by the setState plan's critique: a factor with missing values, 200 sweeps, 11 of 96 factor rules in the kept draws one-way; after setData to complete data, setState of the stored state merges them and returns FALSE on the tip. Shown setData resetting the flags from the new data (about 40 lines; such rules then error in both forms on a setState, copy or reload of an earlier stored state, at every use) against keeping them sticky (about 120 lines; old flags carried, a column setData first makes missing draws its directions as setPredictor does, no new error), recommended sticky, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "setData keeps the flags (sticky)." The rule: setData never lowers a column's missing-value flag; a column it first makes missable draws each existing split's direction with probability one half. The alternative was resetting the flags. Not built. Extends dec-B321 to setData. See also: [dec-B321], [dec-B378].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, H2. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B399]

**Kept draws recorded before a column's first missing value draw its directions too**
Under dec-B321 the first missing value in a column draws each live split's direction with probability one half; the sampler's kept draws from earlier sweeps keep every split on the column sending missing values left, and predict uses every kept draw. Run by the setState plan's critique: 20 kept draws, a first missing value, 5 more; 75 percent of a missing row's posterior mean came from the fixed left. Reached by a predict between the setPredictor that brings the first missing value and the next run. A kept draw is a posterior draw given that time's data, under which no row reached any direction, so each direction's conditional in that draw was its prior, one half, whatever the response then or since; drawing it now completes the snapshot exactly. Asked whether a changed response matters ("Kept trees are snapshots at points in time"): it does not, since no likelihood term in the snapshot involves a direction. Shown leaving them left and documenting it (about 20 lines), drawing them at one half (about 100 lines, recommended), and predict dropping the older draws for such rows (about 250 lines, a ragged predict), the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "OK, you can use your recommendation but we should make sure what happens is clear in the documentation." The rule: when a column first becomes missable, each chain draws, with probability one half, the direction of every split on the column in its kept draws as in its live trees, and a refused update undoes it with the rest; the help says so where it describes how a column becomes missable and what predict does with such a row. The alternatives were the fixed left, documented, and dropping the older draws. Not built. Extends dec-B321. See also: [dec-B321], [dec-B322].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, H3. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B400]

**A state's k transfers literally across a change of prior**
A drawn k acts through the leaf sd, k.scale / k. A state records k, not the k.scale it was drawn against, so a state restored after a setLeafPrior that changed the prior's scale (dec-B393), or taken from a sampler with another prior, installs its k against the recipient's k.scale and the sd changes by the ratio of the two scales. Shown keeping k and documenting the change (about 15 lines) against the state carrying the current sd so a restore puts it back (about 90 lines, recommended), the maintainer on 2026-10-09, asked what "in force" meant: "The k itself should literally transfer - a parameter is a parameter, regardless of the prior. It may be a bad fit, but so be it." The rule: setState, a copy and a reload install a state's k as recorded whatever prior the recipient holds; the help says the sd then follows the recipient's k.scale. dec-B384's re-expression across response units is not named here and stands. The alternative was a state block carrying the current sd. Not built. See also: [dec-B393], [dec-B384].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, H4. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B401]

**stan4bart's restore of a saved fit forces its state**
stan4bart reloads a saved fit by rebuilding a dbarts sampler from the fit's control, model and data and installing the saved state, the operation dbarts's own reload performs, which dec-B305 forces ("copy() and a reload force, having no choice"). Shown forcing (one line; anything not clean repaired silently, the clean check's cost of about one sweep not paid) against stopping when the unforced form returns FALSE (three lines; such a fit could not predict after a reload, and every restore pays the check), recommended forcing, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Force." The rule: stan4bart's restoreBartSampler calls setState with forceUpdate = TRUE. The alternative was stopping on FALSE. Not built. See also: [dec-B305].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, H5; stan4bart bartcore branch. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B402]

**A sparse x takes the linear-model starting sigma, with no warning**
The starting sigma anchors the residual variance prior from a linear fit of y on x. For a caller's sparse x (a dgCMatrix, new in 1.0: 0.9-34 refused it, run 2026-10-09) dbarts used sd(y) and warned on every fit, while an indicator expansion R built sparse already took the linear-model estimate whichever the storage (dec-B370). Run on 2026-10-09 with true sigma 0.5: the sparse fit (Matrix's sparse QR, columns at unit norm, at most n at a time, one dependent column dropped per refactor) matches lm exactly (0.568 and 0.499 at n 200 and 2000, 0.01 s) where sd(y) gave 0.688 and 1.048; at n 20000, p 2000 it gives 0.498 in 65 s; n 100000, p 5000 ran past 20 minutes; with more columns than rows it falls back to sd(y), as BART does silently. Shown keeping the fallback and warning, the sparse fit with no warning (recommended, storage not changing the prior), and sd(y) silently, the maintainer asked how the fit is computed (Matrix's sparse QR; a sparse crossproduct with a pivoted Cholesky estimated at seconds for 2000 columns, not run) and on 2026-10-09: "Yes, just use the sparse regression, no warning." The rule: a sparse x takes the same linear-model starting sigma as its dense equivalent, with no warning; a caller can pass sigest; the help says so. How the fit is computed is an implementation call to be measured. Draws change for sparse fits that did not pass sigest. The alternatives were the fallback with its warning and sd(y) silently. Not built. Extends dec-B370. See also: [dec-B370].
Record: root TODO, sparse-starting-sigma. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B403]

**Logistic k mixing: the collapsed scale move, after a study of whether k needs a prior**
Measured on 2026-10-09 (docs/design/k-mixing-pg-families.md), k under logistic mixes slowly where it did under probit, at small true k and near separation, and under negative binomial it does not where it could be measured; both samplers hold from a start at the truth. The probit rescaling step does not carry over, logistic's latents being precisions. Shown leaving logistic as is for 1.0, building a collapsed scale move before 1.0-0 (leaves times a factor and k divided by it, accepted on the logistic likelihood with the latents integrated out, one pass over the rows a sweep, covering negative binomial too, measured first with a stop if it does not help; recommended, after the install surface), and drawing no k by default for logistic, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "We can use your recommendation, but I first want a study done to see if k even needs to have a prior, or if a fixed k is good enough." and, of the binary hyperprior study's rerun on converged chains (dec-B390) brought forward for it, "You can use the bench box." The rule: before the collapsed move is planned, the binary hyperprior study is rerun with the probit rescaling step in place, on probit and logistic, fixed k against drawn k, and its result brought to the maintainer; the move is built before 1.0-0 if a drawn k is kept. Brings dec-B390's rerun ahead of the merge. Not built. See also: [dec-B390], [dec-B371], [dec-A07].
Record: root TODO, k-mixing-pg-families and binary-k-default-revisit. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B404]

**A kept response scale is a status from the engine, passed through the C API**
dec-B364 keeps the response scale when setResponse or setOffset asks to re-derive it from rows in that hold fewer than two distinct values, and warns once per sampler. stan4bart calls the flat dbarts_sampler_setOffset with updateScale true every warm-up step; the bridge could not tell a kept scale from one re-derived to the same values. Shown a C-layer warning through one new engine read (recommended first), a silent keep on the flat entries, and a status passed through, the maintainer asked who warns and whether outcomes were to be statuses: the engine never warns, reporting errors as C++ exceptions the bridge converts and outcomes that are not errors as typed results (PredictorUpdateResult), so the keep is a status the engine's setResponse and setOffset return; the C API already raises R errors for wrong calls, with values, capability statuses and a rollback flag as returns. Shown the C API warning itself (no header change) against passing the status through as a new return value (a header change; a caller testing for exactly 1 would misread it; stan4bart deciding what its users see), recommended passing it through, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "OK, pass it through I think." The rule: the engine returns the keep as a status; the R interface warns once per sampler; dbarts_sampler_setResponse and dbarts_sampler_setOffset return a distinct value for a swap that kept the scale, documented in dbarts.h with a minor version bump, and do not warn; stan4bart's branch reads it. The alternatives were a warning from the C layer and a silent keep. Not built. Extends dec-B364. See also: [dec-B364].
Record: docs/plans/response-scale-rows.md, the held question. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B405]

**One cut point per gap, weighted by the gap's width, planned and sized first**
dec-B313 gave a column with fewer distinct values than n.cuts one cut point per gap under the default rule, so that no proposal moves among points that split the same rows. Its stated cost was that a gap's chance of being split no longer follows its width. The plan's blind critique ran a prototype on 2026-10-09: the equivalence baselines that move most are zero-inflated sparse columns (up to |z| 32) and continuous columns on about 95 rows, 0/1 columns at most 2.5; on a column 88% zeros the zero/nonzero gap's share of the split prior falls from about a third to 1 over the distinct values, and with three such columns and a presence effect test RMSE was 5.5% worse (+0.0145, se 0.0039); continuous columns on 30 to 60 rows lose 2.5 to 3%, none by 90. Shown the rule as ruled, one point per gap only up to about 8 distinct values (bcf's cutoff), and one point per gap with each gap weighted by its width (no proposal wasted, the split prior essentially today's; weighted cut choice in the engine, TODO cut-point-weights's machinery; not sized), the third recommended with planning first and the cutoff as fallback, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "We can do width-weight gaps, but yes, I'd want it planend and sized first." The rule: under the default rule a column's grid holds one point per gap between distinct values where it has fewer than n.cuts of them, each gap chosen with probability in proportion to its width, in the prior and the proposal; planned and sized before it is built, and brought back if too large for 1.0. Revises dec-B313's equal-gap reading. Not built. See also: [dec-B313], [dec-A178].
Record: root TODO, default-rule-per-gap; docs/plans/default-rule-per-gap.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B406]

**A state moved across a change of response scale keeps its leaf sd, as dec-B384 has it**
dec-B401 transfers a state's k literally across a change of prior ("a parameter is a parameter, regardless of the prior"), which could be read to cover dec-B384, where a state goes into a sampler whose response has another spread: the leaves are converted so the fitted function is the same in the original units, and k is re-expressed so the leaf sd is too. Run by the setState plan: into a sampler with three times the response spread, k.scale 7.83 to 23.48; k re-expressed keeps the leaf sd at 2.47, k literal makes it 7.40 against leaves still about 2.47. Shown keeping dec-B384 for a scale change (recommended: the model is unchanged and re-expressing k converts the leaves' sd as the leaves are converted) against k literal there too, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Keep it for scale changes. If it helps at all, I guess we can think of `k` as just the internal representation and the parameter as the sd. This whole thing has been and continues to be a mess." The rule: dec-B384 stands for a change of response scale; dec-B401 covers a change of prior. Confirms dec-B384; the framing of the sd as the parameter is the maintainer's aside and does not by itself revise dec-B401. See also: [dec-B384], [dec-B401].
Record: docs/plans/setstate-force-update.md, B5. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B407]

**A hazard fit's quantile periods and its no-subject case follow the rows in the likelihood**
dec-B315 puts a hazard fit's periods in dec-B296's class, taken from the subjects in the likelihood at creation, and dec-B302 creates a sampler with no row in from every row, with a warning. The revised plan for response-scale-rows applied both to two cases they do not name: under breaks = K the quantiles are read from the times of the subjects in the likelihood (stated boundaries as given), and a hazard fit with no subject in takes its periods from every subject with dec-B302's warning. Run on 2026-10-09: with two subjects at weight 0 holding unusual times, breaks = 3 gives boundaries 3, 4 and 20 today and 3, 4 and 5 under the call, as with the two dropped. Asked whether these are reasonable for a hazard model: a weight of 0 is how a caller leaves a subject out without reshaping the data (cross-validation folds, which xbart builds this way, a bootstrap weight of zero, an empty mixture component), so a left-out subject's time shaping the periods would leak a held-out fold into the fit; coxph and survreg refuse a zero weight, the BART package's survival fit takes none, and survfit keeps such a time only on its display axis. Shown both calls, recommended, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "OK, both are confirmed for the hazard question." The rule: under breaks = K a hazard fit's quantiles come from the subjects in the likelihood; with none in, its periods come from every subject, with dec-B302's warning. The alternative was treating the hazard fit as an exception. Not built. See also: [dec-B315], [dec-B302], [dec-B296].
Record: docs/plans/response-scale-rows.md, Calls made in planning. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B408]

**Width-weighted gaps go ahead, as sized**
dec-B406 asked for width-weighted gaps to be planned and sized first. The replan sized it at about 1000 lines (1500 to 2000 at the usual overrun), two to three implementer days, one to two of review and about eight hours of gate machine time, one more engine slice before 1.0-0 after the gp slice. On its prototype width weights removed the equal-gap losses (zero-inflated columns +0.0006, se 0.0018, against +0.0145; small-n continuous within noise), a new uneven-gap balance arm passed against the exact posterior under every move mixture and failed the equal-gap answer at |z| up to 162, speed was within 5% on a loaded machine with a quiet comparison owed at 1.03, 8 of 55 equivalence scenarios and three exact gates' draws move and no snapshot file, and the weighted cut choice is the machinery TODO cut-point-weights reuses. Shown building it (recommended) against bcf's cutoff (one point per gap, unweighted, up to about 8 distinct values; 750 to 1000 lines; uneven few-valued columns change their prior; zero-inflated and small-n columns keep their wasted proposals; a threshold where 8 and 9 values differ), asked whether that cutoff is reasonable (for evenly spaced few-valued columns it matches today's prior; not for dbarts's sparse and zero-inflated inputs), the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Build the width-weighted rule." Confirms dec-B406. Not built. See also: [dec-B406], [dec-B313].
Record: docs/plans/default-rule-per-gap.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B409]

**Two distinct values with no double between them take a cut on the lower one**
Cut points sit at midpoints between neighbouring distinct values, and the quantile grid's design says a cut is "a midpoint, never an observed value, so no row sits on one". Where two distinct values are adjacent doubles the computed midpoint rounds onto one of them, so the quantile rule leaves that gap without a cut and the even-spacing rule places cuts that separate nothing; only values differing in the sixteenth significant digit reach it, no recorded baseline and a few unit tests. Shown placing the cut on the lower value in the one fill both rules use (a split sends x <= cut left, so the gap becomes splittable and no row sits ambiguously; about 10 lines; recommended) against leaving such gaps unsplittable, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "For 3b, place the cut on the lower value." The rule: where the midpoint of two neighbouring distinct values is not strictly below the upper one, the cut is the lower value, under the default and quantile rules alike. Not built. See also: [dec-B406], [dec-B409].
Record: docs/plans/default-rule-per-gap.md, Open calls. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B410]

**bartBT follows the width-weighted default rule**
bartBT keeps 0.9-34's and BayesTree's argument names and defaults; when the quantile grid was fixed it followed ("Fix it everywhere, bartBT included."). Under width-weighted gaps (dec-B406, dec-B409) a gap's chance of being split follows its width as BayesTree's 100 evenly spaced points do, to within one point's rounding a gap; what differs is that moves among points splitting the same rows are no longer proposed, changing draws, mixing and run time but not the model. Shown bartBT following (no extra code; recommended) against keeping BayesTree's grid in bartBT (about 60 lines, a rule flag on the data object, two default grids in the package), the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "bartBT follows." The rule: bartBT takes the same default grid as every other entry point. Not built. See also: [dec-B406], [dec-B409].
Record: docs/plans/default-rule-per-gap.md, Open calls. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B411]

**A prior predictive study of the negative binomial default before 1.0-0**
A negative binomial fit draws each row's log mean from the forest under the same drawn-k default as binary fits. Simulating from the default prior to measure k's mixing (docs/design/k-mixing-pg-families.md), about 15% of datasets on 150 rows had counts summing past 10,000, some past 10^9 and a few past 10^24, some overflowing rnbinom; the latent draw's cost grows with the counts. Calibration shows only that the sampler matches its prior, not that the prior is sensible for counts, and a default is hard to change after release. Shown a prior predictive study before 1.0-0 (counts drawn from the default prior over a range of n and centres, the tail against what count-model users expect, candidate defaults brought back with numbers if it is badly off; a few hours on the bench box, no code change; recommended) against leaving it, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Run the study before 1.0-0." The rule: before 1.0-0 the negative binomial default prior's predictive counts are studied and any change to the default is brought to the maintainer. Not built. See also: [dec-B404].
Record: root TODO, nbinom-prior-predictive. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B412]

**sigest beside a fixed residual scale: accepted with a message where they agree, until 1.1-0**
dec-B123 refuses sigest (or the sampler constructor's 0.9-34 spelling, sigma =) beside a fixed residual scale, which ignores it, while a residual prior written two ways is "accepted where they agree". Two uses of dbarts found outside CRAN (a negative-binomial loop and a Tobit BART loop) create their sampler with dbarts(..., resid.prior = fixed(1), sigma = 1): the two agree (the square root of the fixed variance is 1), 0.9-34 ran it, and 1.0 stops at creation with "'sigma' has no effect under a fixed residual scale" (run by the leaf scale note's fact-check, 2026-10-09). Shown keeping the error, accepting where they agree and refusing where they disagree (recommended, dec-B123's rule for duplicate spellings; about 10 lines), and warning without refusing for this release, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Hmm, accept when they agree, refuse when they disagree, give a message and schedule it for error again at 1.1-0." The rule: in 1.0, sigest beside a fixed residual scale is accepted with a message when it equals the square root of the fixed variance, saying it has no effect and will be an error from 1.1-0, and refused when it differs; at 1.1-0 it is refused whatever its value, through the tombstone registry. The alternatives were the error as built and a warning without refusal. Built (60397152, b334fa89). Its review found 0.9-34's sampler constructor took sigma as the fixed residual scale and ignored the value given to fixed, so a differing call that 0.9-34 ran at sigma is now refused; NEWS says so. Revises that clause of dec-B123 for this release. See also: [dec-B123], [dec-A193].
Record: root TODO, sigest-beside-fixed-agree. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B413]

**k is the internal parameter and k.scale never changes internally: to be planned as one rule**
docs/design/leaf-scale-rules.md set out where the leaf-scale rulings keep k and where the sd, the two parting wherever an operation moves k.scale, which today an sd spelling redefines as twice the named sd. The maintainer, asked whether the note should add that a continuous response is rescaled internally to [-0.5, 0.5]: "What I'm getting at is, what if we kept k.scale fixed and didn't redefine it, but translated sds and the scale of prior on sds so that they had the intended value and distribution?" and "But does k.scale change when the response changes? 2 is 2, right? The response is still mapped to -0.5 to 0.5. The parameter itself of course lags, but that's to be expected." The reading put back: on the internal scale k.scale is constant; k is the chain's parameter there; only an operation that rescales the leaves rescales k (a state converted into a sampler on another response, dec-B384 and dec-B407); a re-anchor leaves the internal leaves and k as they were, both lagging; a change of prior touches neither, so keeping k is keeping the sd and dec-B401 and dec-B393 agree; an sd spelling is translated at the interface (sd = s to k = k.scale / s, invchi(df, s) to chi(df, k.scale / s), the reciprocal families the help already states) and a named sd re-translated at each re-anchor so it keeps its value in response units (dec-A105). The redefinition of k.scale by spelling goes. Offered a plan of what each ruling becomes, what is built and what is to build, and its size, for one ruling on the whole, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Sure." The rule for now: plan it; nothing changes until the plan is ruled. See also: [dec-B401], [dec-B393], [dec-B384], [dec-B407], [dec-A105], [dec-B201].
Record: docs/design/leaf-scale-rules.md; the plan to follow. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B414]

**A survey of samplers for the remaining slow tail of k and the fitted values under probit**
With the probit rescaling step, k's autocorrelation time on the calibration census fell from a median of 524 sweeps to 71 and at the 99th percentile from about 23,000 to about 800, the fitted values' 99th percentile sitting near 700: the slowest tenth of those small, near-separated datasets still needs several hundred sweeps per independent draw. Interweaving, parameter expansion (built) and collapsing the latents out of k's update were measured for the probit step. Asked "What about other sampling procedures, specifically designed for these kinds of problems?", the orchestrator named four not yet tried: updating k with every leaf integrated out jointly given the tree structures (blocking), drawing all leaves jointly across trees, calibrated data augmentation (Duan, Johndrow and Dunson 2018) for probit and logistic near separation, and gradient-based updates of the leaves and log k with the latents integrated out; recommended a survey with prototypes of the first three on the same census against what is built, on the bench box after the fixed-versus-drawn k study. The maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Do the survey. Note that we use walnutpie in other bart-ecosystem packages, so we could also vendor it here." The rule: a survey of samplers for the slow tail of k and of the fitted values under probit (and logistic where they apply), with prototypes measured on the census for tail, cost per sweep and exactness, the gradient-based option weighed with Walnutpie (the WALNUTS sampler stan4bart vendors) as an available implementation. Tree-structure stickiness stays with the tree-mixing research after the merge. Not built. See also: [dec-B371], [dec-B404], [dec-B390].
Record: the survey to follow. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B415]

**k is k, the data scale is fixed, and sd is syntactic sugar: the plan approved as a whole**
The plan for dec-B414 (docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md, after a blind critique and revision) makes the scale k is measured against always the data's (the response mapped to [-0.5, 0.5]; 3 for probit, pi sqrt(3) for logistic), never redefined by a prior's spelling: sd = s is translated to k = k.scale / s and invchi(df, s) to chi(df, k.scale / s) wherever a prior comes in (creation, setLeafPrior, setModel, xbart's grid) and re-translated when the response's mapping is re-derived, so keeping k is keeping the spread wherever a prior changes. A prior written with k draws bit for bit as today, apart from installs onto a response of another spread; one written with an sd keeps its model and posterior (matched to 4e-13 on every family and leaf model the critique ran) while fit$k and getK report its real k (3.7 for sd = 0.5 on a data scale of 1.85, where today the spelling's own scale of twice the sd pins k at 2); setModel keeps the spread as setLeafPrior does; a state restored after a change of prior keeps its spread; stan4bart's warm-up re-anchors stop stretching a named sd, a posterior change that is a fix. It reverses dec-B201's definition of k.scale under the sd spelling with dec-A124, and the register's clause of dec-B393 that k.scale is twice the named sd; the maintainer's words in both stand. The maintainer, shown the plan: "That doesn't seem right to me. Shouldn't `k` be `k`, the data scale be fixed, and `sd` syntactic sugar?"; told that is the plan and that what changes is the k reported for an sd-written fit, asked "So why would `fit$k` and `getK` repotr different numbers?" (they agree with each other; both report the real k in place of 2), then on 2026-10-09: "Ah. OK, proceed." The rule: the plan as a whole; its open calls 2 to 4 are ruled separately. Revises dec-B201 and dec-A124 in part. Not built. See also: [dec-B414], [dec-B401], [dec-B393], [dec-B201].
Record: docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B416]

**A drawn sd lags at a re-anchor, as k does**
Under dec-B416 the prior on a named sd is re-translated when the response's mapping is re-derived (setResponse or setOffset with updateScale = TRUE, setData), so it keeps its meaning in response units; open was the chain's current value of a drawn sd at that moment. The leaves are held on the internal scale and stretch with the response; a drawn k stays put and lags. Shown the current sd lagging with k (one rule for k and sd; a drawn residual sd meanwhile stays put in response units, 0.3933 before and after setResponse(3 y, updateScale = TRUE), since the residuals are not stretched; recommended) against holding its value in response units (as built, dec-A105; matches sigma; a drawn k and a drawn sd then differ at a re-anchor and the sd no longer matches the leaves it governs), the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "It lags, like k. It too is just sugar over the actual parameter." The rule: at a re-anchor a drawn leaf sd's current value is k's, which stays, so it stretches with the leaves until the next draw; the named prior is re-translated. Reverses dec-A105 for a drawn sd's current value at a re-anchor; the named sd itself stays in response units. Not built. See also: [dec-B416], [dec-A105], [dec-B414].
Record: docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md, open call 2. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B417]

**One rule for every install: the chain's parameters go in as stored on the internal scale, never converted**
Open call 3 of the k-internal plan asked whether a warm start onto a response of another spread converts k with the leaves. The maintainer: "Well, let's take a step back for warm starts. Ignore any rulings or past decisions. The goal of a warm start is to take tree structure (for the most part), and I guess `k` can ride along for free. Those are both stored in a scale-free way. I wouldn't expect the parameters themselves to produce good fits, but at the next draw I would hope that they move to where they should be. If I fit against a dataset at one scale and then transfered to a new dataset with the same mean structure but different scale, the next sample drawn from a warm-starting should be "burnt-in"." Restated as carrying structure, leaves, k and sigma on the internal scale with no conversion (a pure rescale of the response then a no-op), and asked what would differ for setState, copy and reload: nothing where the response's mapping is the same at both ends (bitwise either way); where it changed (a restore after a re-anchor, a state moved into another sampler) dec-B200 converts the leaves into the new mapping (to rounding; Gaussian-process leaves and amplitude forests refused) and dec-B384 would convert k. The maintainer: "Does restoring across a re-anchor even make sense? The mental model of the sampler is a linear one through time, with each sample being a snapshot." A careful rollback restores the response with the state, a copy or reload shares the mapping, and moving a state to other data is a transfer, so the conversion serves no use the linear model calls for; any parameter values are a position the chain could hold, so such an install is clean. Shown one rule for every install (recommended), the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "OK, let's do it." The rule: a warm start, setState, copy() and a reload install the chain's parameters (tree structure, leaves, k, sigma and the rest) as stored on the internal scale, against the recipient's response mapping, and never convert them; the next draws move them where the data say; a restore across a change of mapping is legal and has no special meaning, which the help says. Reverses dec-B200's conversion of a state into another transform, dec-B384 and dec-B407; settles the k-internal plan's open call 3; the refusal of a state in other units for Gaussian-process leaves and amplitude forests goes. Not built. See also: [dec-B200], [dec-B384], [dec-B407], [dec-B343], [dec-B416], [dec-B417].
Record: docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md, open call 3. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B418]

**stan4bart restores one sampler per chain; per-chain responses and offsets to be designed**
dec-B418 rested on a copy or reload sharing the response mapping. Revising the k-internal plan found stan4bart does not: each chain re-derives its mapping during its own warm-up, and a restore installs every chain into one sampler rebuilt from the data. Converting, today's restore matches the original fit to 6.7e-16; emulated without conversion, a restored continuous fit was off by up to 0.97 against a fit spread of 0.70 sd. Shown stan4bart restoring one sampler per chain at that chain's mapping (about 120 lines there, exact under dec-B418; recommended), keeping the conversion for installs across a mapping, a sampler holding one mapping per chain, and stan4bart no longer re-deriving per chain, the maintainer asked: "stan4bart raises an interesting question - should offsets or responses be held per-chain (and thus response scales) be held per-chain? That would allow a single sampler to hold all of the information." Every chain today shares one response, offset and transform. Proposed: per-chain responses and offsets, one shared scale per sampler. The maintainer: "But how would stan4bart work? In the separate sampler model, each draw of the latents shifts each chain's response scale. They all freeze after burn-in, but they freeze at different values. If you were to require the response scales sync to the same values, you'd have to have the threads block and communicate with each other, right?" Three ways were laid out (per-chain scales, as today, chains targeting slightly different priors; one barrier at the end of warm-up agreeing one scale; the scale fixed at creation from the response less an initial parametric fit), with a measurement of what warm-up re-derivation buys. The maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Sure, go ahead." The rule: stan4bart restores one sampler per chain, landing in lockstep with the k-internal slice; a design note covers per-chain responses and offsets in one sampler and the three scale options with that measurement, for the maintainer to schedule. Keeps dec-B418. Not built. See also: [dec-B418], [dec-B200], [dec-B402].
Record: docs/plans/k-internal-parameter.md, held question; root TODO, per-chain-responses. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B419]

**Binary fits keep a drawn k under chi(1.5, 2)**
dec-B404 asked whether a fixed k would serve binary fits as well as a drawn one, since a drawn k mixes slowly under logistic and, near separation, under probit. The study (docs/design/binary-k-fixed-or-drawn.md) reran September's binary hyperprior study with the probit rescaling step in place, on probit and logistic: fixed k at 1, 1.5, 2 and 3 against five drawn priors, 108 simulated cells and 22 real datasets, at two chain lengths up to 64,000 draws. The best fixed value, k = 1, missed four of the six bars in both links: coverage 5 to 7 points worse, a worst case eight times the default's, and losses on real data in both directions (tictactoe wants a small k, haberman and birthwt one near 2.8); its loss sits in rows with true probability near 0 or 1, and its real log-score failure rests on tictactoe. No drawn prior improved coverage by the two points September's rule requires. Under logistic k did not converge at any length tried, though the predicted probabilities and log score did; the probit step did not converge k at n = 500 and 75 trees either. Shown keeping the drawn k under chi(1.5, 2) for both links (recommended; no change to code or draws, and dec-B404's logistic collapsed move is then built before 1.0-0) against fixed k = 1, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Keep k modeled and its current prior." The rule: binary fits, probit and logistic, keep drawing k under chi(1.5, 2) by default; dec-B404's condition is met, so the logistic collapsed scale move is built before 1.0-0 (docs/plans/collapsed-scale-move.md, its other open calls still the maintainer's). Closes dec-B390's revisit. See also: [dec-B404], [dec-B390], [dec-B415], [dec-A07].
Record: docs/design/binary-k-fixed-or-drawn.md; root TODO, k-mixing-pg-families. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B420]

**A sparse x's starting sigma may differ from lm's in a narrow rank band**
dec-B403 gives a caller's sparse x the dense equivalent's starting sigma. The plan (docs/plans/sparse-starting-sigma.md) takes it from a pivoted Cholesky of the smaller crossproduct, which agrees with dense lm.fit to 1e-11 on 400 random designs and runs 10 to 1000 times faster than the sparse QR it replaces. A crossproduct cannot tell a nearly dependent column from an exactly dependent one at lm's tolerance (an exact dependency's pivot reaches 1.5e-13 at p 5000, above lm's 1e-14), so the plan's rank tolerance is 1e-10. Two near-copies differing by 1e-7 to 1e-5 of their norm both stay in lm's fit and one is dropped here, sigma differing by 0.01 to 4.7 percent and always equal to the dense fit without one copy; a dense column whose spread is below 1e-7 of its mean is dropped by lm as constant and kept here, 14 percent apart where it carries signal. Shown accepting that band (recommended; pinned by tests at its edges) against a QR fallback where the rank is ambiguous, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "Band is fine." The rule: the sparse starting sigma uses the plan's tolerance and may differ from lm's in that band. Not built. See also: [dec-B403].
Record: docs/plans/sparse-starting-sigma.md, open call 1. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B421]

**The starting-sigma regression expands factors into indicators**
Planning sparse-starting-sigma found that 1.0's default factors = "categorical", which keeps an unordered factor as one categorical predictor for the trees, also feeds the factor to the starting-sigma linear regression as its level codes. Confirmed on the build: a 5-level factor and one numeric column (n 300, noise sd 0.5) start sigma at 1.41 under the default, 0.53 under factors = "indicators" and in 0.9-34 (which always expanded), against sd(y) 1.51; every default fit with a factor predictor and no sigest gets a residual prior calibrated off a nearly marginal estimate. Shown expanding factors into indicators for the starting-sigma regression only (recommended; about 15 lines in the sparse-starting-sigma slice, which rewrites the same function, high-cardinality factors taking its sparse path) against keeping the codes, the maintainer on 2026-10-09: "What would keeping the codes mean? Treating them as integers? That doesn't make sense. I think indicators are necessary, right?" The rule: every factor, unordered or ordered, enters the starting-sigma regression as indicator columns (for an ordered factor the same span as lm's polynomial contrasts); the trees' representation is unchanged. Draws change for default fits with a factor predictor and no sigest; the slice re-records the scenarios that move. Not built. See also: [dec-B403], [dec-B421].
Record: docs/plans/sparse-starting-sigma.md, the factor open call. Marked: ruled by the maintainer, 2026-10-09. [dec-B422]

## C. Agent-made decisions with no identified cost

**C entry points register under full names**
The compiled entry points are registered under their full symbol names rather than the unprefixed method names the released package used. No alternative was weighed. Nothing is visible to an R user; the naming is what makes an old compiled consumer fail at lookup instead of resolving a same-named symbol with a different signature. Not yet ruled on.
Record: docs/design/public-surface.md, which calls handle-prefixed names defensible. Marked: not mine. [dec-C01]

**The compiled interface is checked at build**
The package asserts its own side of the compiled interface when it builds: a signature token, a recomputed hash, and the offsets, sizes and alignments of the three structs the interface exposes. No alternative was weighed. Nothing is visible to a user; drift inside the package is caught at compile time. Not yet ruled on.
Record: code only, in the flat C interface implementation. Marked: not mine. [dec-C02]

**Internal call registrations get a new prefix**
The registrations R uses to reach compiled code are renamed to an engine-specific prefix, 58 of 65, with seven utility entries keeping the plain dbarts prefix and the dynamic-library line unchanged. No alternative was weighed. Nothing is visible to a user, and no R-visible name changes. Not yet ruled on.
Record: code only, in the registration table. Marked: not mine. [dec-C03]

**The support library no longer needs R**
The support library is host-agnostic: it carries no standard input-output and no R symbol, its printing declared as function pointers the host installs. No alternative was weighed when this was done; run-time hooks against a choice made when the package is built were weighed later, under dec-A74. Nothing is visible to a user. Nothing calls those pointers, and after the merge they are removed, printing and math for the whole engine being chosen when the package is built. See also: [dec-A74], [dec-B85].
Record: code only, in the support library's input-output unit. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: covered by earlier rulings dec-A74; its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-C04]

**The grown warm start stays opt-in**
A sampler starts from a forest drawn from the prior, and the warm start that grows trees from the root first is available through an argument. The alternative, defaulting that warm start on, was measured against a criterion fixed in advance and killed, the measurement showing a cost in noise-heavy and large-sample designs. A user who wants it asks for it by name, and the manual says which families refuse the option. The maintainer was given three options and took this one. See also: [dec-B94].
Record: docs/design/grow-from-root-default.md, whose status records the measured kill. Marked: superseded by dec-B94. [dec-C05]

**Two research switches ship unbuilt**
Two compile-time switches meant for research measurements ship in the source and are defined by no shipped build. No alternative was weighed. Nothing is visible to a user; both say in the code that they are for private builds only. Not yet ruled on.
Record: code only, in the moves header and the C++ test makefile. Marked: not mine. [dec-C06]

**Two developer tools ship unwired**
The stale-install detector and the snapshot regeneration script ship as local developer tools, wired into no workflow. No alternative was weighed. Nothing is visible to a user; both say so in their own headers. Not yet ruled on.
Record: code only, in the tools directory. Marked: not mine. [dec-C07]

**Two header calls: scope and a parameter name**
Two open questions about the header's shape were settled: the first cleanup item covers seven entries rather than the five originally named, and the basis setter's parameter is renamed to say that the data is row-major instead of transposing the contract to match the old name. The alternatives were the narrower five-entry scope and the transposition. A caller who lays the data out the other way still gets no error, only a parameter name that says which way round it goes. A later ruling took both entries this shaped out of the header. No maintainer ruling on the two choices themselves is on record. See also: [dec-B86].
Record: docs/plans/capi-shape.md, which lists both as open decisions for the maintainer and records the outcome with no attribution. Marked: not mine; superseded by dec-B86, which took both entries it shaped out of the header. [dec-B61]

**Engine test accessors split by body size**
Behind the test peer, the engine's test-only accessors with one-line bodies became static functions on the peer and the four with real bodies became private members; the ForTesting suffix is gone, cross-class forwarders live on the peer, the column store's two reads go straight through its existing public test-set block, and the sampler's per-chain navigation stays public. The alternatives were private members everywhere with peer forwarders, or every body moved into the test header. Nothing is visible to a user. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A81].
Record: docs/plans/test-scaffolding-consolidation.md. Marked: internal, nothing visible to a user; outside the 2026-10-08 triage and not put to the maintainer. [dec-C08]

**Constructor hints name only the list the argument reads**
An unfound prior, family or forest constructor gets a hint naming the constructor list the argument being read takes constructors from, the family and prior lists for the family argument, inside which a residual prior is written, from one shared table, and no hint where no list applies; the hint carries no NEWS entry, as the forest hint had none. The alternative was a hint searching every list, which pointed tree.prior = student(3) at the family list. Nothing breaks. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A75].
Record: docs/plans/constructor-vocabulary.md. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B374. [dec-C09]

**xbart's parallel test uses a built-in generator**
xbart's parity across thread counts under a non-default generator is tested with Knuth-TAOCP-2002, no user-supplied generator being loadable portably in a test; the gaussian equivalence baseline of the time, since superseded, was re-recorded for its two xbart scenarios, with a hand-rebuilt cross-validation cell as the oracle, and a test that tree counts in one unit draw on separate streams was added. The alternative was no parity test for non-default kinds. Nothing is visible to a user. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-A90].
Record: benchmarks/baselines/MANIFEST. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: its internal calls closed as internal under dec-B388. [dec-C10]

**k = chi() resolves through a wrapper's dots**
A k = chi(...) that a wrapper forwards to bart through its dots resolves in the prior vocabulary, as when written directly and as the family and prior arguments already did, which makes true for k the help's statement that constructors resolve inside a wrapper's dots. The alternative was narrowing the help to say that k = chi() must be written in the direct call or as dbartsPriors$chi. Nothing breaks; a call that failed with "could not find function" fits, and the same call to 0.9-34's bart2 failed that way too. Not yet ruled on.
Record: commit 0f5ba74b. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B375. [dec-C11]

**Which warnings carry a class at the edges of the ruling**
Applying dec-B161, the agents left sparseFactor's warnings plain, since they repeat base R's factor warnings (an invalid level giving NA, a replacement length that is not a multiple, an operator not meaningful for factors), which base R leaves unclassed; and classed rbart_vi's fallbacks as dbartsFallbackWarning: verbose output dropped under several threads, a multithreaded run falling back to one thread, group.by recycled for a test set without group.by.test, and random effects drawn from their distribution for test groups training did not see. The alternatives were classing sparseFactor's warnings as fallbacks and leaving rbart_vi's plain. Nothing breaks. Not yet ruled on. See also: [dec-B161].
Record: commits 120644f4 and 6a7067b6. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B382; covered by earlier rulings dec-B161. [dec-C12]

**Argument forwarding reads the formals of the function the call matched**
redirectCall, which re-targets a front door's matched call at the function it forwards to, reads the formals of the function the call matched (its caller by default, or those the caller passes) instead of evaluating the call's head, so a front door called through an alias (fn <- bart; fn(...)) forwards its arguments as when called by name; it had found its own formal fn and sent every bart argument to dbartsControl. The alternative, inferred when this entry was written and not weighed at the time, was evaluating the head in another frame, where an alias may name nothing or the wrong function. The fix also repairs 0.9-34's bart2, which fails when called through an alias made inside a function; nothing else is visible. Not yet ruled on.
Record: commit d6389c28. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: confirmed under dec-B375. [dec-C13]

**A factor basis may leave a level empty on setForestBasis**
setForestBasis accepts a factor basis one of whose levels no row carries, so a basis swap inside a larger sampler can leave a level unobserved for a while, its column contributing nothing until a row returns to it; a numeric all-zero column stays refused, as at creation, where an empty column of either kind is refused. The alternatives were refusing the empty level on the swap as at creation, and accepting numeric zero columns too. Nothing breaks; the help for forest() and setForestBasis says which is accepted where. Not yet ruled on.
Record: commits 6b44f394 and f2142251. Marked: by the maintainer through the 2026-10-08 triage: revised under dec-B346. [dec-C14]
